I hold that there is nothing like one’s own rooftree. It is a great treat to one who, like myself, is located in one of these vast caravanserai—to be exact, the Astor—to pass a few moments in the quiet privacy of an apartment such as this.”

===Chinese===
比利·温莎住在东十四街一间单人房里。纽约的地皮金贵，寻常单身汉的宅邸便是一间屋，外带一间从屋里开门进去的浴室。一到白天，这间屋子夜里用作卧室的一切痕迹便消失无踪。比利·温莎的房间很像公立学校的一间书斋。沿一面墙摆着一张长榻。夜里它是床；白天它就只是长榻，不折不扣的长榻。屋里腾不出地方摆太多家具。有一把摇椅、两把普通椅子、一张桌子、一个书架、一台打字机——纽约没人用钢笔——墙上混杂挂着照片、图画、刀子、兽皮，都是屋主草原岁月的遗物。门头上方，是一颗小熊的头颅。

一进这方圣所，比利头一件事便是放猫出篮。那猫不安分地转悠了一阵，终于断定此处无路可逃，便在长榻一角安顿下来。普史密斯优雅地沉坐到它旁边，伸直双腿，点上一支烟。迈克挑了一把普通椅子；比利·温莎则在摇椅里落了座，开始有节奏地前后摇晃，这番表演他不倦地一直做到了底。

「一片祥和景象。」普史密斯品评道。「三颗伟大的头脑，营业时间里敏锐、机警、不得安宁，此刻放松了。一切安谧，闲谈甚欢。你这住所真是安逸，温莎同志。我素来主张，什么都比不上自家的屋檐。对一个像我这样下榻在偌大客栈里的人——确切说，阿斯特饭店——能在这样一套居室的清静私密中消磨片刻，实在是难得的享受。」

===Original===
“It’s beastly expensive at the Astor,” said Mike.

“The place has that drawback also. Anon, Comrade Jackson, I think we will hunt around for some such cubbyhole as this, built for two. Our nervous systems must be conserved.”

“On Fourth Avenue,” said Billy Windsor, “you can get quite good flats very cheap. Furnished, too. You should move there. It’s not much of a neighbourhood. I don’t know if you mind that?”

“Far from it, Comrade Windsor. It is my aim to see New York in all its phases. If a certain amount of harmless revelry can be whacked out of Fourth Avenue, we must dash there with the vim of highly-trained smell-dogs. Are you with me, Comrade Jackson?”

“All right,” said Mike.

===Chinese===
「阿斯特饭店贵得吓人。」迈克说。

「那地方还有这么一层短处。回头，杰克逊同志，我想我们也去寻摸一间这样的鸽笼，双人的。我们的神经系统必须爱惜。」

「在第四大道，」比利·温莎说，「你能租到相当不错的公寓，还很便宜，连家具都带。你们该搬过去。那一带环境不怎么样。不知你们介意吗？」

「毫不介意，温莎同志。我的目标正是见识纽约的方方面面。倘若第四大道能敲打出些许无害的狂欢，我们就必须以训练有素的嗅犬之猛扑将过去。你随我同行吗，杰克逊同志？」

「行。」迈克说。

===Original===
“And now, Comrade Windsor, it would be a pleasure to me to peruse that little journal of which you spoke. I have had so few opportunities of getting into touch with the literature of this great country.”

Billy Windsor stretched out an arm and pulled a bundle of papers from the bookstand. He tossed them on to the settee by Psmith’s side.

“There you are,” he said, “if you really feel like it. Don’t say I didn’t warn you. If you’ve got the nerve, read on.”

Psmith had picked up one of the papers when there came a shuffling of feet in the passage outside, followed by a knock upon the door. The next moment there appeared in the doorway a short, stout young man. There was an indescribable air of toughness about him, partly due to the fact that he wore his hair in a well-oiled fringe almost down to his eyebrows, which gave him the appearance of having no forehead at all. His eyes were small and set close together. His mouth was wide, his jaw prominent. Not, in short, the sort of man you would have picked out on sight as a model citizen.

===Chinese===
「而今，温莎同志，能拜读一下你说的那份小报，于我将是一桩乐事。与这个伟大国度的文学结缘的机会，我得到过的是如此之少。」

比利·温莎伸出一条胳膊，从书架上抽出一沓报纸，丢在普史密斯身旁的长榻上。

「喏，」他说，「你要真想读就读吧。可别说我没警告过你。你要是有胆量，就往下读。」

普史密斯刚拾起其中一份，外面过道里便响起一阵拖沓的脚步，接着是敲门声。下一刻，门口出现了一个矮胖的年轻人。他周身有一种难以言传的凶悍气，部分归功于他那抹了油的刘海，直压到眉毛边，害得他看上去压根儿没有前额。他眼睛小，两眼挨得紧。嘴阔，下颌突出。总而言之，不是那种你一眼就会挑出来当模范公民的人。

===Original===
His entrance was marked by a curious sibilant sound, which, on acquaintance, proved to be a whistled tune. During the interview which followed, except when he was speaking, the visitor whistled softly and unceasingly.

“Mr. Windsor?” he said to the company at large.

Psmith waved a hand towards the rocking chair. “That,” he said, “is Comrade Windsor. To your right is Comrade Jackson, England’s favourite son. I am Psmith.”

The visitor blinked furtively, and whistled another tune. As he looked round the room, his eye fell on the cat. His face lit up.

“Say!” he said, stepping forward, and touching the cat’s collar, “mine, mister.”

===Chinese===
他进门时伴着一种奇妙的咝咝声，熟识之后才听出那是一支用口哨吹的曲子。接下来这场会面里，只要不开口，来客便轻声地、不歇气地吹着口哨。

「哪位是温莎先生？」他对满屋子人说。

普史密斯朝摇椅一挥手。「那位，」他说，「是温莎同志。你右手边是杰克逊同志，英格兰的宠儿。在下普史密斯。」

来客偷偷眨了眨眼，换了一支曲子吹。他环视屋子，目光落在了猫身上。他脸上顿时放光。

「哎！」他说，上前一步，碰了碰猫的项圈，「俺的，先生。」

===Original===
“Are you Bat Jarvis?” asked Windsor with interest.

“Sure,” said the visitor, not without a touch of complacency, as of a monarch abandoning his incognito.

For Mr. Jarvis was a celebrity.

By profession he was a dealer in animals, birds, and snakes. He had a fancier’s shop in Groome street, in the heart of the Bowery. This was on the ground floor. His living abode was in the upper story of that house, and it was there that he kept the twenty-three cats whose necks were adorned with leather collars, and whose numbers had so recently been reduced to twenty-two. But it was not the fact that he possessed twenty-three cats with leather collars that made Mr. Jarvis a celebrity.

===Chinese===
「你是巴特·贾维斯？」温莎饶有兴致地问。

「正是。」来客说，语气里透着几分得意，像一位君主放弃了隐姓埋名。

因为贾维斯先生是位名人。

论职业，他是兽、鸟、蛇的贩子。他在格鲁姆街（Groome Street）开了一家玩赏动物铺子，正在鲍厄里（the Bowery）的心脏地带。铺子在一楼。他的住处在那栋房子的楼上，他就养着那二十三只脖子上装点着皮项圈的猫——这数目最近刚减为二十二。然而，让贾维斯先生成为名人的，并非他养了二十三只戴皮项圈的猫。

===Original===
A man may win a purely local reputation, if only for eccentricity, by such means. But Mr. Jarvis’s reputation was far from being purely local. Broadway knew him, and the Tenderloin. Tammany Hall knew him. Long Island City knew him. In the underworld of New York his name was a byword. For Bat Jarvis was the leader of the famous Groome Street Gang, the most noted of all New York’s collections of Apaches. More, he was the founder and originator of it. And, curiously enough, it had come into being from motives of sheer benevolence. In Groome Street in those days there had been a dance-hall, named the Shamrock and presided over by one Maginnis, an Irishman and a friend of Bat’s. At the Shamrock nightly dances were given and well attended by the youth of the neighbourhood at ten cents a head. All might have been well, had it not been for certain other youths of the neighbourhood who did not dance and so had to seek other means of getting rid of their surplus energy. It was the practice of these lighthearted sportsmen to pay their ten cents for admittance, and once in, to make hay. And this habit, Mr. Maginnis found, was having a marked effect on his earnings. For genuine lovers of the dance fought shy of a place where at any moment Philistines might burst in and break heads and furniture. In this crisis the proprietor thought of his friend Bat Jarvis. Bat at that time had a solid reputation as a man of his hands. It is true that, as his detractors pointed out, he had killed no one—a defect which he had subsequently corrected; but his admirers based his claim to respect on his many meritorious performances with fists and with the blackjack. And Mr. Maginnis for one held him in the very highest esteem. To Bat accordingly he went, and laid his painful case before him. He offered him a handsome salary to be on hand at the nightly dances and check undue revelry by his own robust methods. Bat had accepted the offer. He had gone to Shamrock Hall; and with him, faithful adherents, had gone such stalwarts as Long Otto, Red Logan, Tommy Jefferson, and Pete Brodie. Shamrock Hall became a place of joy and order; and—more important still—the nucleus of the Groome Street Gang had been formed. The work progressed. Offshoots of the main gang sprang up here and there about the East Side. Small thieves, pickpockets and the like, flocked to Mr. Jarvis as their tribal leader and protector and he protected them. For he, with his followers, were of use to the politicians. The New York gangs, and especially the Groome Street Gang, have brought to a fine art the gentle practice of “repeating”; which, broadly speaking, is the art of voting a number of different times at different polling stations on election days. A man who can vote, say, ten times in a single day for you, and who controls a great number of followers who are also prepared, if they like you, to vote ten times in a single day for you, is worth cultivating. So the politicians passed the word to the police, and the police left the Groome Street Gang unmolested and they waxed fat and flourished.

Such was Bat Jarvis.

---

===Chinese===
靠这种名堂，一个人或许能挣得一份纯地方性的名声——哪怕只是以怪僻闻名。可贾维斯先生的名声远不止于一方街坊。百老汇认得他，坦德林区（the Tenderloin）也认得他。坦慕尼厅（Tammany Hall）认得他。长岛市（Long Island City）认得他。在纽约的地下世界里，他的名字家喻户晓。因为巴特·贾维斯正是大名鼎鼎的格鲁姆街帮的首领——纽约全城各路「阿帕奇」帮派中最负盛名的一伙。还不止于此，他也是这帮的开山鼻祖。而说来也怪，这帮派的诞生，纯粹是出于一片善心。当年格鲁姆街有一家舞厅，名唤「三叶草」（Shamrock），老板是个姓马金尼斯（Maginnis）的爱尔兰人，巴特的朋友。「三叶草」每晚开舞会，左邻右舍的青年踊跃参加，门票一毛钱一位。本来一切太平，坏就坏在附近另有一批青年，他们不跳舞，过剩的精力便得另找出路。这帮没心没肺的运动健将惯常花一毛钱买门票进场，一进门便大闹天宫。马金尼斯先生发现，这个习惯对他的进账影响显著。因为真正热爱跳舞的人，对一个随时可能有野蛮人冲进来砸人脑袋砸桌椅的地方，总是敬而远之。危机当头，老板想起了他的朋友巴特·贾维斯。巴特当年作为一双拳头硬的好汉，早有响当当的名声。诚然，如诋毁他的人所指出的，他还没杀过人——这个缺憾他后来也补上了；但他的仰慕者们认定他值得敬重，凭的是他拳头上和铅头短棍下诸多值得称道的功业。马金尼斯先生就是敬他敬得五体投地的人之一。于是他去找巴特，把自己这桩痛心事和盘托出。他出一笔可观的薪水，请他每晚舞会到场坐镇，以他自己那套过硬的手段遏制过火的狂欢。巴特应承了下来。他去了「三叶草」舞厅；与他同去、忠心追随的，还有高个奥托（Long Otto）、红发洛根（Red Logan）、汤米·杰斐逊（Tommy Jefferson）、皮特·布罗迪（Pete Brodie）这班好汉。「三叶草」舞厅从此成了欢乐与秩序的所在；而——更要紧的是——格鲁姆街帮的核心就此成形。事业蒸蒸日上。主干的分支在下城东区（East Side）各处破土而出。小偷、扒手之流纷纷投奔贾维斯先生，奉他为族长兼保护人，他也确实保护他们。因为他和他的徒众，对政客们大有用处。纽约各黑帮，尤其是格鲁姆街帮，把「重复投票」这门温文尔雅的行当修炼成了精；笼统说来，就是在选举日到各个投票站反复投票多次的艺术。一个人能一天替你投上十票，还统率着一大帮徒众，只要他们喜欢你，也乐意一天替你投十票——这样的人值得结交。于是政客们给警察递了话，警察便对格鲁姆街帮秋毫无犯，他们遂养得脑满肠肥，兴旺发达。

巴特·贾维斯，就是这么一号人物。

---

===Original===
“Pipe de collar,” said Mr. Jarvis, touching the cat’s neck. “Mine, mister.”

“Pugsy said it must be,” said Billy Windsor. “We found two fellows setting a dog on to it, so we took it in for safety.”

Mr. Jarvis nodded approval.

“There’s a basket here, if you want it,” said Billy.

“Nope. Here, kit.”

Mr. Jarvis stooped, and, still whistling softly, lifted the cat. He looked round the company, met Psmith’s eyeglass, was transfixed by it for a moment, and finally turned again to Billy Windsor.

===Chinese===
「瞧这项圈。」贾维斯先生碰着猫脖子说。「俺的，先生。」

「普格西就说准是。」比利·温莎说。「我们发现两个人放狗咬它，就把它收留下来保个平安。」

贾维斯先生点头赞许。

「这儿有个篮子，你要用就拿去。」比利说。

「不用。来，猫咪。」

贾维斯先生弯下腰，口哨依旧轻轻地吹着，把猫抱了起来。他环视众人，与普史密斯的单片眼镜打了个照面，被它定住了一会儿，终于又转向比利·温莎。

===Original===
“Say!” he said, and paused. “Obliged,” he added.

He shifted the cat on to his left arm, and extended his right hand to Billy.

“Shake!” he said.

Billy did so.

Mr. Jarvis continued to stand and whistle for a few moments more.

“Say!” he said at length, fixing his roving gaze once more upon Billy. “Obliged. Fond of de kit, I am.”

Psmith nodded approvingly.

===Chinese===
「哎！」他说，顿了顿。「承情了。」他补上一句。

他把猫挪到左臂上，向比利伸出右手。

「握一个！」他说。

比利照办了。

贾维斯先生又站了一会儿，吹了一会儿口哨。

「哎！」他终于又开口，游移的目光重新落在比利身上。「承情了。俺疼这猫咪。」

普史密斯赞许地点头。

===Original===
“And rightly,” he said. “Rightly, Comrade Jarvis. She is not unworthy of your affection. A most companionable animal, full of the highest spirits. Her knockabout act in the restaurant would have satisfied the most jaded critic. No diner-out can afford to be without such a cat. Such a cat spells death to boredom.”

Mr. Jarvis eyed him fixedly, as if pondering over his remarks. Then he turned to Billy again.

“Say!” he said. “Any time you’re in bad. Glad to be of service. You know the address. Groome Street. Bat Jarvis. Good night. Obliged.”

===Chinese===
「疼得有理，」他说。「有理，贾维斯同志。它绝非辜负你的厚爱。一个顶会作伴的生灵，浑身是最饱满的兴致。它在饭馆里那套滑稽戏，最挑剔的老看客也要称心。凡是外出赴宴的体面人，缺了这样一只猫可不行。有这样一只猫，无聊便死定了。」

贾维斯先生定定地看着他，仿佛在琢磨他这些话。随后又转向比利。

「哎！」他说。「你们哪天落了难。乐意效劳。地址你们知道。格鲁姆街。巴特·贾维斯。晚安。承情了。」

===Original===
He paused and whistled a few more bars, then nodded to Psmith and Mike, and left the room. They heard him shuffling downstairs.

“A blithe spirit,” said Psmith. “Not garrulous, perhaps, but what of that? I am a man of few words myself. Comrade Jarvis’s massive silences appeal to me. He seems to have taken a fancy to you, Comrade Windsor.”

Billy Windsor laughed.

“I don’t know that he’s just the sort of side-partner I’d go out of my way to choose, from what I’ve heard about him. Still, if one got mixed up with any of that East-Side crowd, he would be a mighty useful friend to have. I guess there’s no harm done by getting him grateful.”

“Assuredly not,” said Psmith. “We should not despise the humblest. And now, Comrade Windsor,” he said, taking up the paper again, “let me concentrate myself tensely on this very entertaining little journal of yours. Comrade Jackson, here is one for you. For sound, clearheaded criticism,” he added to Billy, “Comrade Jackson’s name is a byword in our English literary salons. His opinion will be both of interest and of profit to you, Comrade Windsor.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，又吹了几小节，然后朝普史密斯和迈克点点头，出了屋。他们听见他拖沓着脚步下了楼。

「一个快活的精灵。」普史密斯说。「话或许不多，可那又何妨？我自己也是个寡言的人。贾维斯同志那敦实的沉默，正合我心。他似乎对你另眼相看，温莎同志。」

比利·温莎笑了。

「照我听到的那些传闻说，他倒未必是我会特意去挑的那种搭档。不过，谁要是跟下城东区那帮人搅到了一起，有他这么个朋友可就大有用场了。我想，让他承咱们的情，总没有坏处。」

「自然没有。」普史密斯说。「最微末的人物我们也不该轻慢。而今，温莎同志，」他重新拿起报纸，「且让我全神贯注，拜读你这份妙趣横生的小报。杰克逊同志，这份归你。至于犀利清醒的评论，」他对比利补了一句，「杰克逊同志的大名在我们英国文人沙龙里人人挂在嘴边。他的高见对你必将既有意思、又有益处，温莎同志。」

## II Billy Windsor / 比利·温莎
===Original===
Billy Windsor had started life twenty-five years before this story opens on his father’s ranch in Wyoming. From there he had gone to a local paper of the type whose Society column consists of such items as “Pawnee Jim Williams was to town yesterday with a bunch of other cheapskates. We take this opportunity of once more informing Jim that he is a liar and a skunk,” and whose editor works with a revolver on his desk and another in his hip-pocket. Graduating from this, he had proceeded to a reporter’s post on a daily paper in a Kentucky town, where there were blood feuds and other Southern devices for preventing life from becoming dull. All this time New York, the magnet, had been tugging at him. All reporters dream of reaching New York. At last, after four years on the Kentucky paper, he had come East, minus the lobe of one ear and plus a long scar that ran diagonally across his left shoulder, and had worked without much success as a freelance. He was tough and ready for anything that might come his way, but these things are a great deal a matter of luck. The cub reporter cannot make a name for himself unless he is favoured by fortune. Things had not come Billy Windsor’s way. His work had been confined to turning in reports of fires and small street accidents, which the various papers to which he supplied them cut down to a couple of inches.

Billy had been in a bad way when he had happened upon the subeditorship of *Cosy Moments*. He despised the work with all his heart, and the salary was infinitesimal. But it was regular, and for a while Billy felt that a regular salary was the greatest thing on earth. But he still dreamed of winning through to a post on one of the big New York dailies, where there was something doing and a man would have a chance of showing what was in him.

The unfortunate thing, however, was that *Cosy Moments* took up his time so completely. He had no chance of attracting the notice of big editors by his present work, and he had no leisure for doing any other.

All of which may go to explain why his normal aspect was that of a caged eagle.

===Chinese===
故事开场之前二十五年，比利·温莎生在他父亲的怀俄明牧场。从那里他进了一家本地报纸——那类报纸的社会新闻栏里登的都是诸如「波尼·吉姆·威廉姆斯昨日与一帮小气鬼进城。我们借此机会再次通知吉姆：他是个骗子加臭虫」的货色，编辑办公时桌上摆一支左轮手枪，屁股兜里还揣着一支。从这家报纸毕业之后，他又到肯塔基一座城镇的日报谋了个记者职位——那地方血仇械斗成风，还有各式南方特色的消遣，日子绝不至于无聊。这些年里，纽约像磁石一般一直牵着他。天下的记者都做着进纽约的梦。终于在肯塔基那张报纸干满四年之后，他东来了——少了一只耳朵的耳垂，左肩上斜添了一道长长的疤——靠自由撰稿糊口，却没什么起色。他筋骨结实，来什么接什么，可这种事多半要看运气。初出茅庐的记者若不受命运眷顾，就休想闯出名号。好运一直没有落到比利·温莎头上。他干的活计不外乎报道火灾和小型街头事故，稿子交给各家报纸后，都被删得只剩两三英寸。

比利碰上《舒适时光》（*Cosy Moments*）助理编辑这个位子时，正处在穷途潦倒的境地。他从心眼里瞧不起这份活计，薪水也少得可怜。但好歹是固定的，有一阵子比利觉得，一份固定薪水真是天底下顶了不起的东西。不过他仍旧梦想着打入纽约某家大日报——那里才有正经干头，一个人也才有机会显显自己肚里的本事。

可惜的是，《舒适时光》把他的时间占得满满当当。靠眼下的工作他引不起大编辑们的注意，却又挤不出工夫去干别的。

这些原委，或许足以说明他平日的神情何以总像一头关在笼里的鹰。

===Original===
To him, brooding over the outpourings of Luella Granville Waterman, there entered Pugsy Maloney, the office-boy, bearing a struggling cat.

“Say!” said Pugsy.

He was a nonchalant youth, with a freckled, mask-like face, the expression of which never varied. He appeared unconscious of the cat. Its existence did not seem to occur to him.

“Well?” said Billy, looking up. “Hello, what have you got there?”

===Chinese===
他正对着卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼（Luella Granville Waterman）笔下的滔滔倾诉出神，办公室听差普格西·马洛尼（Pugsy Maloney）抱着一只挣扎的猫走了进来。

「哎！」普格西说。

他是个满不在乎的少年，一张长雀斑的脸活像面具，表情从来不见变化。他仿佛对那只猫毫无知觉。猫的存在似乎压根儿没在他脑子里出现过。

「什么事？」比利抬起头来说。「嚯，你抱的那是什么？」

===Original===
Master Maloney eyed the cat, as if he were seeing it for the first time.

“It’s a kitty what I got in de street,” he said.

“Don’t hurt the poor brute. Put her down.”

Master Maloney obediently dropped the cat, which sprang nimbly on to an upper shelf of the bookcase.

===Chinese===
马洛尼少爷打量着那只猫，仿佛头一回瞧见它似的。

「是俺在街上捡的一只猫咪。」他说。

「别弄疼这可怜的小东西。放它下来。」

马洛尼少爷乖乖把猫放下，那猫敏捷地一蹿，跳上了书柜顶上的一层。

===Original===
“I wasn’t hoitin’ her,” he said, without emotion. “Dere was two fellers in de street sickin’ a dawg on to her. An’ I comes up an’ says, ‘G’wan! What do youse t’ink you’re doin’, fussin’ de poor dumb animal?’ An’ one of de guys, he says, ‘G’wan! Who do youse t’ink youse is?’ An’ I says, ‘I’m de guy what’s goin’ to swat youse one on de coco if youse don’t quit fussin’ de poor dumb animal.’ So wit dat he makes a break at swattin’ me one, but I swats him one, an’ I swats de odder feller one, an’ den I swats dem bote some more, an’ I gets de kitty, an’ I brings her in here, cos I t’inks maybe youse’ll look after her.”

And having finished this Homeric narrative, Master Maloney fixed an expressionless eye on the ceiling, and was silent.

===Chinese===
「俺才没欺负它呢。」他不动声色地说。「街上有俩家伙放狗咬它。俺走过去就说：『去去！你们祸害这不会说话的畜生，算干什么玩意儿？』其中一个说：『去去！你算老几？』俺就说：『俺就是你们再祸害这不会说话的畜生，就敲你们一家伙在脑壳上的那位。』那家伙一听就朝俺抡拳头，可俺先敲了他一家伙，又敲了另一个一家伙，接着把他们俩又各补了几下，俺就抱起猫咪，把它带这儿来了，俺寻思你兴许肯照看照看它。」

这番荷马史诗式的叙述讲完，马洛尼少爷一双毫无表情的眼睛盯住天花板，不再作声。

===Original===
Billy Windsor, like most men of the plains, combined the toughest of muscle with the softest of hearts. He was always ready at any moment to become the champion of the oppressed on the slightest provocation. His alliance with Pugsy Maloney had begun on the occasion when he had rescued that youth from the clutches of a large negro, who, probably from the soundest of motives, was endeavouring to slay him. Billy had not inquired into the rights and wrongs of the matter: he had merely sailed in and rescued the office-boy. And Pugsy, though he had made no verbal comment on the affair, had shown in many ways that he was not ungrateful.

“Bully for you, Pugsy!” he cried. “You’re a little sport. Here”—he produced a dollar-bill—“go out and get some milk for the poor brute. She’s probably starving. Keep the change.”

“Sure thing,” assented Master Maloney. He strolled slowly out, while Billy Windsor, mounting a chair, proceeded to chirrup and snap his fingers in the effort to establish the foundations of an *entente cordiale* with the rescued cat.

By the time that Pugsy returned, carrying a five-cent bottle of milk, the animal had vacated the bookshelf, and was sitting on the table, washing her face. The milk having been poured into the lid of a tobacco tin, in lieu of a saucer, she suspended her operations and adjourned for refreshments. Billy, business being business, turned again to Luella Granville Waterman, but Pugsy, having no immediate duties on hand, concentrated himself on the cat.

===Chinese===
比利·温莎和平原上长大的人大多一样，筋骨最硬，心肠最软。哪怕只有一点由头，他随时都肯挺身充当受压迫者的保护人。他与普格西·马洛尼的交情，始于他把那少年从一个高大黑人的魔爪下救出来的那次——那位黑人多半出于最纯正的动机，正努力要他的命。比利没有追究事情的是非曲直：他只是一头冲进去，把听差救了出来。普格西呢，虽然嘴上对此事一字不提，却处处表现出自己并非忘恩负义之辈。

「好样的，普格西！」他叫道。「你真是个小好汉。给」——他掏出一张一元钞票——「出去给这可怜的小东西买点儿牛奶。它多半饿坏了。找头你留着。」

「没错儿。」马洛尼少爷应道。他慢悠悠地踱了出去，比利·温莎则踩上一把椅子，啧啧唤猫、打着响指，努力与这只获救的猫打下「真诚友好关系」（*entente cordiale*）的基础。

等普格西拎着一瓶五分钱的牛奶回来时，那猫已经离开书柜，正蹲在桌上洗脸。牛奶倒进一只烟盒盖里权当碟子，它便中止了梳洗，移驾进餐。比利呢，公事终归是公事，又回头对付卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼去了；普格西手头没有急差，便一门心思看住了猫。

===Original===
“Say!” he said.

“Well?”

“Dat kitty.”

“What about her?”

“Pipe de leather collar she’s wearing.”

Billy had noticed earlier in the proceedings that a narrow leather collar encircled the cat’s neck. He had not paid any particular attention to it. “What about it?” he said.

“Guess I know where dat kitty belongs. Dey all have dose collars. I guess she’s one of Bat Jarvis’s kitties. He’s got a lot of dem for fair, and every one wit one of dem collars round deir neck.”

===Chinese===
「哎！」他说。

「怎么？」

「那只猫咪。」

「它怎么了？」

「瞧它脖子上戴的那条皮圈儿。」

方才这一通忙乱中，比利已注意到猫脖子上箍着一条窄皮圈，只是没大留心。「那圈儿怎么了？」他说。

「俺猜俺知道这猫咪是谁家的。它们都戴这种圈儿。俺寻思它是巴特·贾维斯（Bat Jarvis）家的猫咪之一。他家那样的猫多得吓人，一只只脖子上都戴着这种圈儿。」

===Original===
“Who’s Bat Jarvis? Do you mean the gang leader?”

“Sure. He’s a cousin of mine,” said Master Maloney with pride.

“Is he?” said Billy. “Nice sort of fellow to have in the family. So you think that’s his cat?”

“Sure. He’s got twenty-t’ree of dem, and dey all has dose collars.”

“Are you on speaking terms with the gentleman?”

“Huh?”

“Do you know Bat Jarvis to speak to?”

“Sure. He’s me cousin.”

===Chinese===
「巴特·贾维斯是谁？你是说那个黑帮头子？」

「就是他。他是俺表哥。」马洛尼少爷得意地说。

「是吗？」比利说。「家里有这么门亲戚，可真够体面的。这么说，你认定那是他家的猫？」

「可不。他养了二十三只，个个都戴这种圈儿。」

「你跟这位先生有交情吗？」

「啊？」

「我是说，见了巴特·贾维斯的面，能说得上话不？」

「能啊。他是俺表哥。」

===Original===
“Well, tell him I’ve got the cat, and that if he wants it he’d better come round to my place. You know where I live?”

“Sure.”

“Fancy you being a cousin of Bat’s, Pugsy. Why did you never tell us? Are you going to join the gang some day?”

“Nope. Nothin’ doin’. I’m goin’ to be a cowboy.”

“Good for you. Well, you tell him when you see him. And now, my lad, out you get, because if I’m interrupted any more I shan’t get through tonight.”

“Sure,” said Master Maloney, retiring.

“Oh, and Pugsy…”

“Huh?”

“Go out and get a good big basket. I shall want one to carry this animal home in.”

“Sure,” said Master Maloney.

===Chinese===
「那好，你告诉他，猫在我这儿，他要想要回去，最好上我住的地方来一趟。你知道我住哪儿吧？」

「知道。」

「真没想到你竟是巴特的表哥，普格西。怎么从来不对我们说？你打算将来哪一天也入那个黑帮吗？」

「不。没门儿。俺要当牛仔。」

「有志气。好，你见着他就把话带到。行了，小子，出去吧，再有人打岔，我今晚就别想干完了。」

「好嘞。」马洛尼少爷说着退了出去。

「哦，还有，普格西……」

「啊？」

「出去买一只结实的大提篮来。我得用它把这小东西装回家。」

「好嘞。」马洛尼少爷说。

## XXII Concerning Mr. Waring / 关于沃林先生
===Original===
Psmith pushed back his chair slightly, stretched out his legs, and lit a cigarette. The resources of the Knickerbocker Hotel had proved equal to supplying the fatigued staff of *Cosy Moments* with an excellent dinner, and Psmith had stoutly declined to talk business until the coffee arrived. This had been hard on Billy, who was bursting with his news. Beyond a hint that it was sensational he had not been permitted to go.

“More bright young careers than I care to think of,” said Psmith, “have been ruined by the fatal practice of talking shop at dinner. But now that we are through, Comrade Windsor, by all means let us have it. What’s the name which Comrade Gooch so eagerly divulged?”

Billy leaned forward excitedly.

“Stewart Waring,” he whispered.

===Chinese===
普史密斯把椅子往后稍稍一推，伸直双腿，点上一支烟。尼克博克饭店的库藏证明足以让《舒适时光》疲惫的同仁吃上一顿丰盛的晚餐，而在咖啡端上桌之前，普史密斯坚决拒绝谈正事。这可苦了比利，他的新闻已经憋得五脏俱胀。除了漏出一句「耸人听闻」的提示外，他被勒令一个字也不许多说。

「多少前程似锦的青年，」普史密斯说，「多到我不愿细想，都毁在了吃饭时谈论本行这个致命的习惯上。好在饭已吃完，温莎同志，请务必开讲。古奇同志（Comrade Gooch）那么热切吐露的名字，究竟是哪一位？」

比利激动地探过身来。

「斯图尔特·沃林。」他悄声说。

===Original===
“Stewart who?” asked Psmith.

Billy stared.

“Great Scott, man!” he said, “haven’t you heard of Stewart Waring?”

“The name seems vaguely familiar, like isinglass or Post-toasties. I seem to know it, but it conveys nothing to me.”

“Don’t you ever read the papers?”

“I toy with my *American* of a morning, but my interest is confined mainly to the sporting page which reminds me that Comrade Brady has been matched against one Eddie Wood a month from today. Gratifying as it is to find one of the staff getting on in life, I fear this will cause us a certain amount of inconvenience. Comrade Brady will have to leave the office temporarily in order to go into training, and what shall we do then for a fighting editor? However, possibly we may not need one now. *Cosy Moments* should be able shortly to give its message to the world and ease up for a while. Which brings us back to the point. Who is Stewart Waring?”

===Chinese===
「斯图尔特什么？」普史密斯问。

比利直勾勾地瞪着他。

「老天爷，老兄！」他说，「你没听说过斯图尔特·沃林（Stewart Waring）？」

「这名字似乎隐约耳熟，就像鱼胶或烤玉米片（Post-toasties）。我好像认得它，可它对我什么也没说明。」

「你到底读不读报？」

「每天早上我把玩一阵《美国人报》，兴趣却主要限于体育版——它提醒我，一个月后的今天，布雷迪同志将与一位埃迪·伍德（Eddie Wood）对阵。看到同人中人步步高升固然欣慰，只怕这也会给我们带来些许不便。布雷迪同志将不得不暂时离开办公室去集训，届时我们的战斗编辑从何寻起？不过，如今我们或许用不着了。《舒适时光》应可于近日向世界传达完它的使命，喘上一阵子气了。这就回到正题。斯图尔特·沃林是谁？」

===Original===
“Stewart Waring is running for City Alderman. He’s one of the biggest men in New York!”

“Do you mean in girth? If so, he seems to have selected the right career for himself.”

“He’s one of the bosses. He used to be Commissioner of Buildings for the city.”

“Commissioner of Buildings? What exactly did that let him in for?”

“It let him in for a lot of graft.”

===Chinese===
「斯图尔特·沃林正在竞选市议员（City Alderman）。他是纽约最有分量的人物之一！」

「你说的是腰围上的分量？若如此，他倒是给自己挑对了行当。」

「他是政界大佬之一。从前当过本市建筑局长（Commissioner of Buildings）。」

「建筑局长？那职位究竟给他招来了什么？」

「给他招来了大把的油水。」

===Original===
“How was that?”

“Oh, he took it off the contractors. Shut his eyes and held out his hands when they ran up rotten buildings that a strong breeze would have knocked down, and places like that Pleasant Street hole without any ventilation.”

“Why did he throw up the job?” inquired Psmith. “It seems to me that it was among the World’s Softest. Certain drawbacks to it, perhaps, to the man with the Hair-Trigger Conscience; but I gather that Comrade Waring did not line up in that class. What was his trouble?”

“His trouble,” said Billy, “was that he stood in with a contractor who was putting up a music hall, and the contractor put it up with material about as strong as a heap of meringues, and it collapsed on the third night and killed half the audience.”

“And then?”

“The papers raised a howl, and they got after the contractor, and the contractor gave Waring away. It killed him for the time being.”

===Chinese===
「怎么讲？」

「哦，他的油水是从包工头们身上刮的。那些人盖起一刮大风就能吹倒的烂房子，盖起普莱森特街那种连个通风口都没有的黑窟窿，他就闭起眼睛，摊开双手。」

「那他为何把差事扔了？」普史密斯问道。「依我看，那差事堪称世上顶轻松的营生之一。对怀有『一触即发型良心』的人，或许确有种种不便；可我听说沃林同志不属于那个阵营。他碰上什么麻烦了？」

「他的麻烦，」比利说，「在于他跟一个盖音乐厅的包工头穿一条裤子。那包工头用的材料跟一堆蛋白酥皮饼差不多结实，音乐厅开张第三夜就塌了，压死了半场观众。」

「后来呢？」

「各报一片怒吼，揪住包工头不放，包工头就把沃林兜了出来。一时间他算是完了。」

===Original===
“I should have thought it would have had that excellent result permanently,” said Psmith thoughtfully. “Do you mean to say he got back again after that?”

“He had to quit being Commissioner, of course, and leave the town for a time; but affairs move so fast here that a thing like that blows over. He made a bit of a pile out of the job, and could afford to lie low for a year or two.”

“How long ago was that?”

“Five years. People don’t remember a thing here that happened five years back unless they’re reminded of it.”

Psmith lit another cigarette.

“We will remind them,” he said.

===Chinese===
「我原以为那一劳永逸才是它美满的结果呢。」普史密斯若有所思地说。「你是说，那之后他居然又东山再起了？」

「他当然只好辞了局长，出城避了一阵风头；可这儿的事情变得快，那种事转眼就吹散了。他从那差事里捞了一小笔，歇得起一两年。」

「那是多久前的事？」

「五年。这儿的人除非有人提醒，否则五年前的事一概不记得。」

普史密斯又点了一支烟。

「我们来提醒他们。」他说。

===Original===
Billy nodded.

“Of course,” he said, “one or two of the papers against him in this Aldermanic Election business tried to bring the thing up, but they didn’t cut any ice. The other papers said it was a shame, hounding a man who was sorry for the past and who was trying to make good now; so they dropped it. Everybody thought that Waring was on the level now. He’s been shooting off a lot of hot air lately about philanthropy and so on. Not that he has actually done a thing—not so much as given a supper to a dozen newsboys; but he’s talked, and talk gets over if you keep it up long enough.”

Psmith nodded adhesion to this dictum.

“So that naturally he wants to keep it dark about these tenements. It’ll smash him at the election when it gets known.”

===Chinese===
比利点头。

「当然，」他说，「这次市议员选举里，一两家和他对着干的报纸想把旧事翻出来，可一点儿也没切动冰。别家报纸都说，逼一个已知悔过、正想重新做人未免不体面，于是便撂下了。人人都以为沃林如今改邪归正了。近来他大吹大擂，谈慈善谈公益。倒不是真干过什么——连请一打报童吃顿晚饭都没有过；可他光说，只要说得够久，话也就蒙混过去了。」

普史密斯点头，赞同这条定理。

「所以这些廉租公寓的事，他自然要捂得严严实实。一旦传开，选举就砸了。」

===Original===
“Why is he so set on becoming an Alderman,” inquired Psmith.

“There’s a lot of graft to being an Alderman,” explained Billy.

“I see. No wonder the poor gentleman was so energetic in his methods. What is our move now, Comrade Windsor?”

Billy stared.

“Why, publish the name, of course.”

“But before then? How are we going to ensure the safety of our evidence? We stand or fall entirely by that slip of paper, because we’ve got the beggar’s name in the writing of his own collector, and that’s proof positive.”

“That’s all right,” said Billy, patting his breast pocket. “Nobody’s going to get it from me.”

===Chinese===
「他何以如此一门心思要当市议员？」普史密斯问。

「当市议员油水大得很。」比利解释道。

「明白了。难怪这位可怜的绅士手段如此卖力。眼下我们怎么走，温莎同志？」

比利瞪圆了眼。

「那还用说，把名字登出来呗。」

「可在那之前呢？我们如何确保证据的安全？我们全凭那张纸片决定成败，因为那家伙的名字是他自家收租员亲笔所写，这是铁证。」

「这您就放心吧。」比利拍着胸前的口袋说。「谁也别想从我手里拿走它。」

===Original===
Psmith dipped his hand into his trouser pocket.

“Comrade Windsor,” he said, producing a piece of paper, “how do we go?”

He leaned back in his chair, surveying Billy blandly through his eyeglass. Billy’s eyes were goggling. He looked from Psmith to the paper and from the paper to Psmith.

“What—what the—?” he stammered. “Why, it’s it!”

Psmith nodded.

“How on earth did you get it?”

Psmith knocked the ash off his cigarette.

===Chinese===
普史密斯把手伸进裤袋。

「温莎同志，」他说着，掏出一张纸来，「意下如何？」

他仰回椅背，隔着单片眼镜悠然打量比利。比利的眼睛瞪得溜圆。他看看普史密斯，又看看那张纸，再从纸看到普史密斯。

「这——这是——？」他结结巴巴地说。「老天，就是它！」

普史密斯点了点头。

「你到底是怎么弄到手的？」

普史密斯弹了弹烟灰。

===Original===
“Comrade Windsor,” he said, “I do not wish to cavil or carp or rub it in in any way. I will merely remark that you pretty nearly landed us in the soup, and pass on to more congenial topics. Didn’t you know we were followed to this place?”

“Followed!”

“By a merchant in what Comrade Maloney would call a tall-shaped hat. I spotted him at an early date, somewhere down by Twenty-ninth Street. When we dived into Sixth Avenue for a space at Thirty-third Street, did he dive, too? He did. And when we turned into Forty-second Street, there he was. I tell you, Comrade Windsor, leeches were aloof, and burrs nonadhesive compared with that tall-shaped-hatted blighter.”

“Yes?”

“Do you remember, as you came to the entrance of this place, somebody knocking against you?”

“Yes, there was a pretty big crush in the entrance.”

===Chinese===
「温莎同志，」他说，「我无意挑剔、责难，或以任何方式哪壶不开提哪壶。我只想指出，你差一点儿就把我们俩害进了汤锅，然后便转向更愉快的话题。你不知道我们到这儿来的路上被人跟了吗？」

「被跟了！」

「被一个戴高筒帽的商人——用马洛尼同志的说法。我很早就发现了他，大概在二十九街那一带。我们在三十三街一头扎进第六大道躲了一阵，他扎了没有？扎了。我们拐进四十二街，他又在那儿。我告诉你，温莎同志，跟那位高筒帽瘟神一比，水蛭都算疏远，芒刺都算不粘人。」

「是吗？」

「你还记得，走进这家饭店大门时，有人撞了你一下吗？」

「记得，门口人挤得挺厉害。」

===Original===
“There was; but not so big as all that. There was plenty of room for this merchant to pass if he had wished. Instead of which he butted into you. I happened to be waiting for just that, so I managed to attach myself to his wrist with some vim and give it a fairly hefty wrench. The paper was inside his hand.”

Billy was leaning forward with a pale face.

“Jove!” he muttered.

“That about sums it up,” said Psmith.

Billy snatched the paper from the table and extended it towards him.

===Chinese===
「挤是挤，可没挤到那份上。那位商人要真想过去，空当有的是。他却偏偏一头撞进你怀里。碰巧我等的正是这一下，于是我使出几分猛劲，一把攥住他的手腕，狠狠一拧。那张纸就攥在他手心里。」

比利脸色发白，探身向前。

「天哪！」他喃喃道。

「大意如此。」普史密斯说。

比利从桌上抓起那张纸，朝他递过去。

===Original===
“Here,” he said feverishly, “you take it. Gum, I never thought I was such a mutt! I’m not fit to take charge of a toothpick. Fancy me not being on the watch for something of that sort. I guess I was so tickled with myself at the thought of having got the thing, that it never struck me they might try for it. But I’m through. No more for me. You’re the man in charge now.”

Psmith shook his head.

“These stately compliments,” he said, “do my old heart good, but I fancy I know a better plan. It happened that I chanced to have my eye on the blighter in the tall-shaped hat, and so was enabled to land him among the ribstones; but who knows but that in the crowd on Broadway there may not lurk other, unidentified blighters in equally tall-shaped hats, one of whom may work the same sleight-of-hand speciality on me? It was not that you were not capable of taking care of that paper: it was simply that you didn’t happen to spot the man. Now observe me closely, for what follows is an exhibition of Brain.”

===Chinese===
「拿着，」他急切地说，「你收着。嚯，我从没想到自己是这么个大笨蛋！我连看管一根牙签都不配。亏我还想不到会有人来这一手。我大概是得意坏了，一心想着东西到手，压根儿没料到他们还会来抢。但我受够了。我退出。现在当家人是你。」

普史密斯摇头。

「这隆重的恭维，」他说，「让我这颗老心暖洋洋的，不过我想我有个更好的主意。只因我碰巧盯住了那个高筒帽瘟神，才得以结结实实赏了他一记；可谁知道百老汇的人堆里会不会还潜伏着别的高筒帽瘟神，未曾识别，其中哪一位或许会在我身上使出同一手障眼法绝活？并非你保不住那张纸：你只是恰好没盯住那个人。现在仔细看我操作，接下来是一场大脑的展演。」

===Original===
He paid the bill, and they went out into the entrance hall of the hotel. Psmith, sitting down at a table, placed the paper in an envelope and addressed it to himself at the address of *Cosy Moments*. After which, he stamped the envelope and dropped it into the letterbox at the back of the hall.

“And now, Comrade Windsor,” he said, “let us stroll gently homewards down the Great White Way. What matter though it be fairly stiff with low-browed bravoes in tall-shaped hats? They cannot harm us. From me, if they search me thoroughly, they may scoop a matter of eleven dollars, a watch, two stamps, and a packet of chewing gum. Whether they would do any better with you I do not know. At any rate, they wouldn’t get that paper; and that’s the main thing.”

===Chinese===
他付了账，两人走进饭店的门厅。普史密斯在一张桌前坐下，把纸装进信封，收件人写他自己，地址是《舒适时光》编辑部。随后贴上邮票，把信封投进了大厅后部的邮筒。

「现在，温莎同志，」他说，「让我们沿着白色大道悠悠然漫步回家。哪怕道上挤满了低眉横眼、头戴高筒帽的亡命徒，又何足道哉？他们伤害不了我们。把我搜个底朝天，他们至多捞走大约十一块钱、一块表、两张邮票、一包口香糖。搜你能否更有斩获，我不得而知。无论如何，那张纸他们休想拿到；这才是主要的。」

===Original===
“You’re a genius,” said Billy Windsor.

“You think so?” said Psmith diffidently. “Well, well, perhaps you are right, perhaps you are right. Did you notice the hired ruffian in the flannel suit who just passed? He wore a baffled look, I fancy. And hark! Wasn’t that a muttered ‘Failed!’ I heard? Or was it the breeze moaning in the treetops? Tonight is a cold, disappointing night for Hired Ruffians, Comrade Windsor.”

===Chinese===
「你是个天才。」比利·温莎说。

「你这么觉得？」普史密斯谦逊地说。「好吧好吧，也许你是对的，也许你是对的。方才走过的那个穿法兰绒西装的雇用恶棍，你注意到了吗？我看他一脸受挫。再听！我听见的那声咕哝『失手了！』，是真有其声，还是晚风在树梢呜咽？今夜，对雇用恶棍们来说是个寒凉而失望的夜晚，温莎同志。」

## Conclusion / 尾声
===Original===
It was a drizzly November evening. The streets of Cambridge were a compound of mud, mist, and melancholy. But in Psmith’s rooms the fire burned brightly, the kettle droned, and all, as the proprietor had just observed, was joy, jollity, and song. Psmith, in pyjamas and a college blazer, was lying on the sofa. Mike, who had been playing football, was reclining in a comatose state in an armchair by the fire.

“How pleasant it would be,” said Psmith dreamily, “if all our friends on the other side of the Atlantic could share this very peaceful moment with us! Or perhaps not quite all. Let us say, Comrade Windsor in the chair over there, Comrades Brady and Maloney on the table, and our old pal Wilberfloss sharing the floor with B. Henderson Asher, Bat Jarvis, and the cats. By the way, I think it would be a graceful act if you were to write to Comrade Jarvis from time to time telling him how your Angoras are getting on. He regards you as the World’s Most Prominent Citizen. A line from you every now and then would sweeten the lad’s existence.”

===Chinese===
那是一个细雨蒙蒙的十一月黄昏。剑桥的街道是泥浆、雾气与愁闷的混合物。可是在普史密斯的房间里，炉火烧得正旺，水壶嗡嗡低鸣，而且，正如房主刚刚所说的那样，一切尽是欢乐、快活与歌唱。普史密斯穿着睡衣裤、罩学院运动上衣，躺在沙发上。踢完足球的迈克，正瘫在炉边扶手椅里，处于昏迷状态。

「那该多么惬意啊，」普史密斯悠悠地说，「倘若大西洋彼岸咱们所有的朋友，都能与咱们共享这极为安宁的一刻！或者也不必是全部。就这么说吧：温莎同志坐那边那把椅子，布雷迪同志和马洛尼同志上桌子，咱们的老伙计威尔伯弗洛斯，同 B·亨德森·阿舍、巴特·贾维斯和众猫一起分地板。对了，我想你要是时不时写封信给贾维斯同志，告诉他你的安哥拉猫们近况如何，那将是一桩风雅之举。他把你视为全世界最显赫的公民。你偶尔来上几行，会让那小伙子的人生变甜。」

===Original===
Mike stirred sleepily in his chair.

“What?” he said drowsily.

“Never mind, Comrade Jackson. Let us pass lightly on. I am filled with a strange content tonight. I may be wrong, but it seems to me that all is singularly to de good, as Comrade Maloney would put it. Advices from Comrade Windsor inform me that that prince of blighters, Waring, was rejected by an intelligent electorate. Those keen, clear-sighted citizens refused to vote for him to an extent that you could notice without a microscope. Still, he has one consolation. He owns what, when the improvements are completed, will be the finest and most commodious tenement houses in New York. Millionaires will stop at them instead of going to the Plaza. Are you asleep, Comrade Jackson?”

===Chinese===
迈克在椅子里睡意朦胧地动了动。

「什么？」他迷迷糊糊地说。

「不碍事，杰克逊同志。咱们轻轻翻过这一页。今晚我心里充满一种奇异的满足。我也许错了，不过依我看，一切都好得妥妥的——马洛尼同志会这么说。温莎同志的通报告诉我：活宝王子沃林，已被一群有头脑的选民断然拒绝。那些敏锐、目光清澈的公民不投他的票，投到了不用显微镜也看得出的程度。不过，他也有一桩安慰：等改造完工，他将拥有纽约最精美、最宽敞的廉租公寓。百万富翁们将不去广场饭店，改到他家下榻。你睡着了吗，杰克逊同志？」

===Original===
“Um—m,” said Mike.

“That is excellent. You could not be better employed. Keep listening. Comrade Windsor also stated—as indeed did the sporting papers—that Comrade Brady put it all over friend Eddie Wood, administering the sleep-producer in the eighth round. My authorities are silent as to whether or not the lethal blow was a half-scissor hook, but I presume such to have been the case. The Kid is now definitely matched against Comrade Garvin for the championship, and the experts seem to think that he should win. He is a stout fellow, is Comrade Brady, and I hope he wins through. He will probably come to England later on. When he does, we must show him round. I don’t think you ever met him, did you, Comrade Jackson?”

===Chinese===
「唔——嗯。」迈克说。

「好极了。你再没有比这更有出息的用法了。接着听。温莎同志还说——体育报纸上也登了——布雷迪同志把埃迪·伍德（Eddie Wood）那朋友全面料理了，第八回合就给灌下了催眠丸。至于那记致命一击是不是一记半剪式勾拳，我的情报来源们保持沉默，但我推定正是如此。小子如今正式敲定与加文同志对阵争冠军，专家们似乎认为他该赢。布雷迪同志是条硬汉，我盼着他一路赢下去。他晚些时候多半会来英国。他真来的时候，咱们得领他四处转转。我想你一直没见过他，对吧，杰克逊同志？」

===Original===
“Ur—r,” said Mike.

“Say no more,” said Psmith. “I take you.”

He reached out for a cigarette.

“These,” he said, comfortably, “are the moments in life to which we look back with that wistful pleasure. What of my boyhood at Eton? Do I remember with the keenest joy the brain-tourneys in the old form-room, and the bally rot which used to take place on the Fourth of June? No. Burned deeply into my memory is a certain hot bath I took after one of the foulest cross-country runs that ever occurred outside Dante’s Inferno. So with the present moment. This peaceful scene, Comrade Jackson, will remain with me when I have forgotten that such a person as Comrade Repetto ever existed. These are the real *Cosy Moments*. And while on that subject you will be glad to hear that the little sheet is going strong. The man Wilberfloss is a marvel in his way. He appears to have gathered in the majority of the old subscribers again. Hopping mad but a brief while ago, they now eat out of his hand. You’ve really no notion what a feeling of quiet pride it gives you owning a paper. I try not to show it, but I seem to myself to be looking down on the world from some lofty peak. Yesterday night, when I was looking down from the peak without a cap and gown, a proctor slid up. Today I had to dig down into my jeans for a matter of two plunks. But what of it? Life must inevitably be dotted with these minor tragedies. I do not repine. The whisper goes round, ‘Psmith bites the bullet, and wears a brave smile.’ Comrade Jackson—”

===Chinese===
「呃——啊。」迈克说。

「不必多言，」普史密斯说，「我懂你。」

他伸手够了一支烟。

「这样的时刻，」他舒舒服服地说，「正是我们日后带着怅然的欢愉回首的时刻。我在伊顿（Eton）的少年时代呢？我最欢快地记得的，是旧教室里的脑力比武、还有六月四日那天的种种胡闹吗？不。深深烙进我记忆的，是在一场越野跑之后洗的那个热水澡——那场越野跑之惨烈，但丁《地狱篇》（Dante’s Inferno）以外无出其右。眼下这一刻也是如此。这幅安宁的图景，杰克逊同志，将伴我长存，即使我忘了世上还有雷佩托同志这么一号人。这些才是真正的《舒适时光》。顺便说个相关的喜讯：这份小刊如今势头正劲。威尔伯弗洛斯这个人，自有他的一手奇迹。他似乎又把大多数老订户笼络回来了。前不久还气得直蹦的他们，如今对他言听计从。你可真想象不到，拥有一份报纸会给你一种怎样的、悄然的骄傲。我尽量不露出来，可我总觉得自己正从某座高峰上俯瞰世界。昨天夜里，我没戴方帽、没穿学袍就从峰顶俯瞰，一位学监悄悄滑了过来。今天我只得掏腰包，掏出了两块大洋。可那又如何？人生注定要点点滴滴洒满这类小型悲剧。我不怨叹。低语四处流传：『普史密斯咬紧牙关，面带勇敢的微笑。』杰克逊同志——」

===Original===
A snore came from the chair.

Psmith sighed. But he did not repine. He bit the bullet. His eyes closed.

Five minutes later a slight snore came from the sofa, too. The man behind *Cosy Moments* slept.

===Chinese===
椅子里传来鼾声。

普史密斯叹了口气。但他不怨叹。他咬紧了牙关。他的眼睛合上了。

五分钟后，沙发上也传来了轻微的鼾声。《舒适时光》背后的男人睡着了。

## XX Cornered / 陷入重围
===Original===
He stood in the doorway looking with some surprise at the group inside. He was a smallish, pale-faced man with protruding eyes and teeth which gave him a certain resemblance to a rabbit.

“Hello,” he said.

“Welcome to New York,” said Psmith.

Master Maloney, who had taken advantage of the interruption to edge farther into the room, now appeared to consider the question of his departure permanently shelved. He sidled to a corner and sat down on an empty soapbox with the air of a dramatic critic at the opening night of a new play. The scene looked good to him. It promised interesting developments. Master Maloney was an earnest student of the drama, as exhibited in the theatres of the East Side, and few had ever applauded the hero of “Escaped from Sing-Sing,” or hissed the villain of “Nellie, the Beautiful Cloak-Model” with more fervour than he. He liked his drama to have plenty of action, and to his practised eye this one promised well. Psmith he looked upon as a quite amiable lunatic, from whom little was to be expected; but there was a set expression on Billy Windsor’s face which suggested great things.

His pleasure was abruptly quenched. Billy Windsor, placing a firm hand on his collar, led him to the door and pushed him out, closing the door behind him.

===Chinese===
他站在门口，有些惊奇地望着屋里的一伙人。他是个身材矮小、面色苍白的男人，眼睛外凸，门牙外露，使他颇有几分像只兔子。

「你们好。」他说。

「欢迎来到纽约。」普史密斯说。

马洛尼少爷本已趁着这阵打扰蹭进了屋里更深处，此刻看来，他把「离场」这个问题当作永久搁置了。他侧身挪到墙角，在一只空肥皂箱上坐下，一派剧评家出席新戏首演的神气。这场面在他看来甚好，大有好看的发展可期。马洛尼少爷是下城东区各剧院所展示之戏剧的认真研究者，为《逃出新新监狱》（“Escaped from Sing-Sing”）的主角喝彩、向《妮莉，美丽的外套模特》（“Nellie, the Beautiful Cloak-Model”）的反派喝倒彩，很少有人比他更卖力。他喜欢戏剧多些动作场面，而以他行家的眼光看，眼下这一出前景可观。至于普史密斯，他视之为一个相当和气的疯子，指望不出什么；可比利·温莎脸上那种笃定的神情，预示着要干大事。

他的兴致骤然被浇灭了。比利·温莎一只手牢牢扣住他的领子，把他拎到门口推了出去，随手关上了门。

===Original===
The rent collector watched these things with a puzzled eye. He now turned to Psmith.

“Say, seen anything of the wops that live here?” he inquired.

“I am addressing—?” said Psmith courteously.

“My name’s Gooch.”

Psmith bowed.

“Touching these wops, Comrade Gooch,” he said, “I fear there is little chance of your seeing them tonight, unless you wait some considerable time. With one of them—the son and heir of the family, I should say—we have just been having a highly interesting and informative chat. Comrade Maloney, who has just left us, acted as interpreter. The father, I am told, is in the dungeon below the castle moat for a brief spell for punching his foreman in the eye. The result? The rent is not forthcoming.”

“Then it’s outside for theirs,” said Mr. Gooch definitely.

“It’s a big shame,” broke in Billy, “turning the kid out. Where’s he to go?”

===Chinese===
收租员一脸困惑地看完了这一幕，随即转向普史密斯。

「哎，见过住这儿的几个意大利崽子没有？」他问道。

「请问鄙人是在同哪位讲话——？」普史密斯彬彬有礼地说。

「我叫古奇。」

普史密斯欠身一礼。

「至于那几位意大利崽子，古奇同志，」他说，「恐怕你今晚见不到他们的机会渺茫，除非你肯等上相当长的时间。其中一位——该说是府上的公子兼继承人吧——我们方才正进行过一场妙趣横生、获益良多的交谈。刚离开我们的马洛尼同志担任了通译。至于父亲，据我所知，正因一拳打在工头眼睛上，在城堡护城河底下的地牢里短期服役。结果呢？房租交不出来了。」

「那就把他们轰出去。」古奇先生斩钉截铁地说。

「把那孩子赶出去，太缺德了。」比利插嘴道。「他能上哪儿去？」

===Original===
“That’s up to him. Nothing to do with me. I’m only acting under orders from up top.”

“Whose orders, Comrade Gooch?” inquired Psmith.

“The gent who owns this joint.”

“Who is he?” said Billy.

Suspicion crept into the protruding eyes of the rent collector. He waxed wroth. “Say!” he demanded. “Who are you two guys, anyway, and what do you think you’re doing here? That’s what I’d like to know. What do you want with the name of the owner of this place? What business is it of yours?”

“The fact is, Comrade Gooch, we are newspaper men.”

“I guessed you were,” said Mr. Gooch with triumph. “You can’t bluff me. Well, it’s no good, boys. I’ve nothing for you. You’d better chase off and try something else.”

===Chinese===
「那是他的事。与我无关。我不过是奉上面的命令行事。」

「奉谁的命令，古奇同志？」普史密斯问。

「拥有这地方的那位先生。」

「他是谁？」比利说。

疑心爬进了收租员那双凸眼里。他火了起来。「哎！」他质问，「你们两个到底是什么人，跑到这儿来算干什么的？我倒想弄明白。你们打听这地方房主的名字想干什么？关你们什么事？」

「实不相瞒，古奇同志，我们是报馆的人。」

「我就猜你们是。」古奇先生得意地说。「糊弄不了我。算了吧，没用，二位。我这儿没什么给你们的。你们最好赶紧开路，另寻别路吧。」

===Original===
He became more friendly.

“Say, though,” he said, “I just guessed you were from some paper. I wish I could give you a story, but I can’t. I guess it’s this *Cosy Moments* business that’s been and put your editor on to this joint, ain’t it? Say, though, that’s a queer thing, that paper. Why, only a few weeks ago it used to be a sort of take-home-and-read-to-the-kids affair. A friend of mine used to buy it regular. And then suddenly it comes out with a regular whoop, and started knocking these tenements and boosting Kid Brady, and all that. I can’t understand it. All I know is that it’s begun to get this place talked about. Why, you see for yourselves how it is. Here is your editor sending you down to get a story about it. But, say, those *Cosy Moments* guys are taking big risks. I tell you straight they are, and that goes. I happen to know a thing or two about what’s going on on the other side, and I tell you there’s going to be something doing if they don’t cut it out quick. Mr.—” he stopped and chuckled, “Mr. Jones isn’t the man to sit still and smile. He’s going to get busy. Say, what paper do you boys come from?”

“*Cosy Moments*, Comrade Gooch,” Psmith replied. “Immediately behind you, between you and the door, is Comrade Windsor, our editor. I am Psmith. I subedit.”

===Chinese===
他变得友好起来。

「不过话说，」他说，「我刚才是猜你们是哪家报馆的。我倒想给你们条新闻，可惜办不到。我猜是《舒适时光》那档子事，把你们编辑引到这地方来的，是不是？不过说真的，那份报可真是个怪东西。喏，几个星期前它还是那种带回家念给孩子听的玩意儿。我一个朋友以前期期都买。忽然间它吆喝一声改头换面，开始抨击这些廉租公寓，捧小子布雷迪什么的。我真闹不懂。我只知道，这地方已经给它闹得人人议论了。喏，你们自己瞧瞧，你们编辑不是打发你们下来采访这条新闻了吗。不过哎，你们《舒适时光》那伙人可是冒大险了。我直说吧，千真万确。对面那边在搞什么名堂，我碰巧略知一二，我告诉你们，他们要是不赶紧收手，有热闹瞧的。某先生——」他顿住，嘿嘿一笑，「琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）可不是坐着不动、一笑了之的主儿。他要动真格的了。哎，二位是哪家报馆的？」

「《舒适时光》，古奇同志。」普史密斯答道。「就在你身后、横在你与门之间的，是温莎同志，我们的主编。在下普史密斯，副刊编辑。」

===Original===
For a moment the inwardness of the information did not seem to come home to Mr. Gooch. Then it hit him. He spun round. Billy Windsor was standing with his back against the door and a more than nasty look on his face.

“What’s all this?” demanded Mr. Gooch.

“I will explain all,” said Psmith soothingly. “In the first place, however, this matter of Comrade Spaghetti’s rent. Sooner than see that friend of my boyhood slung out to do the wandering-child-in-the-snow act, I will brass up for him.”

“Confound his rent. Let me out.”

===Chinese===
有一会儿，这条信息的内在含义似乎没有钻进古奇先生的脑子。随即它砸中了他。他猛地转过身。比利·温莎正背靠着门站着，脸上是一副凶得不能再凶的神色。

「这是干什么？」古奇先生质问。

「一切自会解释。」普史密斯安抚道。「不过首先，是意面同志的房租问题。与其眼看我的少年之交被扫地出门、演一出雪中流浪儿的戏，我宁可替他掏腰包。」

「他的房租见鬼去吧。放我出去。」

===Original===
“Business before pleasure. How much is it? Twelve dollars? For the privilege of suffocating in this compact little Black Hole? By my halidom, Comrade Gooch, that gentleman whose name you are so shortly to tell us has a very fair idea of how to charge! But who am I that I should criticise? Here are the simoleons, as our young friend, Comrade Maloney, would call them. Push me over a receipt.”

“Let me out.”

“Anon, gossip, anon.—Shakespeare. First, the receipt.”

Mr. Gooch scribbled a few words in his notebook and tore out the page. Psmith thanked him.

===Chinese===
「先公事后享乐。多少来着？十二块？就为换得在这个紧凑的小黑洞里窒息的特权？以我的圣物起誓，古奇同志，那位你马上就要说出大名的先生，收费的行情可是摸得门儿清！可我算老几，敢说三道四？大洋在此——用我们年轻朋友马洛尼同志的说法。递一张收据给我。」

「放我出去。」

「这就来，老伙计，这就来。——莎士比亚。先来收据。」

古奇先生在记事本上潦草写了几笔，撕下那一页。普史密斯向他道谢。

===Original===
“I will see that it reaches Comrade Spaghetti,” he said. “And now to a more important matter. Don’t put away that notebook. Turn to a clean page, moisten your pencil, and write as follows. Are you ready? By the way, what is your Christian name? … Gooch, Gooch, this is no way to speak! Well, if you are sensitive on the point, we will waive the Christian name. It is my duty to tell you, however, that I suspect it to be Percy. Let us push on. Are you ready, once more? Pencil moistened? Very well, then. ‘I’—comma—‘being of sound mind and body’—comma—‘and a bright little chap altogether’—comma—Why, you’re not writing.”

“Let me out,” bellowed Mr. Gooch. “I’ll summon you for assault and battery. Playing a fool game like this! Get away from that door.”

===Chinese===
「我会保证它送到意面同志手里。」他说。「现在说一件更要紧的事。别收起那本子。翻到干净的一页，润润铅笔，照下面写。准备好了吗？对了，你的教名叫什么？……古奇，古奇，这可不是说话的规矩！好吧，你若对这个敏感，教名的事我们略过不表。不过我有责任告诉你，我猜是珀西。我们继续。再问一遍，准备好了？铅笔润好了？很好。那么：『我』——逗号——『身心健康』——逗号——『且是个机灵的小家伙』——逗号——咦，你怎么不写。」

「放我出去！」古奇先生怒吼。「我要告你们人身侵害。玩这种蠢把戏！滚开，别挡门。」

===Original===
“There has been no assault and battery yet, Comrade Gooch, but who shall predict how long so happy a state of things will last? Do not be deceived by our gay and smiling faces, Comrade Gooch. We mean business. Let me put the whole position of affairs before you; and I am sure a man of your perception will see that there is only one thing to be done.”

He dusted the only chair in the room with infinite care and sat down. Billy Windsor, who had not spoken a word or moved an inch since the beginning of the interview, continued to stand and be silent. Mr. Gooch shuffled restlessly in the middle of the room.

“As you justly observed a moment ago,” said Psmith, “the staff of *Cosy Moments* is taking big risks. We do not rely on your unsupported word for that. We have had practical demonstration of the fact from one J. Repetto, who tried some few nights ago to put us out of business. Well, it struck us both that we had better get hold of the name of the blighter who runs these tenements as quickly as possible, before Comrade Repetto’s next night out. That is what we should like you to give us, Comrade Gooch. And we should like it in writing. And, on second thoughts, in ink. I have one of those patent non-leakable fountain pens in my pocket. The Old Journalist’s Best Friend. Most of the ink has come out and is permeating the lining of my coat, but I think there is still sufficient for our needs. Remind me later, Comrade Gooch, to continue on the subject of fountain pens. I have much to say on the theme. Meanwhile, however, business, business. That is the cry.”

===Chinese===
「人身侵害嘛，还没有，古奇同志，可谁敢担保这般幸福的状态能维持多久？别让我们欢快的笑脸骗了你，古奇同志。我们是动真格的。且听我把全局摆在你面前；我确信，以你的悟性，自会看出只有一件事可做。」

他无比仔细地掸了掸屋里仅有的一把椅子，坐了下来。比利·温莎自这场会谈开始就没说过一个字、挪过一寸，继续站着，继续沉默。古奇先生在屋子当中局促地挪来挪去。

「正如你方才公允指出的，」普史密斯说，「《舒适时光》的同人正在冒大险。这一点我们并非只凭你一面之词。某位 J·雷佩托已向我们实际演示过——几个夜晚之前，他想让我们歇业关门。于是我们俩不约而同地想到，最好赶在雷佩托同志下一次夜游之前，尽快搞到经营这些廉租公寓的那位瘟神的大名。这就是我们想请你给的，古奇同志。而且我们想要书面的。再一琢磨，用墨水笔。我口袋里正有一支专利防漏自来水笔。老记者的挚友。大半墨水已经漏出来，正浸透在我外套的衬里里，但我想余下的还够我们用。回头提醒我，古奇同志，接着谈谈自来水笔这个题目。关于它我有许多话要说。不过眼下，谈正事，谈正事。这是当前的呼声。」

===Original===
He produced a pen and an old letter, the last page of which was blank, and began to write.

“How does this strike you?” he said. “ ‘I’—(I have left a blank for the Christian name: you can write it in yourself later)—‘I, blank Gooch, being a collector of rents in Pleasant Street, New York, do hereby swear’—hush, Comrade Gooch, there is no need to do it yet—‘that the name of the owner of the Pleasant Street tenements, who is responsible for the perfectly foul conditions there, is—’ And that is where you come in, Comrade Gooch. That is where we need your specialised knowledge. Who is he?”

Billy Windsor reached out and grabbed the rent collector by the collar. Having done this, he proceeded to shake him.

Billy was muscular, and his heart was so much in the business that Mr. Gooch behaved as if he had been caught in a high wind. It is probable that in another moment the desired information might have been shaken out of him, but before this could happen there was a banging at the door, followed by the entrance of Master Maloney. For the first time since Psmith had known him, Pugsy was openly excited.

===Chinese===
他掏出一支笔和一封旧信——信的末页还是空白——动笔写了起来。

「这个开头你觉得如何？」他说。「『我』——（教名处我留了空白：你回头自己填上）——『我，某某·古奇，系纽约普莱森特街收租员，兹郑重宣誓』——嘘，古奇同志，暂且不必起誓——『普莱森特街廉租公寓业主、对彼处污浊不堪之状况负全责者的姓名，为——』接下来就轮到你了，古奇同志。这正是我们仰仗你专业知识的地方。他是谁？」

比利·温莎伸手一把揪住收租员的领子，揪住之后，接着便摇晃起他来。

比利筋骨强健，干这活又全情投入，古奇先生晃得像被卷进了大风。再过片刻，想要的信息多半就能从他身上摇出来了，可就在这时，门外响起擂门声，马洛尼少爷闯了进来。自普史密斯认识他以来，普格西头一回公开地激动了。

===Original===
“Say,” he began, “youse had better beat it quick, you had. Dey’s coming!”

“And now go back to the beginning, Comrade Maloney,” said Psmith patiently, “which in the exuberance of the moment you have skipped. Who are coming?”

“Why, dem. De guys.”

Psmith shook his head.

“Your habit of omitting essentials, Comrade Maloney, is going to undo you one of these days. When you get to that ranch of yours, you will probably start out to gallop after the cattle without remembering to mount your mustang. There are four million guys in New York. Which section is it that is coming?”

“Gum! I don’t know how many dere is ob dem. I seen Spider Reilly an’ Jack Repetto an’—”

===Chinese===
「哎，」他开口道，「你们最好赶紧开溜，真的。他们来了！」

「现在请从头说起，马洛尼同志，」普史密斯耐心地说，「方才情急，你把开头跳过去了。谁来了？」

「就他们呀。那帮家伙。」

普史密斯摇了摇头。

「你这种漏掉要件的毛病，马洛尼同志，早晚要坑了你。等你到了你那牧场，八成会撒腿去追牛群，却忘了先跨上你的野马。纽约的家伙有四百万。来的是哪一路？」

「嚯！俺不知道他们有多少。俺瞧见蜘蛛莱利、杰克·雷佩托，还有——」

===Original===
“Say no more,” said Psmith. “If Comrade Repetto is there, that is enough for me. I am going to get on the roof and pull it up after me.”

Billy released Mr. Gooch, who fell, puffing, on to the low bed, which stood in one corner of the room.

“They must have spotted us as we were coming here,” he said, “and followed us. Where did you see them, Pugsy?”

“On de street just outside. Dere was a bunch of dem talkin’ togedder, and I hears dem say you was in here. One of dem seen you come in, an dere ain’t no ways out but de front, so dey ain’t hurryin’! Dey just reckon to pike along upstairs, lookin’ into each room till dey finds you. An dere’s a bunch of dem goin’ to wait on de street in case youse beat it past down de stairs while de udder guys is rubberin’ for youse. Say, gents, it’s pretty fierce, dis proposition. What are youse goin’ to do?”

===Chinese===
「不必多说了。」普史密斯说。「雷佩托同志既然在场，对我就足够了。我打算上房顶去，随后把入口收上来。」

比利放开古奇先生，后者呼哧带喘地瘫倒在屋角那张矮床上。

「咱们来这儿的路上准是被他们盯上了，」他说，「一路跟来的。你在哪儿看见他们的，普格西？」

「就在外头街上。一帮人正扎堆嘀咕，俺听见他们说你们在这儿。其中一个瞧见你们进来的，这楼除了前门没别的出口，所以他们也不着急！他们就打算慢悠悠上楼，一间间屋瞧，直到找着你们。还有一帮要守在街上，防着你们趁那帮家伙搜的时候溜下楼开溜。哎，二位，这局面够呛。你们打算怎么办？」

===Original===
Mr. Gooch, from the bed, laughed unpleasantly.

“I guess you ain’t the only assault-and-battery artists in the business,” he said. “Looks to me as if someone else was going to get shaken up some.”

Billy looked at Psmith.

“Well?” he said. “What shall we do? Go down and try and rush through?”

Psmith shook his head.

“Not so, Comrade Windsor, but about as much otherwise as you can jolly well imagine.”

===Chinese===
古奇先生在床上不怀好意地笑了。

「我猜干人身侵害这行的，可不止你们两位艺术家。」他说。「依我看，别人也要挨顿好摇了。」

比利看向普史密斯。

「怎么样？」他说。「我们该怎么办？下楼硬冲出去？」

普史密斯摇了摇头。

「非也，温莎同志，而且恰恰相反到你能想象的最大限度。」

===Original===
“Well, what then?”

“We will stay here. Or rather we will hop nimbly up on to the roof through that skylight. Once there, we may engage these varlets on fairly equal terms. They can only get through one at a time. And while they are doing it I will give my celebrated imitation of Horatius. We had better be moving. Our luggage, fortunately, is small. Merely Comrade Gooch. If you will get through the skylight, I will pass him up to you.”

Mr. Gooch, with much verbal embroidery, stated that he would not go. Psmith acted promptly. Gripping the struggling rent collector round the waist, and ignoring his frantic kicks as mere errors in taste, he lifted him to the trapdoor, whence the head, shoulders and arms of Billy Windsor protruded into the room. Billy collected the collector, and then Psmith turned to Pugsy.

===Chinese===
「那怎么办？」

「我们留在这儿。确切说，我们轻捷地跳上那个天窗，到房顶上去。一旦上去了，我们便可与这帮恶徒在相当公平的条件下交战。他们一次只能过来一个。趁他们往过钻的工夫，我将献上我那远近闻名的贺雷修斯模仿秀。我们最好动身。所幸我们的行李不多。只有古奇同志一件。你先钻上天窗，我把他递给你。」

古奇先生旁征博引、千言万语，声明他坚决不去。普史密斯雷厉风行。他拦腰抱住挣扎的收租员，把他疯狂的蹬踹只当作趣味上的小小失误，全不理会，把他举到活板门口——比利的脑袋、肩膀和手臂正从那里探进屋来。比利收下了收租员，随后普史密斯转向普格西。

===Original===
“Comrade Maloney.”

“Huh?”

“Have I your ear?”

“Huh?”

“Are you listening till you feel that your ears are the size of footballs? Then drink this in. For weeks you have been praying for a chance to show your devotion to the great cause; or if you haven’t, you ought to have been. That chance has come. You alone can save us. In a sense, of course, we do not need to be saved. They will find it hard to get at us, I fancy, on the roof. But it ill befits the dignity of the editorial staff of a great New York weekly to roost like pigeons for any length of time; and consequently it is up to you.”

“Shall I go for de cops, Mr. Smith?”

“No, Comrade Maloney, I thank you. I have seen the cops in action, and they did not impress me. We do not want allies who will merely shake their heads at Comrade Repetto and the others, however sternly. We want someone who will swoop down upon these merry roisterers, and, as it were, soak to them good. Do you know where Dude Dawson lives?”

===Chinese===
「马洛尼同志。」

「啊？」

「你在听吗？」

「啊？」

「你听进去了吗，听到觉得耳朵有足球那么大了吗？那就把这个灌进去。几个星期以来，你一直在祈求一个机会，向伟大的事业献上你的忠诚；就算你没有，你也早该这么做了。机会来了。唯有你能救我们。当然，从某种意义上说，我们并不需要拯救。在房顶上，我想他们很难够到我们。可一家纽约大周刊的编辑部同人，像鸽子一样久久栖在檐上，实在有失体统；所以，这事就全看你的了。」

「俺去叫警察吗，史密斯先生？」

「不必了，马洛尼同志，谢谢。我见识过警察执行任务，未能给我留下深刻印象。我们不要只会朝雷佩托同志之流摇头的盟友，无论摇得多么严厉。我们要的是能凌空扑向这伙欢乐酒徒、并且狠狠教训他们一顿的人。你知道纨绔子道森住哪儿吗？」

===Original===
The light of intelligence began to shine in Master Maloney’s face. His eye glistened with respectful approval. This was strategy of the right sort.

“Dude Dawson? Nope. But I can ask around.”

“Do so, Comrade Maloney. And when found, tell him that his old college chum, Spider Reilly, is here. He will not be able to come himself, I fear, but he can send representatives.”

“Sure.”

“That’s all, then. Go downstairs with a gay and jaunty air, as if you had no connection with the old firm at all. Whistle a few lively bars. Make careless gestures. Thus shall you win through. And now it would be no bad idea, I fancy, for me to join the rest of the brains of the paper up aloft. Off you go, Comrade Maloney. And, in passing, don’t take a week about it. Leg it with all the speed you possess.”

===Chinese===
智慧之光在马洛尼少爷脸上亮起。他眼睛放光，满是敬佩的赞许。这才是正路子的战略。

「纨绔子道森？不知道。可俺可以四处打听。」

「就这样办，马洛尼同志。找到之后告诉他，他的老大学同窗蜘蛛莱利正在此地。恐怕他本人无法亲临，但他可以派代表来。」

「好嘞。」

「那么，就这么些。下楼去时要满面春风、脚步轻快，装作跟老东家毫无瓜葛的样子。吹几小节欢快的口哨。做几个漫不经心的手势。如此你便能闯过去。而现在，我想，我也不妨上去与本报其余的头脑们会合了。去吧，马洛尼同志。顺便说一句，别磨蹭一个礼拜。撒开你的腿，能跑多快跑多快。」

===Original===
Pugsy vanished, and Psmith closed the door behind him. Inspection revealed the fact that it possessed no lock. As a barrier it was useless. He left it ajar, and, jumping up, gripped the edge of the opening in the roof and pulled himself through.

Billy Windsor was seated comfortably on Mr. Gooch’s chest a few feet away. By his side was his big stick. Psmith possessed himself of this, and looked about him. The examination was satisfactory. The trapdoor appeared to be the only means of access to the roof, and between their roof and that of the next house there was a broad gulf.

“Practically impregnable,” he murmured. “Only one thing can dish us, Comrade Windsor; and that is if they have the sense to get on to the roof next door and start shooting. Even in that case, however, we have cover in the shape of the chimneys. I think we may fairly say that all is well. How are you getting along? Has the patient responded at all?”

“Not yet,” said Billy. “But he’s going to.”

===Chinese===
普格西一溜烟没了影，普史密斯关上他身后的门。一番检查发现，门上没有锁。作为屏障它一无是处。他虚掩上门，纵身一跃，抓住屋顶开口的边沿，引体钻了上去。

几步开外，比利·温莎正舒舒服服地坐在古奇先生的胸口上。他手边放着那根大手杖。普史密斯把它拿到手里，环顾四周。检查结果令人满意。这活板门似乎是登上房顶的唯一通道，他们的房顶与隔壁房子之间，隔着一道宽宽的鸿沟。

「几乎固若金汤。」他喃喃道。「只有一件事能坏我们的事，温莎同志：那就是他们若想得起来，登上隔壁房顶开枪。不过即便那样，我们还有烟囱作掩体。我想我们大可宣布万事大吉。你那边进展如何？病人有反应吗？」

「还没有。」比利说。「快了。」

===Original===
“He will be in your charge. I must devote myself exclusively to guarding the bridge. It is a pity that the trap has not got a bolt this side. If it had, the thing would be a perfect picnic. As it is, we must leave it open. But we mustn’t expect everything.”

Billy was about to speak, but Psmith suddenly held up his hand warningly. From the room below came a sound of feet.

For a moment the silence was tense. Then from Mr. Gooch’s lips there escaped a screech.

“This way! They’re up—”

The words were cut short as Billy banged his hand over the speaker’s mouth. But the thing was done.

“On top de roof,” cried a voice. “Dey’ve beaten it for de roof.”

The chair rasped over the floor. Feet shuffled. And then, like a jack-in-the-box, there popped through the opening a head and shoulders.

===Chinese===
「他归你管。我则须专心致志守卫大桥。可惜这活板门这一侧没有门闩。若有，此事便是完美的野餐了。如今只好敞着它。但我们不能事事求全。」

比利正要开口，普史密斯忽然举手示意噤声。楼下屋里传来了脚步声。

一时间，寂静紧绷。随即，古奇先生嘴里迸出一声尖叫。

「这边！他们在上——」

比利一巴掌捂住说话人的嘴，话音戛然而止。可事情已经成了。

「在房顶上。」一个声音喊道。「他们跑房顶上去了。」

椅子刮过地板。脚步杂沓。接着，就像弹簧玩偶匣里蹦出的小人，一个脑袋和一副肩膀从开口里冒了出来。

## I “Cosy Moments” / 《舒适时光》
===Original===
The man in the street would not have known it, but a great crisis was imminent in New York journalism.

Everything seemed much as usual in the city. The cars ran blithely on Broadway. Newsboys shouted “Wux-try!” into the ears of nervous pedestrians with their usual Caruso-like vim. Society passed up and down Fifth Avenue in its automobiles, and was there a furrow of anxiety upon Society’s brow? None. At a thousand street corners a thousand policemen preserved their air of massive superiority to the things of this world. Not one of them showed the least sign of perturbation. Nevertheless, the crisis was at hand. Mr. J. Fillken Wilberfloss, editor-in-chief of *Cosy Moments*, was about to leave his post and start on a ten weeks’ holiday.

===Chinese===
寻常路人不会知道，纽约新闻界一场大危机已迫在眉睫。

城里一切看来照旧。汽车欢快地跑在百老汇。报童们照例以卡鲁索（Caruso）般的充沛嗓门，冲着神经紧张的行人耳朵里喊「号外——！」。上流社会乘着汽车在第五大道来来往往，他们额头上可有一丝焦虑的皱纹？一条也没有。一千个街角上，一千名警察保持着对尘世万物的巍然优越感，没有一人显露半点慌乱的迹象。然而，危机就在眼前。《舒适时光》的主编 J·菲尔肯·威尔伯弗洛斯先生（Mr. J. Fillken Wilberfloss）即将离任，开始十个星期的休假。

===Original===
In New York one may find every class of paper which the imagination can conceive. Every grade of society is catered for. If an Eskimo came to New York, the first thing he would find on the bookstalls in all probability would be the *Blubber Magazine*, or some similar production written by Eskimo for Eskimo. Everybody reads in New York, and reads all the time. The New Yorker peruses his favourite paper while he is being jammed into a crowded compartment on the subway or leaping like an antelope into a moving street car.

There was thus a public for *Cosy Moments*. *Cosy Moments*, as its name (an inspiration of Mr. Wilberfloss’s own) is designed to imply, is a journal for the home. It is the sort of paper which the father of the family is expected to take home with him from his office and read aloud to the chicks before bedtime. It was founded by its proprietor, Mr. Benjamin White, as an antidote to yellow journalism. One is forced to admit that up to the present yellow journalism seems to be competing against it with a certain measure of success. Headlines are still of as generous a size as heretofore, and there is no tendency on the part of editors to scamp the details of the last murder case.

Nevertheless, *Cosy Moments* thrives. It has its public.

===Chinese===
在纽约，想象得出的任何一类报纸都能找到。社会各阶层各有专属的读物。假使一个爱斯基摩人来到纽约，他在书报摊上头一样撞见的，十有八九会是《鲸脂杂志》（*Blubber Magazine*），或是其他由爱斯基摩人写给爱斯基摩人看的类似刊物。在纽约人人都读报，而且时时刻刻都在读。纽约人一边被人往地铁拥挤的车厢里塞，一边还能像羚羊似的跃进正开行的街车，同时照读心爱的报纸不误。

这样，《舒适时光》也就有了它的读者。正如其刊名（那是威尔伯弗洛斯先生本人的神来之笔）所要昭示的，这是一份面向家庭的刊物。这类报纸，按理应当由一家之父从办公室带回家，在孩子们睡前念给他们听。它由老板本杰明·怀特先生（Mr. Benjamin White）创办，本意是解黄色新闻之毒。人们不得不承认，时至今日，黄色新闻与之竞争似乎还占了点上风。标题照旧印得阔气十足，编辑们对最近一桩凶杀案的细节也毫无潦草敷衍之势。

尽管如此，《舒适时光》依旧兴旺。它有它的读者群。

===Original===
Its contents are mildly interesting, if you like that sort of thing. There is a “Moments in the Nursery” page, conducted by Luella Granville Waterman, to which parents are invited to contribute the bright speeches of their offspring, and which bristles with little stories about the nursery canary, by Jane (aged six), and other works of rising young authors. There is a “Moments of Meditation” page, conducted by the Reverend Edwin T. Philpotts; a “Moments Among the Masters” page, consisting of assorted chunks looted from the literature of the past, when foreheads were bulgy and thoughts profound, by Mr. Wilberfloss himself; one or two other pages; a short story; answers to correspondents on domestic matters; and a “Moments of Mirth” page, conducted by an alleged humorist of the name of B. Henderson Asher, which is about the most painful production ever served up to a confiding public.

===Chinese===
它的内容还算有点趣味——假如你喜欢这一路的话。有一版「育婴时光」，由卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼主持，邀请父母们投稿记下自家孩子的妙语，版面上密密麻麻尽是「简（六岁）」写的育婴室金丝雀小故事，以及文坛新苗的其他大作。有一版「冥想时光」，由埃德温·T·菲尔波茨牧师（the Reverend Edwin T. Philpotts）主持；有一版「名家时光」，由威尔伯弗洛斯先生亲自操刀，内容是从往昔文学——那个前额隆起、思想深邃的年代——里东抄西摘的杂烩；此外还有一两版其他内容、一篇短篇小说、家事读者问答，以及一版「欢笑时光」，由一位据说名叫 B·亨德森·阿舍（B. Henderson Asher）的幽默作家主持，那大约是有史以来端给轻信读者们的最折磨人的读物。

===Original===
The guiding spirit of *Cosy Moments* was Mr. Wilberfloss. Circumstances had left the development of the paper mainly to him. For the past twelve months the proprietor had been away in Europe, taking the waters at Carlsbad, and the sole control of *Cosy Moments* had passed into the hands of Mr. Wilberfloss. Nor had he proved unworthy of the trust or unequal to the duties. In that year *Cosy Moments* had reached the highest possible level of domesticity. Anything not calculated to appeal to the home had been rigidly excluded. And as a result the circulation had increased steadily. Two extra pages had been added, “Moments Among the Shoppers” and “Moments with Society.” And the advertisements had grown in volume. But the work had told upon the Editor. Work of that sort carries its penalties with it. Success means absorption, and absorption spells softening of the brain. Whether it was the strain of digging into the literature of the past every week, or the effort of reading B. Henderson Asher’s “Moments of Mirth” is uncertain. At any rate, his duties, combined with the heat of a New York summer, had sapped Mr. Wilberfloss’s health to such an extent that the doctor had ordered him ten weeks’ complete rest in the mountains. This Mr. Wilberfloss could, perhaps, have endured, if this had been all. There are worse places than the mountains of America in which to spend ten weeks of the tail-end of summer, when the sun has ceased to grill and the mosquitoes have relaxed their exertions. But it was not all. The doctor, a farseeing man who went down to first causes, had absolutely declined to consent to Mr. Wilberfloss’s suggestion that he should keep in touch with the paper during his vacation. He was adamant. He had seen copies of *Cosy Moments* once or twice, and he refused to permit a man in the editor’s state of health to come in contact with Luella Granville Waterman’s “Moments in the Nursery” and B. Henderson Asher’s “Moments of Mirth.” The medicine-man put his foot down firmly.

===Chinese===
《舒适时光》的主心骨正是威尔伯弗洛斯先生。种种情势使这份报纸的发展大权基本落在他一人手里。过去十二个月，老板远在欧洲，在卡尔斯巴德（Carlsbad）疗养饮用矿泉水，《舒适时光》的独断大权便移交到了威尔伯弗洛斯先生手中。他也确实不曾辜负这份信托，也没有辜负这份职守。这一年里，《舒适时光》把家庭气做到了登峰造极。凡属不打算讨家庭欢心的东西，一概严拒门外。结果发行量稳步上升。还添了两版新栏目，「购物时光」和「社交时光」。广告也越登越多。可是这份操劳在主编身上索了代价。这类工作总带着它的惩罚。成功意味着沉迷，沉迷则导致脑力软化。究竟是每周挖掘往昔文学伤了神，还是拜读 B·亨德森·阿舍的「欢笑时光」费了力，无从查考。总之，职务之劳加上纽约的暑热，把威尔伯弗洛斯先生的身体耗得大夫下令叫他去山里彻底静养十个星期。倘若仅此而已，威尔伯弗洛斯先生或许还忍得下。夏末十个星期在美国山间度过——那时日头已不再烤人，蚊子也已松了劲——比这糟的地方有的是。可惜不止于此。那位大夫是个追根究底、深谋远虑的人，威尔伯弗洛斯先生提议休假期间与报纸保持联络，被大夫断然驳回。他毫不动摇。他见过一两期《舒适时光》，说什么也不肯让一位健康处于如此状况的人接触卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼的「育婴时光」和 B·亨德森·阿舍的「欢笑时光」。这位医士铁面无私，寸步不让。

===Original===
“You must not see so much as the cover of the paper for ten weeks,” he said. “And I’m not so sure that it shouldn’t be longer. You must forget that such a paper exists. You must dismiss the whole thing from your mind, live in the open, and develop a little flesh and muscle.”

To Mr. Wilberfloss the sentence was almost equivalent to penal servitude. It was with tears in his voice that he was giving his final instructions to his subeditor, in whose charge the paper would be left during his absence. He had taken a long time doing this. For two days he had been fussing in and out of the office, to the discontent of its inmates, more especially Billy Windsor, the subeditor, who was now listening moodily to the last harangue of the series, with the air of one whose heart is not in the subject. Billy Windsor was a tall, wiry, loose-jointed young man, with unkempt hair and the general demeanour of a caged eagle. Looking at him, one could picture him astride of a bronco, rounding up cattle, or cooking his dinner at a campfire. Somehow he did not seem to fit into the *Cosy Moments* atmosphere.

===Chinese===
「十个星期之内，连报纸的封面你也不许看一眼，」他说。「我还拿不准是不是该更久些。你必须忘掉世上有这么一份报纸。你必须把这一切从脑子里统统赶出去，住到露天里去，好歹长点儿肉、长点儿筋骨。」

对威尔伯弗洛斯先生而言，这判决几乎等同于服苦役。他正带着哭腔向副主编交代最后的指示——他离职期间，报纸将托付此人掌管。这事他已经干了很久。两天来他在办公室里进进出出、忙忙叨叨，惹得同人们怨声不绝，尤其是副主编比利·温莎，此刻正闷闷地听着这一系列训话的末一篇，一副心不在焉的神气。比利·温莎是个瘦长精悍、骨架松散的年轻人，头发蓬乱，通身气度像头关在笼里的鹰。看他一眼，人们就能想见他跨着野马围拢牛群、或在篝火边烧晚饭的模样。不知怎么，他似乎和《舒适时光》的空气格格不入。

===Original===
“Well, I think that that is all, Mr. Windsor,” chirruped the editor. He was a little man with a long neck and large *pince-nez*, and he always chirruped. “You understand the general lines on which I think the paper should be conducted?” The subeditor nodded. Mr. Wilberfloss made him tired. Sometimes he made him more tired than at other times. At the present moment he filled him with an aching weariness. The editor meant well, and was full of zeal, but he had a habit of covering and recovering the ground. He possessed the art of saying the same obvious thing in a number of different ways to a degree which is found usually only in politicians. If Mr. Wilberfloss had been a politician, he would have been one of those dealers in glittering generalities who used to be fashionable in American politics.

“There is just one thing,” he continued. “Mrs. Julia Burdett Parslow is a little inclined—I may have mentioned this before—”

“You did,” said the subeditor.

===Chinese===
「那么，我想就这些了，温莎先生。」主编叽叽喳喳地说。他是个长脖子、大夹鼻眼镜的小个子男人，说起话来永远叽叽喳喳。「我主张办这份报纸的总方针，你都明白了吧？」副主编点点头。威尔伯弗洛斯先生让他疲乏。有时候比别的时候更让他疲乏。此刻他浑身充满了一种隐隐作痛的倦意。这位主编心眼不坏，也满腔热忱，只是有个把同一块地翻来覆去耕的习惯。他把一句人人明白的废话用种种不同说法讲出来的功夫，已臻通常只在政客身上得见的造诣。倘若威尔伯弗洛斯先生当了政客，准是那种惯卖浮华空话的角色——这号人从前在美国政坛上曾经风行一时。

「还有一件事，」他接着说。「朱莉娅·伯德特·帕斯洛太太（Mrs. Julia Burdett Parslow）有点儿爱——我以前大概提过——」

「提过。」副主编说。

===Original===
Mr. Wilberfloss chirruped on, unchecked.

“A little inclined to be late with her ‘Moments with Budding Girlhood.’ If this should happen while I am away, just write her a letter, quite a pleasant letter, you understand, pointing out the necessity of being in good time. The machinery of a weekly paper, of course, cannot run smoothly unless contributors are in good time with their copy. She is a very sensible woman, and she will understand, I am sure, if you point it out to her.”

The subeditor nodded.

===Chinese===
威尔伯弗洛斯先生毫不理会，继续叽喳。

「有点儿爱拖稿，误她那版『豆蔻时光』的事。我不在期间若再发生，就给她写封信，一封客客气气的信，你懂的，指出准时交稿的必要。一份周报的机器，撰稿人若不按时交稿，当然就转不灵光。她是位明白事理的女士，只要你说给她听，我相信她一定领会。」

副主编点了点头。

===Original===
“And there is just one other thing. I wish you would correct a slight tendency I have noticed lately in Mr. Asher to be just a trifle—well, not precisely *risky*, but perhaps a shade *broad* in his humour.”

“His what?” said Billy Windsor.

“Mr. Asher is a very sensible man, and he will be the first to acknowledge that his sense of humour has led him just a little beyond the bounds. You understand? Well, that is all, I think. Now I must really be going, or I shall miss my train. Goodbye, Mr. Windsor.”

“Goodbye,” said the subeditor thankfully.

===Chinese===
「还有最后一件事。希望你纠正一下我近来察觉的阿舍先生的一种小小倾向——他的幽默，稍稍有点儿，怎么说呢，倒不完全是出格，只是或许略微有点儿荤。」

「他的什么？」比利·温莎说。

「阿舍先生是个非常明白事理的人，他会头一个承认，他的幽默感有点儿把他带过了界限。你明白吗？好，我想就这些了。现在我真得走了，不然要误火车了。再见，温莎先生。」

「再见。」副主编如释重负地说。

===Original===
At the door Mr. Wilberfloss paused with the air of an exile bidding farewell to his native land, sighed, and trotted out.

Billy Windsor put his feet upon the table, and with a deep scowl resumed his task of reading the proofs of Luella Granville Waterman’s “Moments in the Nursery.”

===Chinese===
到了门口，威尔伯弗洛斯先生驻足片刻，一派流亡者告别故土的神情，叹了口气，小步跑着出去了。

比利·温莎把两只脚往桌上一搁，眉头紧锁，重新拾起那桩苦差——校读卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼「育婴时光」的清样。

## IX Full Steam Ahead / 全速前进
===Original===
When Psmith returned to the office, he found Billy Windsor in the doorway, just parting from a thickset young man, who seemed to be expressing his gratitude to the editor for some good turn. He was shaking him warmly by the hand.

Psmith stood aside to let him pass.

“An old college chum, Comrade Windsor?” he asked.

“That was Kid Brady.”

“The name is unfamiliar to me. Another contributor?”

“He’s from my part of the country—Wyoming. He wants to fight anyone in the world at a hundred and thirty-three pounds.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯回到报社时，看见比利·温莎正站在门口，与一个敦实的青年道别——那人似乎在为某桩好事向主编表达感激，正热情地握着他的手。

普史密斯侧身让他过去。

「大学时代的老哥们儿，温莎同志？」他问。

「那是小子布雷迪。」

「这名字我不熟。又一位撰稿人？」

「他跟我同乡——怀俄明来的。他想在一百三十三磅量级上跟全世界随便谁干一架。」

===Original===
“We all have our hobbies. Comrade Brady appears to have selected a somewhat exciting one. He would find stamp collecting less exacting.”

“It hasn’t given him much excitement so far, poor chap,” said Billy Windsor. “He’s in the championship class, and here he has been pottering about New York for a month without being able to get a fight. It’s always the way in this rotten East,” continued Billy, warming up as was his custom when discussing a case of oppression and injustice. “It’s all graft here. You’ve got to let half a dozen brutes dip into every dollar you earn, or you don’t get a chance. If the kid had a manager, he’d get all the fights he wanted. And the manager would get nearly all the money. I’ve told him that we will back him up.”

“You have hit it, Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith with enthusiasm. “*Cosy Moments* shall be Comrade Brady’s manager. We will give him a much-needed boost up in our columns. A sporting section is what the paper requires more than anything.”

===Chinese===
「人各有好。布雷迪同志选的这一样似乎略嫌刺激。集邮会让他觉得省力些。」

「到眼下为止还没什么刺激可说呢，可怜的家伙。」比利·温莎说。「他是有冠军级身手的，可他却在纽约瞎转悠了一个月，连一场比赛都捞不着。这个烂透的东部向来就是这副德行。」比利接着说，一讨论起压迫和不公的案例，他照例火气就往上冒。「这儿到处是敲竹杠。你每挣一块钱，都得让六七个狠角色伸手抽头，不然你连机会都没有。这小子要是有个经纪人，想要多少场比赛就有多少场。而钱也几乎全进了经纪人的腰包。我跟他说了，我们给他撑腰。」

「你算说对了，温莎同志。」普史密斯热情地说。「《舒适时光》就来当布雷迪同志的经纪人。我们要在版面上给他来一场久旱逢甘霖的力捧。这份报纸最缺的，就是体育版。」

===Original===
“If things go on as they’ve started, what it will require still more will be a fighting editor. Pugsy tells me you had visitors while I was out.”

“A few,” said Psmith. “One or two very entertaining fellows. Comrades Asher, Philpotts, and others. I have just been giving them a bite of lunch at the Knickerbocker.”

“Lunch!”

“A most pleasant little lunch. We are now as brothers. I fear I have made you perhaps a shade unpopular with our late contributors; but these things must be. We must clench our teeth and face them manfully. If I were you, I think I should not drop in at the house of Comrade Asher and the rest to take potluck for some little time to come. In order to soothe the squad I was compelled to curse you to some extent.”

“Don’t mind me.”

“I think I may say I didn’t.”

===Chinese===
「照眼下这个开局闹下去，它更缺的还会是一位战斗编辑。普格西告诉我，我出去那会儿来了几位访客。」

「来了几位。」普史密斯说。「一两位非常有意思的人物。阿舍同志、菲尔波茨同志，还有其他几位。我刚在尼克博克饭店请他们吃了顿便饭。」

「便饭！」

「一顿极其愉快的小宴。我们现在亲如兄弟了。恐怕我让诸位前撰稿人对你生了几分嫌恶；但事已至此，别无他法。我们必须咬紧牙关，拿出男子汉气概去面对。我要是你，往后一小段日子里，就不会顺道去阿舍同志和其他诸位府上赶饭吃。为了安抚那队人马，我不得不在一定程度上把你咒骂了一番。」

「不用顾着我。」

「我想我可以这么说：我确实没顾着。」

===Original===
“Say, look here, you must charge up the price of that lunch to the office. Necessary expenses, you know.”

“I could not dream of doing such a thing, Comrade Windsor. The whole affair was a great treat to me. I have few pleasures. Comrade Asher alone was worth the money. I found his society intensely interesting. I have always believed in the Darwinian theory. Comrade Asher confirmed my views.”

===Chinese===
「我说，听我说，那顿饭的钱你必须记到报社账上。必要开销嘛，你懂的。」

「这种事我想都不会想，温莎同志。整件事于我是莫大的享受。我的乐趣本就不多。光是阿舍同志就值回票价。我发现与他为伴妙趣无穷。我一向信奉达尔文学说。阿舍同志印证了我的观点。」

===Original===
They went into the inner office. Psmith removed his hat and coat.

“And now once more to work,” he said. “Psmith the *flaneur* of Fifth Avenue ceases to exist. In his place we find Psmith the hardheaded subeditor. Be so good as to indicate a job of work for me, Comrade Windsor. I am champing at my bit.”

Billy Windsor sat down, and lit his pipe.

“What we want most,” he said thoughtfully, “is some big topic. That’s the only way to get a paper going. Look at *Everybody’s Magazine*. They didn’t amount to a row of beans till Lawson started his ‘Frenzied Finance’ articles. Directly they began, the whole country was squealing for copies. *Everybody’s* put up their price from ten to fifteen cents, and now they lead the field.”

===Chinese===
两人走进里间办公室。普史密斯摘下帽子，脱了外衣。

「好了，重新开工。」他说。「第五大道的闲逛者（flaneur）普史密斯就此退场。顶替他上场的，是头脑冷静的副主编普史密斯。劳驾给我指派一件活儿吧，温莎同志。我嚼着嚼子，早已按捺不住。」

比利·温莎坐下，点了烟斗。

「我们现在最需要的，」他若有所思地说，「是某个大题目。这是把一张报纸办活的唯一法门。瞧瞧《人人杂志》（Everybody’s Magazine）。在劳森（Lawson）开始写他的《疯狂金融》（Frenzied Finance）系列文章之前，他们不值一排豆子。文章一开张，全国都嗷嗷叫着抢购。《人人》把定价从十分涨到十五分，如今领跑整个行业。」

===Original===
“The country must squeal for *Cosy Moments*,” said Psmith firmly. “I fancy I have a scheme which may not prove wholly scaly. Wandering yesterday with Comrade Jackson in a search for Fourth Avenue, I happened upon a spot called Pleasant Street. Do you know it?”

Billy Windsor nodded.

“I went down there once or twice when I was a reporter. It’s a beastly place.”

“It is a singularly beastly place. We went into one of the houses.”

===Chinese===
「全国也必须为《舒适时光》嗷嗷叫。」普史密斯语气坚定。「我自忖有一个方案，未必全然寒碜。昨天我和杰克逊同志寻摸第四大道时，误打误撞撞见一个叫普莱森特街的地方。你知道那儿吗？」

比利·温莎点了点头。

「我当记者那会儿下去过一两回。那是个糟糕透顶的地方。」

「那是个格外糟糕透顶的地方。我们进了其中一栋楼。」

===Original===
“They’re pretty bad.”

“Who owns them?”

“I don’t know. Probably some millionaire. Those tenement houses are about as paying an investment as you can have.”

“Hasn’t anybody ever tried to do anything about them?”

“Not so far as I know. It’s pretty difficult to get at these fellows, you see. But they’re fierce, aren’t they, those houses!”

===Chinese===
「那些楼都够呛。」

「产业归谁？」

「不知道。多半是哪位百万富翁。那些廉租公寓楼，算得上数一数二赚钱的投资。」

「就从来没有人想法子治治它们？」

「据我所知没有。你要明白，很难揪住这些家伙的把柄。不过那些楼也真够吓人的，是不是！」

===Original===
“What,” asked Psmith, “is the precise difficulty of getting at these merchants?”

“Well, it’s this way. There are all sorts of laws about the places, but anyone who wants can get round them as easy as falling off a log. The law says a tenement house is a building occupied by more than two families. Well, when there’s a fuss, all the man has to do is to clear out all the families but two. Then, when the inspector fellow comes along, and says, let’s say, ‘Where’s your running water on each floor? That’s what the law says you’ve got to have, and here are these people having to go downstairs and out of doors to fetch their water supplies,’ the landlord simply replies, ‘Nothing doing. This isn’t a tenement house at all. There are only two families here.’ And when the fuss has blown over, back come the rest of the crowd, and things go on the same as before.”

===Chinese===
「那么，」普史密斯问，「揪住这帮买卖人的确切难处何在？」

「嗐，是这么回事。关于这些地方的法规多得很，可谁想绕，都易如反掌。法律规定，住两家以上人家的房子才算廉租公寓。于是风声一紧，房主只消把两家之外的人家统统清走。等督察员来了，说——比如这么说吧——『你们每层的自来水呢？法律明文规定必须有，可这些人却得下楼、出屋去打水』，房东只消回一句：『没的事。这压根不是廉租公寓。这儿就住了两家人。』等风头一过，其余人马杀回来，一切照旧。」

===Original===
“I see,” said Psmith. “A very cheery scheme.”

“Then there’s another thing. You can’t get hold of the man who’s really responsible, unless you’re prepared to spend thousands ferreting out evidence. The land belongs in the first place to some corporation or other. They lease it to a lessee. When there’s a fuss, they say they aren’t responsible, it’s up to the lessee. And he lies so low that you can’t find out who he is. It’s all just like the East. Everything in the East is as crooked as Pearl Street. If you want a square deal, you’ve got to come out Wyoming way.”

===Chinese===
「我明白了。」普史密斯说。「一套非常欢快的机制。」

「还有一桩。你抓不到真正担责任的人，除非你舍得花几千块钱去挖证据。地皮首先归某个什么公司所有。他们把地转租给一个二房东（lessee）。风声一紧，他们就说自己概不负责，有事找二房东。而那位藏得深的，你连他是谁都查不出来。这整套就跟东部一个样。东部的一切都像珍珠街（Pearl Street）一样七拐八绕。想要公道，你就得奔怀俄明那边去。」

===Original===
“The main problem, then,” said Psmith, “appears to be the discovery of the lessee, lad? Surely a powerful organ like *Cosy Moments*, with its vast ramifications, could bring off a thing like that?”

“I doubt it. We’ll try, anyway. There’s no knowing but what we may have luck.”

“Precisely,” said Psmith. “Full steam ahead, and trust to luck. The chances are that, if we go on long enough, we shall eventually arrive somewhere. After all, Columbus didn’t know that America existed when he set out. All he knew was some highly interesting fact about an egg. What that was, I do not at the moment recall, but it bucked Columbus up like a tonic. It made him fizz ahead like a two-year-old. The facts which will nerve us to effort are two.
