In the first place, we know that there must be someone at the bottom of the business. Secondly, as there appears to be no law of libel whatsoever in this great and free country, we shall be enabled to haul up our slacks with a considerable absence of restraint.”

===Chinese===
「那么，」普史密斯说，「主要问题看来就是查明那位二房东了，老弟？以《舒适时光》这样一台势力雄厚、爪牙四布的机关，总不至于办不成这点小事吧？」

「我看悬。不过总要试试。没准儿咱们运气好呢。」

「正是。」普史密斯说。「全速前进，听天由命。只要走得够久，我们多半终能抵达什么地方。毕竟，哥伦布启航时也不知道美洲的存在。他知道的只是关于一只鸡蛋的某个极有趣的事实。那到底是什么，我眼下回忆不起来了，但它像一剂强心针，给哥伦布提了气，让他像匹两岁小马似的喷着响鼻一路飞奔。为我们鼓劲的事实有两条。其一，我们知道这桩生意背后必定有个主使。其二，这个伟大而自由的国家看来压根没有诽谤法，我们便可以毫无顾忌、甩开膀子大干一场。」

===Original===
“Sure,” said Billy Windsor. “Which of us is going to write the first article?”

“You may leave it to me, Comrade Windsor. I am no hardened old journalist, I fear, but I have certain qualifications for the post. A young man once called at the office of a certain newspaper, and asked for a job. ‘Have you any special line?’ asked the editor. ‘Yes,’ said the bright lad, ‘I am rather good at invective.’ ‘Any special kind of invective?’ queried the man up top. ‘No,’ replied our hero, ‘just general invective.’ Such is my own case, Comrade Windsor. I am a very fair purveyor of good, general invective. And as my visit to Pleasant Street is of such recent date, I am tolerably full of my subject. Taking full advantage of the benevolent laws of this country governing libel, I fancy I will produce a screed which will make this anonymous lessee feel as if he had inadvertently seated himself upon a tin-tack. Give me pen and paper, Comrade Windsor, instruct Comrade Maloney to suspend his whistling till such time as I am better able to listen to it; and I think we have got a success.”

===Chinese===
「那是。」比利·温莎说。「头一篇文章由咱们哪个来写？」

「这就交给我吧，温莎同志。我算不上久经沙场的老报人，但对这个职位，我自有几分资质。从前有个年轻人到某家报社登门求职。『你有什么专长吗？』主编问。『有，』那个机灵的小伙子说，『我相当擅长骂人。』『骂人有什么专门的门类吗？』上头那位追问。『没有，』咱们的主人公答道，『就是泛泛地骂人。』我的情况正是如此，温莎同志。我是个相当不错的供货商，供应上好的、泛用的骂人文章。加之我造访普莱森特街尚在新鲜头上，肚子里这个题目装得满满的。趁势把这国关于诽谤的仁慈法律用到十足，我想我能写出一篇雄文，让那位隐名二房东感觉像一屁股坐在了图钉上。给我纸笔，温莎同志；吩咐马洛尼同志暂停吹口哨，等我腾出耳朵好生欣赏；然后我想，咱们就大功告成了。」

## X Going Some / 越干越来劲
===Original===
There was once an editor of a paper in the Far West who was sitting at his desk, musing pleasantly of life, when a bullet crashed through the window and embedded itself in the wall at the back of his head. A happy smile lit up the editor’s face. “Ah,” he said complacently, “I knew that Personal column of ours was going to be a success!”

What the bullet was to the Far West editor, the visit of Mr. Francis Parker to the offices of *Cosy Moments* was to Billy Windsor.

===Chinese===
从前，远西地区有位报纸编辑正坐在桌前，悠然遐想着人生，一颗子弹破窗而入，钉进了他后脑勺后面的墙里。编辑脸上绽开幸福的微笑。「啊，」他心满意足地说，「我早知道咱们那个『私事专栏』准能成功！」

那颗子弹之于远西编辑，正如弗朗西斯·帕克先生（Mr. Francis Parker）造访《舒适时光》编辑部之于比利·温莎。

===Original===
It occurred in the third week of the new regime of the paper. *Cosy Moments*, under its new management, had bounded ahead like a motorcar when the throttle is opened. Incessant work had been the order of the day. Billy Windsor’s hair had become more dishevelled than ever, and even Psmith had at moments lost a certain amount of his dignified calm. Sandwiched in between the painful case of Kid Brady and the matter of the tenements, which formed the star items of the paper’s contents, was a mass of bright reading dealing with the events of the day. Billy Windsor’s newspaper friends had turned in some fine, snappy stuff in their best Yellow Journal manner, relating to the more stirring happenings in the city. Psmith, who had constituted himself guardian of the literary and dramatic interests of the paper, had employed his gift of general invective to considerable effect, as was shown by a conversation between Master Maloney and a visitor one morning, heard through the open door.

===Chinese===
这事发生在报纸新班底上任的第三个礼拜。《舒适时光》在新管理层治下，像油门踩到底的汽车一样蹿了出去。连轴转的苦干成了每天的章程。比利·温莎的头发比以往更加凌乱，就连普史密斯，也有几回失掉了他的几分雍容镇定。夹在小子布雷迪那桩伤心案子和廉租公寓问题——本报内容的两大明星栏目——之间的，是一大批鲜亮的读物，谈的全是时事。比利·温莎的报界朋友们以他们最拿手的黄色新闻笔法，交来了一批漂亮利落的稿子，讲的都是本城比较轰动的事件。普史密斯自任本报文学与戏剧利益的守护人，把他那泛用型谩骂的天赋发挥得相当见效——某天早晨马洛尼少爷和一位来访者的一段对话便可见一斑，那是透过敞开的门听来的。

===Original===
“I wish to see the editor of this paper,” said the visitor.

“Editor not in,” said Master Maloney, untruthfully.

“Ha! Then when he returns I wish you to give him a message.”

“Sure.”

===Chinese===
「我要见这家报纸的编辑。」来客说。

「编辑不在。」马洛尼少爷说，说的是假话。

「哈！那么等他回来，请替我传句话。」

「行。」

===Original===
“I am Aubrey Bodkin, of the National Theatre. Give him my compliments, and tell him that Mr. Bodkin does not lightly forget.”

An unsolicited testimonial which caused Psmith the keenest satisfaction.

===Chinese===
「我是国家剧院的奥布里·博德金（Aubrey Bodkin）。请向他致意，并告诉他，博德金先生可不是轻易忘事的人。」

这份不请自来的颂词，让普史密斯感到了最热切的满足。

===Original===
The section of the paper devoted to Kid Brady was attractive to all those with sporting blood in them. Each week there appeared in the same place on the same page a portrait of the Kid, looking moody and important, in an attitude of self-defence, and under the portrait the legend, “Jimmy Garvin must meet this boy.” Jimmy was the present holder of the lightweight title. He had won it a year before, and since then had confined himself to smoking cigars as long as walking sticks and appearing nightly as the star in a music hall sketch entitled “A Fight for Honour.” His reminiscences were appearing weekly in a Sunday paper. It was this that gave Psmith the idea of publishing Kid Brady’s autobiography in *Cosy Moments*, an idea which made the Kid his devoted adherent from then on. Like most pugilists, the Kid had a passion for bursting into print, and his life had been saddened up to the present by the refusal of the press to publish his reminiscences. To appear in print is the fighter’s accolade. It signifies that he has arrived. Psmith extended the hospitality of page four of *Cosy Moments* to Kid Brady, and the latter leaped at the chance. He was grateful to Psmith for not editing his contributions. Other pugilists, contributing to other papers, groaned under the supervision of a member of the staff who cut out their best passages and altered the rest into Addisonian English. The readers of *Cosy Moments* got Kid Brady raw.

===Chinese===
本报献给小子布雷迪的版面，对所有血管里流着运动血液的人都有莫大的吸引力。每周同一页的同一位置都会登出一张小子的肖像：神情阴郁而郑重，摆着自卫的架势，肖像下方一行题词：「吉米·加文必须应战此子。」吉米是现任轻量级头衔持有者。一年前他赢得了这个头衔，此后便专心致志于抽手杖那么长的雪茄，每晚在小品剧《为荣誉而战》（A Fight for Honour）里挂头牌。他的回忆录正在一家礼拜日报上连载。正是这事给了普史密斯灵感，在《舒适时光》上连载小子布雷迪的自传——这个主意使小子从此成了他死心塌地的拥趸。与多数拳手一样，小子有一种见报瘾，而在此之前，报界一概拒登他的回忆录，这让他的人生一直黯淡无光。见报是拳手的加冕礼，意味着他已经功成名就。普史密斯把《舒适时光》第四版的版面向小子布雷迪敞开，后者立即扑向了这个机会。他感激普史密斯不加删改地刊登他的来稿。别的拳手给别的报纸投稿，都在报馆里某位先生的监督下叫苦不迭——那人把他们最精彩的段落删掉，把余下的部分改成艾迪生式英语。《舒适时光》的读者读到的是原汁原味的小子布雷迪。

===Original===
“Comrade Brady,” said Psmith to Billy, “has a singularly pure and pleasing style. It is bound to appeal powerfully to the many-headed. Listen to this bit. Our hero is fighting Battling Jack Benson in that eminent artist’s native town of Louisville, and the citizens have given their native son the Approving Hand, while receiving Comrade Brady with chilly silence. Here is the Kid on the subject: ‘I looked around that house, and I seen I hadn’t a friend in it. And then the gong goes, and I says to myself how I has one friend, my poor old mother way out in Wyoming, and I goes in and mixes it, and then I seen Benson losing his goat, so I ups with an awful half-scissor hook to the plexus, and in the next round I seen Benson has a chunk of yellow, and I gets in with a haymaker and I picks up another sleep-producer from the floor and hands it him, and he takes the count all right.’ … Crisp, lucid, and to the point. That is what the public wants. If this does not bring Comrade Garvin up to the scratch, nothing will.”

===Chinese===
「布雷迪同志，」普史密斯对比利说，「有一种出奇纯正、讨人喜欢的文笔。它必定能强烈打动芸芸众生。听听这一段。我们的主人公正在路易斯维尔（Louisville）——那位杰出艺术家的家乡——与『斗士』杰克·本森（Battling Jack Benson）交手，当地市民把『赞许之手』给了他们的本土子弟，却用冷冰冰的沉默迎接布雷迪同志。以下是小子就此事的论述：『我朝场子四面一瞅，就明白我在里头一个朋友也没有。跟着锣响了，我寻思我还有一位朋友，就是远在怀俄明的我那可怜的老娘，我就冲上去跟他对撸，跟着我就瞅见本森慌了神，于是我抡起一记要命的半剪式勾拳砸他太阳神经丛，到下一回合，我瞅见本森露了怯，我就送上一记大抡拳，又从地板上再捡一记催眠丸递给他，他就规规矩矩数完十下啦。』……干脆、晓畅、切中要害。这才是公众要的东西。这要是还请不动加文同志上擂台，那就没什么能请得动了。」

===Original===
But the feature of the paper was the “Tenement” series. It was late summer now, and there was nothing much going on in New York. The public was consequently free to take notice. The sale of *Cosy Moments* proceeded briskly. As Psmith had predicted, the change of policy had the effect of improving the sales to a marked extent. Letters of complaint from old subscribers poured into the office daily. But, as Billy Windsor complacently remarked, they had paid their subscriptions, so that the money was safe whether they read the paper or not. And, meanwhile, a large new public had sprung up and was growing every week. Advertisements came trooping in. *Cosy Moments*, in short, was passing through an era of prosperity undreamed of in its history.

“Young blood,” said Psmith nonchalantly, “young blood. That is the secret. A paper must keep up to date, or it falls behind its competitors in the race. Comrade Wilberfloss’s methods were possibly sound, but too limited and archaic. They lacked ginger. We of the younger generation have our fingers more firmly on the public pulse. We read off the public’s unspoken wishes as if by intuition. We know the game from A to Z.”

===Chinese===
但本报真正的王牌，还是「廉租公寓」系列。时下是晚夏，纽约没什么大事发生。公众因而得闲关注此事。《舒适时光》的销路蒸蒸日上。正如普史密斯所预言的，改版方针使销量显著攀升。老订户的抱怨信每天涌进编辑部。不过，正如比利·温莎心满意足的点评：订费他们已经付了，所以无论他们读不读，钱都已经稳稳到手。与此同时，一大批新读者异军突起，且逐周壮大。广告成群结队而来。简而言之，《舒适时光》正经历它历史上做梦也想不到的鼎盛时代。

「新鲜血液，」普史密斯漫不经心地说，「新鲜血液。这就是秘诀。报纸必须跟上时代，否则就会在竞赛中落到对手后面。威尔伯弗洛斯同志的方法或许不无道理，但太局限、太陈旧了。缺一股姜味儿。我们这些新一代，指头把公众的脉搏搭得更紧。我们像凭直觉似的，就能读出公众没说出口的愿望。这门行当，我们从 A 到 Z 无一不晓。」

===Original===
At this moment Master Maloney entered, bearing in his hand a card.

“‘Francis Parker’?” said Billy, taking it. “Don’t know him.”

“Nor I,” said Psmith. “We make new friends daily.”

“He’s a guy with a tall-shaped hat,” volunteered Master Maloney, “an’ he’s wearin’ a dude suit an’ shiny shoes.”

“Comrade Parker,” said Psmith approvingly, “has evidently not been blind to the importance of a visit to *Cosy Moments*. He has dressed himself in his best. He has felt, rightly, that this is no occasion for the old straw hat and the baggy flannels. I would not have it otherwise. It is the right spirit. Shall we give him audience, Comrade Windsor?”

“I wonder what he wants.”

===Chinese===
这时马洛尼少爷走了进来，手里拿着一张名片。

「『弗朗西斯·帕克』？」比利接过名片，「不认识。」

「我也不认识，」普史密斯说，「我们天天结识新朋友。」

「是个戴高筒帽的家伙，」马洛尼少爷主动通报，「穿着花花公子西装，皮鞋锃亮。」

「帕克同志显然没有忽视造访《舒适时光》的重要性，」普史密斯赞许地说，「他穿上了自己最好的行头。他准确地感觉到，这不是戴旧草帽、穿宽松法兰绒裤的场合。我正求之不得。这是正确的精神。我们要不要接见他，温莎同志？」

「不知道他想干什么。」

===Original===
“That,” said Psmith, “we shall ascertain more clearly after a personal interview. Comrade Maloney, show the gentleman in. We can give him three and a quarter minutes.”

Pugsy withdrew.

Mr. Francis Parker proved to be a man who might have been any age between twenty-five and thirty-five. He had a smooth, clean-shaven face, and a catlike way of moving. As Pugsy had stated in effect, he wore a tailcoat, trousers with a crease which brought a smile of kindly approval to Psmith’s face, and patent-leather boots of pronounced shininess. Gloves and a tall hat, which he carried, completed an impressive picture.

He moved softly into the room.

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，」普史密斯说，「面谈之后我们就会弄得更清楚。马洛尼同志，请那位先生进来。我们可以给他三分十五秒。」

普格西退了出去。

弗朗西斯·帕克先生原来是个年龄可能在二十五到三十五岁之间的男人。他脸庞光滑，胡子刮得干干净净，举手投足有一股猫样的劲儿。正如普格西方才实际上描述的那样，他穿一件燕尾服，裤子上一道笔挺的裤线——那裤线让普史密斯脸上浮起慈祥赞许的微笑——脚上是一双亮度惊人的漆皮靴。手中的手套和高筒帽，凑成了一幅气派的画像。

他轻手轻脚地走进屋来。

===Original===
“I wished to see the editor.”

Psmith waved a hand towards Billy.

“The treat has not been denied you,” he said. “Before you is Comrade Windsor, the Wyoming crackerjack. He is our editor. I myself—I am Psmith—though but a subordinate, may also claim the title in a measure. Technically, I am but a subeditor; but such is the mutual esteem in which Comrade Windsor and I hold each other that we may practically be said to be inseparable. We have no secrets from each other. You may address us both impartially. Will you sit for a space?”

He pushed a chair towards the visitor, who seated himself with the care inspired by a perfect trouser-crease. There was a momentary silence while he selected a spot on the table on which to place his hat.

===Chinese===
「我是想见见编辑。」

普史密斯朝比利一挥手。

「这个眼福没有让你落空，」他说，「你面前这位是温莎同志，怀俄明奇才。他就是我们的编辑。我自己嘛——我是普史密斯——虽然只是个下属，某种程度上也可以领有这个头衔。严格说来，我只是个助理编辑；但温莎同志与我彼此敬重如此之深，以致我们实际上可以说密不可分。我们之间没有秘密。你可以不分彼此地对我们两位一同讲话。请稍坐片刻？」

他把一把椅子推向来客，来客怀着那道完美裤线所激发的小心坐下了。他挑着桌上一块放帽子的地方，一时无人说话。

===Original===
“The style of the paper has changed greatly, has it not, during the past few weeks?” he said. “I have never been, shall I say, a constant reader of *Cosy Moments*, and I may be wrong. But is not its interest in current affairs a recent development?”

“You are very right,” responded Psmith. “Comrade Windsor, a man of alert and restless temperament, felt that a change was essential if *Cosy Moments* was to lead public thought. Comrade Wilberfloss’s methods were good in their way. I have no quarrel with Comrade Wilberfloss. But he did not lead public thought. He catered exclusively for children with water on the brain, and men and women with solid ivory skulls. Comrade Windsor, with a broader view, feels that there are other and larger publics. He refuses to content himself with ladling out a weekly dole of mental predigested breakfast food. He provides meat. He—”

“Then—excuse me—” said Mr. Parker, turning to Billy, “You, I take it, are responsible for this very vigorous attack on the tenement house owners?”

“You can take it I am,” said Billy.

===Chinese===
「过去这几个礼拜，贵报的风格大变，是吧？」他说，「说来惭愧，我从来不曾是《舒适时光》的固定读者，我也许弄错了。但它对时事的这份兴趣，是不是近来才有的事？」

「你说得极是，」普史密斯应道，「温莎同志，一位性情警觉、不安于现状的人士，感到《舒适时光》要想引领公众思想，改革势在必行。威尔伯弗洛斯同志的方法各有其道理，我对威尔伯弗洛斯同志无可指摘。但他不曾引领公众思想。他专事伺候脑积水的儿童，和长着实心象牙脑壳的男女。温莎同志眼界更宽，感到世上还有别样的、更广大的公众。他不肯满足于每周施舍一勺预先消化好的精神早餐糊。他供应的是肉。他——」

「那么——恕我冒昧——」帕克先生转向比利说，「对廉租公寓房东们的这番火力凶猛的抨击，该由您负责吧？」

「你尽管这么理解。」比利说。

===Original===
Psmith interposed.

“We are both responsible, Comrade Parker. If any husky guy, as I fancy Master Maloney would phrase it, is anxious to aim a swift kick at the man behind those articles, he must distribute it evenly between Comrade Windsor and myself.”

“I see.” Mr. Parker paused. “They are—er—very outspoken articles,” he added.

“Warm stuff,” agreed Psmith. “Distinctly warm stuff.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯插了进来。

「我们两个都有责任，帕克同志。倘若哪个彪形大汉——我想马洛尼少爷大约会这么措辞——急着朝那些文章背后的男人飞起一脚，这一脚必须由温莎同志和我二人均匀分摊。」

「明白。」帕克先生顿了顿。「这些文章——呃——相当直言不讳。」他补充道。

「火辣文字，」普史密斯附和道，「不折不扣的火辣文字。」

===Original===
“May I speak frankly?” said Mr. Parker.

“Assuredly, Comrade Parker. There must be no secrets, no restraint between us. We would not have you go away and say to yourself, ‘Did I make my meaning clear? Was I too elusive?’ Say on.”

“I am speaking in your best interests.”

“Who would doubt it, Comrade Parker. Nothing has buoyed us up more strongly during the hours of doubt through which we have passed than the knowledge that you wish us well.”

===Chinese===
「我可以直言吗？」帕克先生说。

「尽管说，帕克同志。我们之间不可以有秘密、有拘束。我们不愿让您走了以后独自嘀咕：『我把意思说明白了吗？我是不是太隐晦了？』请讲。」

「我是为你们的切身利益说话。」

「谁会怀疑呢，帕克同志。在我们一路走来的那些怀疑时刻里，最能支撑我们的，莫过于知道您盼着我们好。」

===Original===
Billy Windsor suddenly became militant. There was a feline smoothness about the visitor which had been jarring upon him ever since he first spoke. Billy was of the plains, the home of blunt speech, where you looked your man in the eye and said it quick. Mr. Parker was too bland for human consumption. He offended Billy’s honest soul.

“See here,” cried he, leaning forward, “what’s it all about? Let’s have it. If you’ve anything to say about those articles, say it right out. Never mind our best interests. We can look after them. Let’s have what’s worrying you.”

Psmith waved a deprecating hand.

“Do not let us be abrupt on this happy occasion. To me it is enough simply to sit and chat with Comrade Parker, irrespective of the trend of his conversation. Still, as time is money, and this is our busy day, possibly it might be as well, sir, if you unburdened yourself as soon as convenient. Have you come to point out some flaw in those articles? Do they fall short in any way of your standard for such work?”

===Chinese===
比利·温莎骤然好斗起来。这位来客身上有一股猫一样的圆滑，从一开口就在比利的神经上磨啊磨。比利是平原上的人，那里盛行直来直去的说话：你直视着对方的眼睛，一句话痛快撂出去。帕克先生温吞得叫人无法下咽。他冒犯了比利的正直灵魂。

「我说，」他俯身向前喊道，「这到底是怎么回事？摊开说吧。你要是对那些文章有什么话，就直截了当地说出来。别管什么我们的利益，我们自己照看得过来。把你憋着的话倒出来。」

普史密斯摆手示意不必。

「在这大喜的日子里，咱们不要弄得剑拔弩张。对我来说，能坐着同帕克同志闲谈便是足够的乐事，管他谈话往哪个方向漂。不过，时间就是金钱，今天又是我们的大忙日，先生，您若能尽快卸下心头重担，或许不失为上策。您是来指出那些文章的什么瑕疵吗？它们在哪些方面够不上您对这类作品的标准？」

===Original===
Mr. Parker’s smooth face did not change its expression, but he came to the point.

“I should not go on with them if I were you,” he said.

“Why?” demanded Billy.

“There are reasons why you should not,” said Mr. Parker.

“And there are reasons why we should.”

“Less powerful ones.”

===Chinese===
帕克先生光滑的脸庞纹丝没变，但他直奔了正题。

「我要是你们，就不接着登了。」他说。

「为什么？」比利逼问。

「你们不该接着登，是有原因的。」帕克先生说。

「我们该接着登，也有原因。」

「没那么硬气。」

===Original===
There proceeded from Billy a noise not describable in words. It was partly a snort, partly a growl. It resembled more than anything else the preliminary sniffing snarl a bulldog emits before he joins battle. Billy’s cowboy blood was up. He was rapidly approaching the state of mind in which the men of the plains, finding speech unequal to the expression of their thoughts, reach for their guns.

Psmith intervened.

“We do not completely gather your meaning, Comrade Parker. I fear we must ask you to hand it to us with still more breezy frankness. Do you speak from purely friendly motives? Are you advising us to discontinue the articles merely because you fear that they will damage our literary reputation? Or are there other reasons why you feel that they should cease? Do you speak solely as a literary connoisseur? Is it the style or the subject matter of which you disapprove?”

===Chinese===
比利体内发出一种难以言传的声音。它一半是喷气，一半是低吼，最像的东西莫过于斗牛犬临战之前那种嗅嗅咆哮的前奏。比利的牛仔血液沸腾了。他正迅速逼近那种心境——平原上的汉子们一到这境地，便发觉语言不足以表达思想，转而伸手去摸枪。

普史密斯出面调停了。

「我们没有完全领会您的意思，帕克同志。恐怕我们得请您以更痛快的坦率把它递过来。您纯粹是出于友好的动机吗？您劝我们停登那些文章，仅仅是因为担心它们有损我们的文学声誉吗？还是说，您觉得它们该停，另有原因？您纯粹是以文学鉴赏家的身份说话吗？您不赞成的是文体，还是题材？」

===Original===
Mr. Parker leaned forward.

“The gentleman whom I represent—”

“Then this is no matter of your own personal taste? You are an emissary?”

“These articles are causing a certain inconvenience to the gentleman whom I represent. Or, rather, he feels that, if continued, they may do so.”

“You mean,” broke in Billy explosively, “that if we kick up enough fuss to make somebody start a commission to inquire into this rotten business, your friend who owns the private Hades we’re trying to get improved, will have to get busy and lose some of his money by making the houses fit to live in? Is that it?”

===Chinese===
帕克先生俯身向前。

「我所代表的那位先生——」

「那么这就不是您个人趣味的问题了？您是位使者？」

「这些文章正给我所代表的那位先生造成某种不便。或者说，他感觉到，倘若继续登下去，它们可能会造成不便。」

「你的意思是，」比利炸雷般地插进来，「只要我们把动静闹大，闹到有人成立个委员会来查这摊烂事，你那位朋友——咱们正想法子改造的那座私人地狱的房东——就得忙活起来，掏出点钱，把那些房子拾掇得能住人？是这么回事吧？」

===Original===
“It is not so much the money, Mr. Windsor, though, of course, the expense would be considerable. My employer is a wealthy man.”

“I bet he is,” said Billy disgustedly. “I’ve no doubt he makes a mighty good pile out of Pleasant Street.”

“It is not so much the money,” repeated Mr. Parker, “as the publicity involved. I speak quite frankly. There are reasons why my employer would prefer not to come before the public just now as the owner of the Pleasant Street property. I need not go into those reasons. It is sufficient to say that they are strong ones.”

“Well, he knows what to do, I guess. The moment he starts in to make those houses decent, the articles stop. It’s up to him.”

===Chinese===
「倒不主要是钱的事，温莎先生，虽然开销当然不会小。我的雇主是个有钱人。」

「那还用说。」比利厌恶地说，「他靠普莱森特街（Pleasant Street）榨出的油水，保准相当可观。」

「倒不主要是钱，」帕克先生重复道，「要紧的是这会招人耳目。我坦白讲。我的雇主此刻不愿以普莱森特街产业所有人的身份在公众面前曝光，这里头有种种原因。原因何在，我不必细说。只需讲一句：这些原因相当有力。」

「那好，该怎么办他自己清楚。他什么时候开始把那些房子收拾得像点样，文章什么时候停。球在他那边。」

===Original===
Psmith nodded.

“Comrade Windsor is correct. He has hit the mark and rung the bell. No conscientious judge would withhold from Comrade Windsor a cigar or a coconut, according as his private preference might dictate. That is the matter in a nutshell. Remove the reason for those very scholarly articles, and they cease.”

Mr. Parker shook his head.

“I fear that is not feasible. The expense of reconstructing the houses makes that impossible.”

“Then there’s no use in talking,” said Billy. “The articles will go on.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯点了点头。

「温莎同志说得对。他正中靶心，铃铛铛作响。任何有良心的裁判都不会克扣温莎同志应得的奖品——一支雪茄，或者一只椰子，全看裁判个人的偏好而定。事情的实质尽在于此。铲除那些学术性极强的文章存在的原因，它们自然就停了。」

帕克先生摇了摇头。

「恐怕这行不通。翻修那些房子的开销，使它不可能。」

「那就没什么好谈的了，」比利说，「文章照登。」

===Original===
Mr. Parker coughed. A tentative cough, suggesting that the situation was now about to enter upon a more delicate phase. Billy and Psmith waited for him to begin. From their point of view the discussion was over. If it was to be reopened on fresh lines, it was for their visitor to effect that reopening.

“Now, I’m going to be frank, gentlemen,” said he, as who should say, “We are all friends here. Let us be hearty.” “I’m going to put my cards on the table, and see if we can’t fix something up. Now, see here: We don’t want unpleasantness. You aren’t in this business for your healths, eh? You’ve got your living to make, just like everybody else, I guess. Well, see here. This is how it stands. To a certain extant, I don’t mind admitting, seeing that we’re being frank with one another, you two gentlemen have got us—that’s to say, my employer—in a cleft stick. Frankly, those articles are beginning to attract attention, and if they go on there’s going to be a lot of inconvenience for my employer. That’s clear, I reckon. Well, now, here’s a square proposition. How much do you want to stop those articles? That’s straight. I’ve been frank with you, and I want you to be frank with me. What’s your figure? Name it, and, if it’s not too high, I guess we needn’t quarrel.”

===Chinese===
帕克先生咳嗽了一声。一声试探性的咳嗽，暗示局面即将进入一个更微妙的阶段。比利和普史密斯等他开口。在他们看来，讨论已经结束。若要另起炉灶重开谈判，那也得由来客来开这个头。

「好了，二位，我打算打开天窗说亮话，」他说，那架势仿佛在说「这儿都是朋友，咱们热络一点」，「我打算把牌摊到桌面上，看看咱们能不能谈出点什么。是这么回事：我们不想伤和气。你们干这行又不是为了保健，对吧？我猜你们跟所有人一样，也得挣钱吃饭。好，听着，事情是这样。既然咱们彼此坦白，我不妨承认：你们二位已经把我们——也就是我的雇主——架在了劈柴棍上，进退两难。坦白说，那些文章开始引人注意了，再登下去，我的雇主麻烦多多。这一点我想很清楚。那么，好，这是个公道的提议：停掉那些文章，你们要多少钱？这是实在话。我对你们坦白了，也请你们对我坦白。你们的价码是多少？开出来，只要不离谱，我看咱们犯不着伤感情。」

===Original===
He looked expectantly at Billy. Billy’s eyes were bulging. He struggled for speech. He had got as far as “Say!” when Psmith interrupted him. Psmith, gazing sadly at Mr. Parker through his monocle, spoke quietly, with the restrained dignity of some old Roman senator dealing with the enemies of the Republic.

“Comrade Parker,” he said, “I fear that you have allowed constant communication with the conscienceless commercialism of this worldly city to undermine your moral sense. It is useless to dangle rich bribes before our eyes. *Cosy Moments* cannot be muzzled. You doubtless mean well, according to your—if I may say so—somewhat murky lights, but we are not for sale, except at ten cents weekly. From the hills of Maine to the Everglades of Florida, from Sandy Hook to San Francisco, from Portland, Oregon, to Melonsquashville, Tennessee, one sentence is in every man’s mouth. And what is that sentence? I give you three guesses. You give it up? It is this: ‘*Cosy Moments* cannot be muzzled!’”

===Chinese===
他满怀期待地望着比利。比利眼珠子都凸了出来。他挣扎着想说话，才挤出个「我说——」普史密斯就把他截住了。普史密斯透过单片眼镜哀伤地注视着帕克先生，静静地说了话，带着某位古罗马元老应付共和国之敌时的克制威严。

「帕克同志，」他说，「恐怕您已让与这座世俗之城那不讲良心的拜金风过从太密，侵蚀了您的道德感。在咱们眼前晃悠厚重的贿赂是徒劳的。《舒适时光》是堵不住嘴的。按您那——恕我直言——颇为暗淡的良心之光来看，您无疑是一片好意，但我们不出售，除非按每期十美分零售。从缅因的山丘到佛罗里达的大沼泽，从桑迪胡克到旧金山，从俄勒冈的波特兰到田纳西的梅隆斯夸什维尔（Melonsquashville），每个男人嘴边都挂着一句话。哪句话？给您三次机会猜。认输了？是这句：『《舒适时光》是堵不住嘴的！』」

===Original===
Mr. Parker rose.

“There’s nothing more to be done then,” he said.

“Nothing,” agreed Psmith, “except to make a noise like a hoop and roll away.”

“And do it quick,” yelled Billy, exploding like a firecracker.

Psmith bowed.

===Chinese===
帕克先生站起身。

「那就没什么可谈的了。」他说。

「没什么，」普史密斯附和道，「只除了学铁环那样，一路响着滚出去。」

「还要滚得快。」比利喊道，像爆竹一样炸开了。

普史密斯欠身一礼。

===Original===
“Speed,” he admitted, “would be no bad thing. Frankly—if I may borrow the expression—your square proposition has wounded us. I am a man of powerful self-restraint, one of those strong, silent men, and I can curb my emotions. But I fear that Comrade Windsor’s generous temperament may at any moment prompt him to start throwing inkpots. And in Wyoming his deadly aim with the inkpot won him among the admiring cowboys the sobriquet of Crack-Shot Cuthbert. As man to man, Comrade Parker, I should advise you to bound swiftly away.”

“I’m going,” said Mr. Parker, picking up his hat. “And I’ll give you a piece of advice, too. Those articles are going to be stopped, and if you’ve any sense between you, you’ll stop them yourselves before you get hurt. That’s all I’ve got to say, and that goes.”

He went out, closing the door behind him with a bang that added emphasis to his words.

===Chinese===
「速度，」他承认，「确实不是坏事。坦白说——恕我借用您的措辞——您那个公道的提议伤害了我们。我是一个自控力极强的人，寡言强者中的一员，能够驾驭自己的情感。但我担心温莎同志豪爽的天性随时可能驱使他开始扔墨水瓶。想当年在怀俄明，他百发百中的墨水瓶绝技，为他在满心敬仰的牛仔们中间赢得了『神枪手卡斯伯特（Crack-Shot Cuthbert）』的名号。男人对男人，帕克同志，我劝您还是麻溜地弹走为妙。」

「我这就走，」帕克先生拿起帽子说，「我也奉送你们一句忠告。那些文章早晚会被停掉，你们两个但凡还有点脑子，就趁没吃亏自己先停。我要说的就这些，说一不二。」

他出去了，随手把门砰的一声带上，那一声为他这番话加重了语气。

===Original===
“To men of nicely poised nervous organisation such as ourselves, Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith, smoothing his waistcoat thoughtfully, “these scenes are acutely painful. We wince before them. Our ganglions quiver like cinematographs. Gradually recovering command of ourselves, we review the situation. Did our visitor’s final remarks convey anything definite to you? Were they the mere casual badinage of a parting guest, or was there something solid behind them?”

Billy Windsor was looking serious.

===Chinese===
「对像你我这样神经组织精妙平衡的人来说，温莎同志，」普史密斯若有所思地抚平马甲，说道，「这类场面是尖锐的痛苦。我们在它面前瑟缩。我们的神经节像放映机一样颤动。等逐渐恢复了对自己的指挥权，我们来复盘一下局势。咱们这位来客临别的那几句话，在你听来可曾传达出什么确切的含义？那只是告辞的客人顺口的玩笑话，还是背后有什么实实在在的东西？」

比利·温莎神情严肃。

===Original===
“I guess he meant it all right. He’s evidently working for somebody pretty big, and that sort of man would have a pull with all kinds of Thugs. We shall have to watch out. Now that they find we can’t be bought, they’ll try the other way. They mean business sure enough. But, by George, let ’em! We’re up against a big thing, and I’m going to see it through if they put every gang in New York on to us.”

“Precisely, Comrade Windsor. *Cosy Moments*, as I have had occasion to observe before, cannot be muzzled.”

“That’s right,” said Billy Windsor. “And,” he added, with the contented look the Far West editor must have worn as the bullet came through the window, “we must have got them scared, or they wouldn’t have shown their hand that way. I guess we’re making a hit. *Cosy Moments* is going some now.”

===Chinese===
「我看他可不是说着玩的。他显然是在给某个大人物办事，那种人物跟各路恶徒都拉得上关系。我们得当心了。如今他们发现咱们收买不动，就要换另一手了。他们可是动真格的。不过，老天，来就来吧！咱们顶上的是桩大事，就算他们把纽约的黑帮全派到咱们头上，我也要干到底。」

「正是，温莎同志。《舒适时光》，正如我从前就有机会指出的那样，是堵不住嘴的。」

「这就对了，」比利·温莎说。「而且，」他补上一句，脸上带着远西那位编辑在子弹破窗而入时必定有过的那种心满意足的神情，「咱们准是把他们吓着了，不然他们不会那样亮出底牌。我看咱们是打出名堂来了。《舒适时光》如今可是越干越来劲了。」

## XVII Guerilla Warfare / 游击战
===Original===
Thus ended the opening engagement of the campaign, seemingly in a victory for the *Cosy Moments* army. Billy Windsor, however, shook his head.

“We’ve got mighty little out of it,” he said.

“The victory,” said Psmith, “was not bloodless. Comrade Brady’s ear, my hat—these are not slight casualties. On the other hand, surely we are one up? Surely we have gained ground? The elimination of Comrade Repetto from the scheme of things in itself is something. I know few men I would not rather meet in a lonely road than Comrade Repetto. He is one of Nature’s sandbaggers. Probably the thing crept upon him slowly. He started, possibly, in a merely tentative way by slugging one of the family circle. His nurse, let us say, or his young brother. But, once started, he is unable to resist the craving. The thing grips him like dram-drinking. He sandbags now not because he really wants to, but because he cannot help himself. To me there is something consoling in the thought that Comrade Repetto will no longer be among those present.”

===Chinese===
战役的第一场交锋就此收场，表面上看是《舒适时光》大军的胜利。然而比利·温莎摇了摇头。

「咱们从中捞到的好处少得可怜。」他说。

「这场胜利，」普史密斯说，「并非不流血。布雷迪同志的耳朵，我的帽子——这些可不是轻微的伤亡。不过话说回来，咱们总归是领先一分了吧？总归是推进了一步吧？雷佩托同志从万物格局中被除名这件事，本身就算一项收获。比起雷佩托同志，我认识的人里没几个是我更不愿在僻静小路上遇见的。他是造化亲手打造的沙袋客。这毛病多半是慢慢缠上他的。起初，或许只是试探性地敲自家圈子里某一位的闷棍。比方说他的奶妈，或者他的小弟弟。可一旦开了头，他就再也抗拒不了这种瘾。这毛病像贪杯烈酒一样攫住他。他如今抡沙袋，不是因为真心想抡，而是身不由己。想到雷佩托同志从此不会再出现在场面上，我心底便涌起一丝安慰。」

===Original===
“What makes you think that?”

“I should imagine that a benevolent Law will put him away in his little cell for at least a brief spell.”

“Not on your life,” said Billy. “He’ll prove an alibi.”

Psmith’s eyeglass dropped out of his eye. He replaced it, and gazed, astonished, at Billy.

“An alibi? When three keen-eyed men actually caught him at it?”

“He can find thirty toughs to swear he was five miles away.”

===Chinese===
「你凭什么这么想？」

「我料想，仁慈的法律会把他请进那间小号房里，至少小住一阵。」

「想都别想。」比利说。「他会拿出不在场证明。」

普史密斯的单片眼镜从眼眶里掉了下来。他把它装回去，惊愕地瞪着比利。

「不在场证明？三个眼尖的人可是当场逮住他干的？」

「他能找来三十个打手，赌咒发誓说他当时在五英里外。」

===Original===
“And get the court to believe it?” said Psmith.

“Sure,” said Billy disgustedly. “You don’t catch them hurting a gangsman unless they’re pushed against the wall. The politicians don’t want the gangs in gaol, especially as the Aldermanic elections will be on in a few weeks. Did you ever hear of Monk Eastman?”

“I fancy not, Comrade Windsor. If I did, the name has escaped me. Who was this cleric?”

“He was the first boss of the East Side gang, before Kid Twist took it on.”

“Yes?”

===Chinese===
「还能让法庭信以为真？」普史密斯说。

「当然能。」比利厌恶地说。「不逼到墙角，你别想见他们动黑帮分子一根毫毛。政客们不想让黑帮蹲大牢，何况再过几个星期就要进行市议员选举了。你听说过蒙克·伊斯曼（Monk Eastman）吗？」

「我想没有，温莎同志。就算听过，这名字也从我记忆里溜走了。这位教士是何许人？」

「他是下城东区帮的头一任龙头，后来帮派归了小子特威斯特（Kid Twist）。」

「哦？」

===Original===
“He was arrested dozens of times, but he always got off. Do you know what he said once, when they pulled him for thugging a fellow out in New Jersey?”

“I fear not, Comrade Windsor. Tell me all.”

“He said, ‘You’re arresting me, huh? Say, you want to look where you’re goin’; I cut some ice in this town. I made half the big politicians in New York!’ That was what he said.”

“His small talk,” said Psmith, “seems to have been bright and well-expressed. What happened then? Was he restored to his friends and his relations?”

===Chinese===
「他被抓过几十回，可回回都脱了身。你知道有一回他在新泽西地界上行凶揍了人、被警方逮住时，说过什么吗？」

「恐怕不知道，温莎同志。细细讲来。」

「他说：『你们要抓我，是吧？我说，你们走路得看看脚下；我在这个城里是有斤两的。纽约一半的大政客都是我造出来的！』他就是这么说的。」

「他的寒暄，」普史密斯说，「看来既风趣又得体。后来呢？他被送还亲友身边了吗？」

===Original===
“Sure, he was. What do you think? Well, Jack Repetto isn’t Monk Eastman, but he’s in with Spider Reilly, and the Spider’s in with the men behind. Jack’ll get off.”

“It looks to me, Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith thoughtfully, “as if my stay in this great city were going to cost me a small fortune in hats.”

Billy’s prophecy proved absolutely correct. The police were as good as their word. In due season they rounded up the impulsive Mr. Repetto, and he was haled before a magistrate. And then, what a beautiful exhibition of brotherly love and auld-lang-syne camaraderie was witnessed! One by one, smirking sheepishly, but giving out their evidence with unshaken earnestness, eleven greasy, wandering-eyed youths mounted the witness stand and affirmed on oath that at the time mentioned dear old Jack had been making merry in their company in a genial and law-abiding fashion, many, many blocks below the scene of the regrettable assault. The magistrate discharged the prisoner, and the prisoner, meeting Billy and Psmith in the street outside, leered triumphantly at them.

===Chinese===
「当然是。不然还能怎样？嗐，杰克·雷佩托不是蒙克·伊斯曼，但他跟蜘蛛莱利是一伙的，蜘蛛又跟幕后那班人是一伙的。杰克会脱身的。」

「依我看，温莎同志，」普史密斯若有所思地说，「我在这座伟大城市的居留，看来要让我在帽子上破一小笔财了。」

比利的预言分毫不差。警方说到做到。时机一到，他们便将那位冲动的雷佩托先生缉拿归案，押到了治安法官面前。接下来，人们目睹了一场何等美好的表演——手足之爱，加上「友谊地久天长」式的袍泽情谊！十一个油头垢面、贼眼溜溜的青年挨个儿登上证人席，讪讪地笑着，却以毫不动摇的诚恳态度宣誓作证：在所述时刻，亲爱的老杰克正在他们的陪伴下，以和蔼守法的方式寻欢作乐，地点距那起令人遗憾的行凶现场隔了不知多少个街区。治安法官当庭开释了被告，而这位被告在街面上撞见比利和普史密斯时，得意扬扬地朝他们狞笑。

===Original===
Billy stepped up to him. “You may have wriggled out of this,” he said furiously, “but if you don’t get a move on and quit looking at me like that, I’ll knock you over the Singer Building. Hump yourself.”

Mr. Repetto humped himself.

So was victory turned into defeat, and Billy’s jaw became squarer and his eye more full of the light of battle than ever. And there was need of a square jaw and a battle-lit eye, for now began a period of guerilla warfare such as no New York paper had ever had to fight against.

===Chinese===
比利逼上前去。「这回算你滑脱了，」他怒气冲冲地说，「可你要是不赶紧挪窝、还敢这么瞪着我，我就把你打飞过辛格大厦（Singer Building）。滚。」

雷佩托先生赶紧滚了。

胜利就这样变成了失败，而比利的下颌比以往更见方正，眼里也比以往更燃着战斗的光。这副方正的下颌和燃着战火的眼睛确乎派得上用场，因为一段游击战时期就此开场——纽约还从没有哪家报纸不得不与这样的阵仗搏斗过。

===Original===
It was Wheeler, the gaunt manager of the business side of the journal, who first brought it to the notice of the editorial staff. Wheeler was a man for whom in business hours nothing existed but his job; and his job was to look after the distribution of the paper. As to the contents of the paper he was absolutely ignorant. He had been with *Cosy Moments* from its start, but he had never read a line of it. He handled it as if it were so much soap. The scholarly writings of Mr. Wilberfloss, the mirth-provoking sallies of Mr. B. Henderson Asher, the tender outpourings of Luella Granville Waterman—all these were things outside his ken. He was a distributor, and he distributed.

A few days after the restoration of Mr. Repetto to East Side Society, Mr. Wheeler came into the editorial room with information and desire for information.

He endeavoured to satisfy the latter first.

===Chinese===
头一个把这事端到编辑部面前的，是惠勒（Wheeler），本报经营部门的瘦高经理。惠勒这个人，在上班时间里除了本职便目中无他；而他的本职，就是料理报纸的发行。至于报纸的内容，他一窍不通。他从《舒适时光》创刊便效力至今，却从没读过它一行字。他经手它，就像经手一批肥皂。威尔伯弗洛斯先生的学术文章，B·亨德森·阿舍先生逗人发笑的妙语，卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼的绵绵倾诉——这一切都在他的见识之外。他是发行人，他发行。

雷佩托先生荣归下城东区社交界几天之后，惠勒先生走进编辑室，带来一条消息，也想打听一桩事。

他先设法满足后者。

===Original===
“What’s doing, anyway?” he asked. He then proceeded to his information. “Someone’s got it in against the paper, sure,” he said. “I don’t know what it’s all about. I ha’n’t never read the thing. Don’t see what anyone could have against a paper with a name like *Cosy Moments*, anyway. The way things have been going last few days, seems it might be the organ of a blamed mining-camp what the boys have took a dislike to.”

“What’s been happening?” asked Billy with gleaming eyes.

“Why, nothing in the world to fuss about, only our carriers can’t go out without being beaten up by gangs of toughs. Pat Harrigan’s in the hospital now. Just been looking in on him. Pat’s a feller who likes to fight. Rather fight he would than see a ballgame. But this was too much for him. Know what happened? Why, see here, just like this it was. Pat goes out with his cart. Passing through a low-down street on his way uptown he’s held up by a bunch of toughs. He shows fight. Half a dozen of them attend to him, while the rest gets clean away with every copy of the paper there was in the cart. When the cop comes along, there’s Pat in pieces on the ground and nobody in sight but a Dago chewing gum. Cop asks the Dago what’s been doing, and the Dago says he’s only just come round the corner and ha’n’t seen nothing of anybody. What I want to know is, what’s it all about? Who’s got it in for us and why?”

===Chinese===
「到底出什么事了？」他问。接着才说到他的消息。「有人在跟这报纸过不去，肯定的。」他说。「我不知道到底为啥。这玩意儿我一行都没读过。反正我看不出，谁会跟一张名叫《舒适时光》的报纸过不去。照这几天的情形，它倒像是某座该死矿区营地的机关报，惹得哥们儿嫌弃上了。」

「到底出了什么事？」比利目光灼灼地问。

「嗐，天大的事也算不上，不过就是我们的送报员一出门就挨帮派流氓的揍。帕特·哈里根（Pat Harrigan）这会儿正躺在医院里。我刚去看了他。帕特是个爱打架的主儿。让他挑，他宁可打架也不看球赛。可这回也太过分了。知道怎么回事吗？嗐，你听着，事情是这样式的。帕特推着车出去。穿一条下三滥街上城的时候，被一伙流氓拦住了。他摆开架势打。六个人招呼他一个，其余的把车上的报纸一张不剩地席卷一空。等警察赶到，帕特已经七零八碎躺在地上，四下里只剩一个嚼着口香糖的南欧佬。警察问那南欧佬出了什么事，南欧佬说他刚从拐角那边过来，什么人也没瞧见。我就想知道，这到底是怎么一回事？谁跟我们过不去，图什么？」

===Original===
Mr. Wheeler leaned back in his chair, while Billy, his hair rumpled more than ever and his eyes glowing, explained the situation. Mr. Wheeler listened absolutely unmoved, and, when the narrative had come to an end, gave it as his opinion that the editorial staff had sand. That was his sole comment.

“It’s up to you,” he said, rising. “You know your business. Say, though, someone had better get busy right quick and do something to stop these guys roughhousing like this. If we get a few more carriers beat up the way Pat was, there’ll be a strike. It’s not as if they were all Irishmen. The most of them are Dagoes and such, and they don’t want any more fight than they can get by beating their wives and kicking kids off the sidewalk. I’ll do my best to get this paper distributed right and it’s a shame if it ain’t, because it’s going big just now—but it’s up to you. Good day, gents.”

===Chinese===
惠勒先生往椅背上一靠，听比利——头发比平时更乱、双眼放光——说明情由。惠勒先生听着，纹丝不动，等叙述终了，发表见解：编辑部有胆量。这就是他的全部评语。

「瞧你们的了。」他起身说。「你们懂行。不过我说，最好赶紧有人忙活起来，想法子治治这帮家伙这么胡闹。要是再有几个送报员落得帕特这下场，就要罢工了。好在他们也不是个个都爱尔兰人。多半是南欧佬之流，他们想要的打斗，无非是打老婆、把小孩踢下人行道，再多的就不要了。我会尽力把报纸发行到位——发行不到位就可惜了，眼下正卖得红火——不过，瞧你们的了。回见，各位。」

===Original===
He went out. Psmith looked at Billy.

“As Comrade Wheeler remarks,” he said, “it is up to us. What do you propose to do about it? This is a move of the enemy which I have not anticipated. I had fancied that their operations would be confined exclusively to our two selves. If they are going to strew the street with our carriers, we are somewhat in the soup.”

Billy said nothing. He was chewing the stem of an unlighted pipe. Psmith went on.

===Chinese===
他走了出去。普史密斯看着比利。

「正如惠勒同志所言，」他说，「得瞧我们的了。你打算如何应对？敌人这一手我未曾料到。我原以为他们的行动只会局限于我们两个本人。他们若是打算拿我们的送报员铺满街道，咱们可就陷进汤锅里了。」

比利没说话。他嚼着一支没点着的烟斗的柄。普史密斯继续说下去。

===Original===
“It means, of course, that we must buck up to a certain extent. If the campaign is to be a long one, they have us where the hair is crisp. We cannot stand the strain. *Cosy Moments* cannot be muzzled, but it can undoubtedly be choked. What we want to do is to find out the name of the man behind the tenements as soon as ever we can and publish it; and, then, if we perish, fall yelling the name.”

Billy admitted the soundness of this scheme, but wished to know how it was to be done.

“Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith. “I have been thinking this thing over, and it seems to me that we are on the wrong track, or rather we aren’t on any track at all; we are simply marking time. What we want to do is to go out and hustle round till we stir up something. Our line up to the present has been to sit at home and scream vigorously in the hope of some stout fellow hearing and rushing to help. In other words, we’ve been saying in the paper what an outsize in scugs the merchant must be who owns those tenements, in the hope that somebody else will agree with us and be sufficiently interested to get to work and find out who the blighter is. That’s all wrong. What we must do now, Comrade Windsor, is put on our hats, such hats as Comrade Repetto has left us, and sally forth as sleuthhounds on our own account.”

===Chinese===
「这当然意味着，我们必须在一定程度上振作起来。这场战役若是打成长期的，他们就把我们攥在脆处了。我们熬不住这份消耗。《舒适时光》套不上口套，却无疑可以被掐住喉咙。我们要做的，就是尽早查出廉租公寓背后那个人的名字，公之于众；然后，即便覆灭，也要喊着那个名字倒下。」

比利承认此计甚稳妥，但想知道如何落实。

「温莎同志，」普史密斯说。「我把这事前前后后想了一遍，依我看，我们走错了路子——或者不如说，压根没走上任何路子；我们只是在原地踏步。我们要做的是出门奔走，四处折腾，直到搅出点名堂。迄今为止，我们的路线是坐在家里高声尖叫，指望哪个壮士听见，赶来相助。换句话说，我们一直在报纸上说，拥有那些廉租公寓的那位买卖人，准是个特大号的恶棍，指望旁人附和我们，并且热心到肯动手去查那家伙是谁。这就全错了。眼下我们要做的，温莎同志，是戴上帽子——戴雷佩托同志给我们剩下的那些帽子——以私家猎犬的身份，自行出巡。」

===Original===
“Yes, but how?” demanded Billy. “That’s all right in theory, but how’s it going to work in practice? The only thing that can corner the man is a commission.”

“Far from it, Comrade Windsor. The job may be worked more simply. I don’t know how often the rents are collected in these places, but I should say at a venture once a week. My idea is to hang negligently round till the rent-collector arrives, and when he has loomed up on the horizon, buttonhole him and ask him quite politely, as man to man, whether he is collecting those rents for himself or for somebody else, and if somebody else, who that somebody else is. Simple, I fancy? Yet brainy. Do you take me, Comrade Windsor?”

Billy sat up, excited. “I believe you’ve hit it.”

Psmith shot his cuffs modestly.

===Chinese===
「是，可是怎么干？」比利追问。「理论上是好的，实际操作起来怎么弄？能把这个逼到墙角的，只有专门委员会。」

「远非如此，温莎同志。这活儿可以办得更简单些。那些地方的房租多久收一回，我不清楚，但我姑且猜是每周一次。我的想法是：吊儿郎当地在附近闲逛，直到收租员驾到；等他一在地平线上冒头，就拉住他不放，以男人对男人的方式客客气气地问他：这些租金他是替自己收，还是替别人收；若是替别人，那位别人又是何方神圣。简单吧，我想？然而高明。听懂了吗，温莎同志？」

比利兴奋地坐直了。「我看你说到点子上了。」

普史密斯谦虚地抖了抖衬衫袖口。

## XIX In Pleasant Street / 在普莱森特街
===Original===
Careful inquiries, conducted incognito by Master Maloney among the denizens of Pleasant Street, brought the information that rents in the tenements were collected not weekly but monthly, a fact which must undoubtedly cause a troublesome hitch in the campaign. Rent-day, announced Pugsy, fell on the last day of the month.

“I rubbered around,” he said, “and did de sleut’ act, and I finds t’ings out. Dere’s a feller comes round ’bout supper time dat day, an’ den it’s up to de fam’lies what lives in de tenements to dig down into deir jeans fer de stuff, or out dey goes dat same night.”

“Evidently a hustler, our nameless friend,” said Psmith.

“I got dat from a kid what knows anuder kid what lives dere,” explained Master Maloney. “Say,” he proceeded confidentially, “dat kid’s in bad, sure he is. Dat second kid, de one what lives dere. He’s a wop kid, an—”

===Chinese===
马洛尼少爷微服私访，在普莱森特街（Pleasant Street）的居民中间细细打探，带回情报说：廉租公寓的房租不是按周收，而是按月收——这一事实无疑会给战役添一处麻烦的梗阻。普格西宣称，收租日就落在每月最后一天。

「我四处转悠，耍了把侦探的手段，摸出了些名堂。」他说。「那天晚饭前后会来一个家伙，到时候住在廉租公寓里的各家各户，就得往裤兜深处掏票子凑数，不然当天夜里就卷铺盖滚蛋。」

「咱们这位无名氏朋友，显然是个雷厉风行的人物。」普史密斯说。

「这事是我从一个小孩那儿听来的，那小孩认识另一个住在那儿的小孩。」马洛尼少爷解释道。「我说，」他压低嗓门，神神秘秘地往下讲，「那孩子正倒霉着呢，肯定的。就是第二个小孩，住那儿那个。他是个意大利崽子，还有——」

===Original===
“A what, Comrade Maloney?”

“A wop. A Dago. Why, don’t you get next? Why, an Italian. Sure, dat’s right. Well, dis kid, he is sure to de bad, ’cos his father come over from Italy to work on de Subway.”

“I don’t see why that puts him in bad,” said Billy Windsor wonderingly.

“Nor I,” agreed Psmith. “Your narratives, Comrade Maloney, always seem to me to suffer from a certain lack of construction. You start at the end, and then you go back to any portion of the story which happens to appeal to you at the moment, eventually winding up at the beginning. Why should the fact that this stripling’s father has come over from Italy to work on the Subway be a misfortune?”

“Why, sure, because he got fired an’ went an’ swatted de foreman one on de coco, an’ de magistrate gives him t’oity days.”

“And then, Comrade Maloney? This thing is beginning to get clearer. You are like Sherlock Holmes. After you’ve explained a thing from start to finish—or, as you prefer to do, from finish to start—it becomes quite simple.”

===Chinese===
「一个什么，马洛尼同志？」

「意大利崽子。南欧佬。嗐，你咋还没转过弯来？就是意大利人呗。没错，就这么回事。话说回来，这孩子准是彻底倒了霉，因为他爹从意大利跑来修地铁。」

「我看不出这怎么就害他倒了霉。」比利·温莎纳闷地说。

「我也看不出。」普史密斯附和道。「你那些叙述，马洛尼同志，在我看来总有一种结构欠佳的毛病。你从结尾讲起，然后跳回故事里此刻恰好合你心意的任何一段，最后才在开头落定。这个少年的父亲从意大利过来修地铁，这一事实为什么就算一场灾祸呢？」

「嗐，当然啦，因为他被炒了鱿鱼，气不过跑过去照工头的脑瓜来了那么一下，法官就判了他三十天。」

「后来呢，马洛尼同志？这事开始变得清楚了。你就像夏洛克·福尔摩斯。一件事经你从头到尾解释一遍——或者，照你偏爱的做法，从尾到头——就变得相当简单了。」

===Original===
“Why, den dis kid’s in bad for fair, ’cos der ain’t nobody to pungle de bones—”

“Pungle de what, Comrade Maloney?”

“De bones. De stuff. Dat’s right. De dollars. He’s all alone, dis kid, so when de rent-guy blows in, who’s to slip him over de simoleons? It’ll be outside for his, quick.”

Billy warmed up at this tale of distress in his usual way. “Somebody ought to do something. It’s a vile shame the kid being turned out like that.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，这么着那孩子这回是彻底完了，因为没人给他掏大洋——」

「掏什么，马洛尼同志？」

「大洋。就是票子。没错。美元。这孩子孤零零一个人，等收租的家伙杀进门来，谁来给他递上钱？他立马就得睡大街。」

比利一听这桩惨事，照例热血上涌。「总得有人管管。就这么把孩子扫地出门，真是缺德带冒烟。」

===Original===
“We will see to it, Comrade Windsor. *Cosy Moments* shall step in. We will combine business with pleasure, paying the stripling’s rent and corralling the rent-collector at the same time. What is today? How long before the end of the month? Another week! A murrain on it, Comrade Windsor. Two murrains. This delay may undo us.”

But the days went by without any further movement on the part of the enemy. A strange quiet seemed to be brooding over the other camp. As a matter of fact, the sudden outbreak of active hostilities with the Table Hill contingent had had the effect of taking the minds of Spider Reilly and his warriors off *Cosy Moments* and its affairs, much as the unexpected appearance of a mad bull would make a man forget that he had come out butterfly-hunting. Psmith and Billy could wait; they were not likely to take the offensive; but the Table Hillites demanded instant attention.

War had broken out, as was usual between the gangs, in a somewhat tentative fashion at first sight. There had been sniping and skirmishes by the wayside, but as yet no pitched battle. The two armies were sparring for an opening.

---

===Chinese===
「我们会过问的，温莎同志。《舒适时光》要出手了。咱们公私兼顾：一边替这少年付房租，一边把收租员收入囊中。今天几号？离月底还有多久？还有一个星期！真是遭瘟，温莎同志。遭双重瘟。这拖延可能坏事。」

然而日子一天天过去，敌方再无动作。对方营垒上空似乎笼罩着一种古怪的宁静。事实是，与桌山（Table Hill）帮骤然爆发的正面战火，把蜘蛛莱利（Spider Reilly）和他那班勇士的心思从《舒适时光》及其事务上整个引开了——就好比扑面撞上一头疯牛，会让人忘了自己本是出来捕蝴蝶的。普史密斯和比利等得起；他们不像会主动出击；可桌山帮众却需要立刻应付。

战事既起，帮派之间素来如此，乍一看还颇有几分试探的味道。路旁有过放冷枪和小规模的遭遇，但尚无一场堂堂之阵的对决。两军人马都晃着拳找破绽。

---

===Original===
The end of the week arrived, and Psmith and Billy, conducted by Master Maloney, made their way to Pleasant Street. To get there it was necessary to pass through a section of the enemy’s country; but the perilous passage was safely negotiated. The expedition reached its unsavoury goal intact.

The wop kid, whose name, it appeared, was Giuseppe Orloni, inhabited a small room at the very top of the building next to the one Psmith and Mike had visited on their first appearance in Pleasant Street. He was out when the party, led by Pugsy up dark stairs, arrived; and, on returning, seemed both surprised and alarmed to see visitors. Pugsy undertook to do the honours. Pugsy as interpreter was energetic but not wholly successful. He appeared to have a fixed idea that the Italian language was one easily mastered by the simple method of saying “da” instead of “the,” and tacking on a final “a” to any word that seemed to him to need one.

===Chinese===
周末到了，普史密斯和比利由马洛尼少爷引路，一路去往普莱森特街。要到那里，必须穿过一段敌占区；但这趟险程有惊无险地走完了。远征队毫发无损地抵达了那个气味不佳的目的地。

那个意大利孩子——名字原来叫朱塞佩·奥洛尼（Giuseppe Orloni）——住在小楼的最高层一间小屋里，就在普史密斯和迈克初到普莱森特街时造访过的那栋隔壁。一行人由普格西领着摸黑上楼时，他不在家；等他回来，一见有客，又是吃惊又是慌张。普格西担当起招待宾客的差事。普格西当翻译，干劲十足，成效却不尽如人意。他似乎抱有一个固定的观念：意大利语这门学问，只需把 the 说成 da、再给他看着需要补一个的任何词添上个 a，便可轻松精通。

===Original===
“Say, kid,” he began, “has da rent-a-man come yet-a?”

The black eyes of the wop kid clouded. He gesticulated, and said something in his native language.

“He hasn’t got next,” reported Master Maloney. “He can’t git on to me curves. Dese wop kids is all boneheads. Say, kid, look-a here.” He walked out of the room and closed the door; then, rapping on it smartly from the outside, reentered and, assuming a look of extreme ferocity, stretched out his hand and thundered: “Unbelt-a! Slip-a me da stuff!”

The wop kid’s puzzlement became pathetic.

“This,” said Psmith, deeply interested, “is getting about as tense as anything I ever struck. Don’t give in, Comrade Maloney. Who knows but that you may yet win through? I fancy the trouble is that your too perfect Italian accent is making the youth homesick. Once more to the breach, Comrade Maloney.”

Master Maloney made a gesture of disgust. “I’m t’roo. Dese Dagoes makes me tired. Dey don’t know enough to go upstairs to take de Elevated. Beat it, you mutt,” he observed with moody displeasure to the wop kid, accompanying the words with a gesture which conveyed its own meaning. The wop kid, plainly glad to get away, slipped out of the door like a shadow.

===Chinese===
「我说，小子啊，」他开口道，「收租的人啊，来过没有啊？」

意大利孩子的黑眼睛蒙上了阴云。他比画着，用本国语说了一串话。

「他没听出门道。」马洛尼少爷汇报。「他接不住我的球路。这些意大利崽子全是榆木脑袋。我说，小子，瞧这儿啊。」他走出房门，把门关上；随即在外头干脆利落地敲门，重新进屋，摆出一副凶神恶煞的脸相，伸出手，雷鸣般吼道：「松腰带啊！把东西啊交来啊！」

意大利孩子的困惑变得令人心酸。

「这局面，」普史密斯兴致盎然地说，「紧张得差不多赶上我平生所见之最了。别认输，马洛尼同志。谁知道你不会最终大功告成呢？我看毛病出在：你那过于纯正的意大利腔，勾得这位少年想家了。再冲一次缺口吧，马洛尼同志。」

马洛尼少爷做了个厌恶的手势。「我不干了。这些意大利佬真叫我腻味。他们连上楼去坐高架电车都不懂。滚吧，你这蠢货。」他闷闷不乐地对意大利孩子撂下这句，还配了个不言自明的手势。意大利孩子巴不得脱身，影子似的溜出了门。

===Original===
Pugsy shrugged his shoulders.

“Gents,” he said resignedly, “it’s up to youse.”

“I fancy,” said Psmith, “that this is one of those moments when it is necessary for me to unlimber my Sherlock Holmes system. As thus. If the rent collector *had* been here, it is certain, I think, that Comrade Spaghetti, or whatever you said his name was, wouldn’t have been. That is to say, if the rent collector had called and found no money waiting for him, surely Comrade Spaghetti would have been out in the cold night instead of under his own rooftree. Do you follow me, Comrade Maloney?”

===Chinese===
普格西耸了耸肩。

「各位爷，」他听天由命地说，「瞧你们的了。」

「我看，」普史密斯说，「眼下正是我不得不架起我那套福尔摩斯体系的时刻之一。推理如下。倘若收租员*已经*来过，那么我想，意面同志（Comrade Spaghetti）——不管你方才说他叫什么——就必定不会在这里了。也就是说，如果收租员登了门、发现没有钱等着他，那么意面同志此刻必然在寒夜里挨冻，而不会待在自己的屋檐底下。听明白了吗，马洛尼同志？」

===Original===
“That’s right,” said Billy Windsor. “Of course.”

“Elementary, my dear Watson, elementary,” murmured Psmith.

“So all we have to do is to sit here and wait.”

“All?” said Psmith sadly. “Surely it is enough. For of all the scaly localities I have struck this seems to me the scaliest. The architect of this Stately Home of America seems to have had a positive hatred for windows. His idea of ventilation was to leave a hole in the wall about the size of a lima bean and let the thing go at that. If our friend does not arrive shortly, I shall pull down the roof. Why, gadzooks! Not to mention stap my vitals! Isn’t that a trapdoor up there? Make a long-arm, Comrade Windsor.”

===Chinese===
「说的是。」比利·温莎道。「当然。」

「基本演绎，我亲爱的华生，基本演绎。」普史密斯喃喃道。

「那咱们只需要坐在这儿干等就行了。」

「只是？」普史密斯悲伤地说。「这已经够受的了。在我到过的所有寒碜地方里，此处在我看来寒碜之最。这座美利坚庄严府邸的建筑师，对窗户似乎怀有一种切齿的仇恨。他关于通风的理念，就是在墙上留一个利马豆大小的窟窿，然后就此拉倒。咱们的朋友若再不驾到，我就要把房顶揪下来了。哎呀，天哪！更别提我的老命了！那上头是不是有个活板天窗？伸长胳膊，温莎同志。」

===Original===
Billy got on a chair and pulled the bolt. The trapdoor opened downwards. It fell, disclosing a square of deep blue sky.

“Gum!” he said. “Fancy living in this atmosphere when you don’t have to. Fancy these fellows keeping that shut all the time.”

“I expect it is an acquired taste,” said Psmith, “like Limburger cheese. They don’t begin to appreciate air till it is thick enough to scoop chunks out of with a spoon. Then they get up on their hind legs and inflate their chests and say, ‘This is fine! This beats ozone hollow!’ Leave it open, Comrade Windsor. And now, as to the problem of dispensing with Comrade Maloney’s services?”

===Chinese===
比利踩上椅子，拔开了门闩。活板门向下翻开，掉了下来，露出一方深蓝色的天空。

「嚯！」他说。「明明用不着，却要住在这么一股空气里，真是难以想象。想想吧，这些家伙居然一年到头把这天窗关死。」

「我料想这是一种养出来的口味，」普史密斯说，「就像林堡奶酪（Limburger）一样。空气不浓到能用勺子一块一块舀出来的地步，他们是不会开始欣赏空气的。到那时他们才后腿直立、鼓起胸膛说：『好空气！这可比臭氧强多啦！』让门开着吧，温莎同志。现在，来解决让马洛尼同志卸任的问题？」

===Original===
“Sure,” said Billy. “Beat it, Pugsy, my lad.”

Pugsy looked up, indignant.

“Beat it?” he queried.

“While your shoe leather’s good,” said Billy. “This is no place for a minister’s son. There may be a rough house in here any minute, and you would be in the way.”

===Chinese===
「那是。」比利说。「撤吧，普格西，老弟。」

普格西抬起头，一脸愤慨。

「撤？」他质问道。

「趁你的鞋底还结实。」比利说。「这儿可不是牧师的儿子待的地方。随时可能在这儿上演一场全武行，你在这儿碍手碍脚。」

===Original===
“I want to stop and pipe de fun,” objected Master Maloney.

“Never mind. Cut off. We’ll tell you all about it tomorrow.”

Master Maloney prepared reluctantly to depart. As he did so there was a sound of a well-shod foot on the stairs, and a man in a snuff-coloured suit, wearing a brown Homburg hat and carrying a small notebook in one hand, walked briskly into the room. It was not necessary for Psmith to get his Sherlock Holmes system to work. His whole appearance proclaimed the newcomer to be the long-expected collector of rents.

===Chinese===
「我想留下来瞧瞧热闹。」马洛尼少爷抗议道。

「不行。快走。明天我们把一切讲给你听。」

马洛尼少爷磨磨蹭蹭地准备开拔。就在这时，楼梯上响起一双好皮鞋的脚步声，一个穿鼻烟色西装、头戴棕色洪堡帽、一手拿着小笔记本的男人，脚步轻快地走进屋来。普史密斯这回用不着动用他的福尔摩斯体系了。来人的整副外表都在宣告：此人正是那位久候多时的收租员。

## V Planning Improvements / 筹划改良
===Original===
“By the way,” said Psmith, “what is your exact position on this paper? Practically, we know well, you are its backbone, its lifeblood; but what is your technical position? When your proprietor is congratulating himself on having secured the ideal man for your job, what precise job does he congratulate himself on having secured the ideal man for?”

“I’m subeditor.”

“Merely sub? You deserve a more responsible post than that, Comrade Windsor. Where is your proprietor? I must buttonhole him and point out to him what a wealth of talent he is allowing to waste itself. You must have scope.”

“He’s in Europe. At Carlsbad, or somewhere. He never comes near the paper. He just sits tight and draws the profits. He lets the editor look after things. Just at present I’m acting as editor.”

===Chinese===
「对了，」普史密斯说，「你在这份报纸上的确切职位是什么？实际上嘛，咱们都清楚，你是它的脊梁骨、它的命脉；可你名义上的职位是什么呢？当你的老板为自己觅得填补你这个职位的理想人选而暗自庆幸时，他庆幸觅得理想人选来填补的，究竟是个什么差事？」

「我是副主编。」

「区区副职？你该得到一个比这更吃重的职位，温莎同志。你的老板人在哪里？我必须拉住他好好理论一番，向他指出，他正坐视怎样一笔人才财富白白荒废。你必须有施展的天地。」

「他在欧洲。在卡尔斯巴德，还是别的什么地方。他从来不踏进报社一步。他只管稳坐不动，坐收利润。事情全交给主编打理。眼下就由我代理主编。」

===Original===
“Ah! then at last you have your big chance. You are free, untrammelled.”

“You bet I’m not,” said Billy Windsor. “Guess again. There’s no room for developing free untrammelled ideas on this paper. When you’ve looked at it, you’ll see that each page is run by someone. I’m simply the fellow who minds the shop.”

Psmith clicked his tongue sympathetically. “It is like setting a gifted French chef to wash up dishes,” he said. “A man of your undoubted powers, Comrade Windsor, should have more scope. That is the cry, ‘more scope!’ I must look into this matter. When I gaze at your broad, bulging forehead, when I see the clear light of intelligence in your eyes, and hear the grey matter splashing restlessly about in your cerebellum, I say to myself without hesitation, ‘Comrade Windsor must have more scope.’ ” He looked at Mike, who was turning over the leaves of his copy of *Cosy Moments* in a sort of dull despair. “Well, Comrade Jackson, and what is your verdict?”

Mike looked at Billy Windsor. He wished to be polite, yet he could find nothing polite to say. Billy interpreted the look.

===Chinese===
「啊！那么你终于等来了大好机会。你自由了，无拘无束。」

「你想得美。」比利·温莎说。「再猜猜。这份报纸上根本没有余地让人施展什么自由无拘的点子。等你看过内容就明白了，每一版都各有其主。我不过是个看店的伙计。」

普史密斯同情地咂了咂舌。「这就像叫一位天才的法国大厨去洗盘子。」他说。「以你无可置疑的才干，温莎同志，本该有更广阔的天地。呼声正在于此：『要天地！』这事我必须过问。当我凝望你那宽阔凸出的额头，当我看见你眼中智慧的神采，听见脑灰质在你小脑里不安分地晃荡作响，我便毫不犹豫地对自己说：『温莎同志必须有更广阔的天地。』」他看向迈克——后者正以一种木然的绝望，一页页翻着他手里那册《舒适时光》。「那么，杰克逊同志，你的裁决如何？」

迈克看了看比利·温莎。他本想客气几句，却实在找不出可客气的话。比利读懂了这个眼神。

===Original===
“Go on,” he said. “Say it. It can’t be worse than what I think.”

“I expect some people would like it awfully,” said Mike.

“They must, or they wouldn’t buy it. I’ve never met any of them yet, though.”

Psmith was deep in Luella Granville Waterman’s “Moments in the Nursery.” He turned to Billy Windsor.

===Chinese===
「直说吧。」他说。「尽管说。总不至于比我自己想的更糟。」

「我想有些人会非常喜欢它的。」迈克说。

「肯定有，不然不会有人买。不过我一个都还没碰见过。」

普史密斯正埋头研读卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼的《育婴时光》。他转向比利·温莎。

===Original===
“Luella Granville Waterman,” he said, “is not by any chance your *nom de plume*, Comrade Windsor?”

“Not on your life. Don’t think it.”

“I am glad,” said Psmith courteously. “For, speaking as man to man, I must confess that for sheer, concentrated bilge she gets away with the biscuit with almost insolent ease. Luella Granville Waterman must go.”

“How do you mean?”

“She must go,” repeated Psmith firmly. “Your first act, now that you have swiped the editorial chair, must be to sack her.”

===Chinese===
「卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼，」他说，「该不会就是你的笔名（nom de plume）吧，温莎同志？」

「想都别想。别往那处想。」

「我很欣慰。」普史密斯彬彬有礼地说。「因为，咱们男人对男人说句掏心话，我必须承认：单论纯粹而高度浓缩的废话，她以近乎狂妄的轻巧便独占鳌头。卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼必须走。」

「你这话怎么讲？」

「她必须走。」普史密斯斩钉截铁地重复道。「既然你已抢下主编交椅，你的第一把火就得是辞退她。」

===Original===
“But, say, I can’t. The editor thinks a heap of her stuff.”

“We cannot help his troubles. We must act for the good of the paper. Moreover, you said, I think, that he was away?”

“So he is. But he’ll come back.”

“Sufficient unto the day, Comrade Windsor. I have a suspicion that he will be the first to approve your action. His holiday will have cleared his brain. Make a note of improvement number one—the sacking of Luella Granville Waterman.”

“I guess it’ll be followed pretty quick by improvement number two—the sacking of William Windsor. I can’t go monkeying about with the paper that way.”

===Chinese===
「可是，喂，不行啊。主编对她的稿子宝贝得很。」

「他的烦恼，我们爱莫能助。我们必须为报纸的利益行事。况且，我记得你说过，他眼下不在？」

「是不在。可他总要回来的。」

「一天的难处一天当就够了，温莎同志。我有一种预感：他准会是头一个赞许你此举的人。度假会把他的脑子洗干净。记下改良第一号——辞退卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼。」

「我估计紧接着就得来改良第二号——辞退威廉·温莎。我可不能那样胡乱折腾这份报纸。」

===Original===
Psmith reflected for a moment.

“Has this job of yours any special attractions for you, Comrade Windsor?”

“I guess not.”

“As I suspected. You yearn for scope. What exactly are your ambitions?”

“I want to get a job on one of the big dailies. I don’t see how I’m going to fix it, though, at the present rate.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯沉吟片刻。

「你这份差事，对你有什么特别的吸引力吗，温莎同志？」

「我看没有。」

「果然不出我所料。你渴望天地。你的抱负究竟是什么？」

「我想进一家大日报谋个职位。不过照眼下这个架势，我看不出怎么能成。」

===Original===
Psmith rose, and tapped him earnestly on the chest.

“Comrade Windsor, you have touched the spot. You are wasting the golden hours of your youth. You must move. You must hustle. You must make Windsor of *Cosy Moments* a name to conjure with. You must boost this sheet up till New York rings with your exploits. On the present lines that is impossible. You must strike out a line for yourself. You must show the world that even *Cosy Moments* cannot keep a good man down.”

He resumed his seat.

“How do you mean?” said Billy Windsor.

===Chinese===
普史密斯站起身，郑重其事地敲了敲他的胸口。

「温莎同志，你算是说到点子上了。你正在浪费青春的金色时光。你必须行动。你必须奔忙。你必须让《舒适时光》的温莎成为一个一提就灵的名号。你必须把这张报纸捧起来，捧到整个纽约回响着你的丰功伟绩。照现在这套路线，那是办不到的。你必须自闯一条新路。你必须让世人看看，就连《舒适时光》也压不住一个好汉。」

他重新落座。

「你这话怎么讲？」比利·温莎说。

===Original===
Psmith turned to Mike.

“Comrade Jackson, if you were editing this paper, is there a single feature you would willingly retain?”

“I don’t think there is,” said Mike. “It’s all pretty bad rot.”

“My opinion in a nutshell,” said Psmith, approvingly. “Comrade Jackson,” he explained, turning to Billy, “has a secure reputation on the other side for the keenness and lucidity of his views upon literature. You may safely build upon him. In England when Comrade Jackson says ‘Turn’ we all turn. Now, my views on the matter are as follows. *Cosy Moments*, in my opinion (worthless, were it not backed by such a virtuoso as Comrade Jackson), needs more snap, more go. All these putrid pages must disappear. Letters must be despatched tomorrow morning, informing Luella Granville Waterman and the others (and in particular B. Henderson Asher, who from a cursory glance strikes me as an ideal candidate for a lethal chamber) that, unless they cease their contributions instantly, you will be compelled to place yourself under police protection. After that we can begin to move.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯转向迈克。

「杰克逊同志，假如由你来编这份报纸，可有哪一个栏目是你愿意保留的？」

「我看一个都没有。」迈克说。「全是烂得可以的胡说八道。」

「一言以蔽之，正是我的看法。」普史密斯赞许地说。「杰克逊同志，」他转向比利解释道，「在大洋彼岸素以其文学见解的敏锐与明晰享有稳固的声誉。你大可放心倚重他。在英国，杰克逊同志说『转』，我们全体就转。好了，我对这件事的看法如下。《舒适时光》——这是我的看法（若无杰克逊同志这样一位行家大师背书，本不足道）——需要更多劲头、更多锐气。这些腐臭的版面必须统统消失。明早必须寄出信函，通知卢埃拉·格兰维尔·沃特曼及其他诸位（尤其是 B·亨德森·阿舍，草草一瞥，我便觉得他是进毒气室的理想人选）：若不即刻停笔，你将不得不把自己置于警方的保护之下。此后，我们便可以开始行动了。」

===Original===
Billy Windsor sat and rocked himself in his chair without replying. He was trying to assimilate this idea. So far the grandeur of it had dazed him. It was too spacious, too revolutionary. Could it be done? It would undoubtedly mean the sack when Mr. J. Fillken Wilberfloss returned and found the apple of his eye torn asunder and, so to speak, deprived of its choicest pips. On the other hand … His brow suddenly cleared. After all, what was the sack? One crowded hour of glorious life is worth an age without a name, and he would have no name as long as he clung to his present position. The editor would be away ten weeks. He would have ten weeks in which to try himself out. Hope leaped within him. In ten weeks he could change *Cosy Moments* into a real live paper. He wondered that the idea had not occurred to him before. The trifling fact that the despised journal was the property of Mr. Benjamin White, and that he had no right whatever to tinker with it without that gentleman’s approval, may have occurred to him, but, if it did, it occurred so momentarily that he did not notice it. In these crises one cannot think of everything.

===Chinese===
比利·温莎坐在椅子里前后摇晃着，没有作声。他正努力消化这个念头。迄今为止，它的宏大气魄已把他震得头晕目眩。这格局太宏阔、太革命了。能办成吗？等 J·菲尔肯·威尔伯弗洛斯先生回来，发现自己的心头宝被撕成两半，而且，不妨这么说，连最金贵的果核都被抠了去，那毫无疑问意味着卷铺盖走人。可是换个角度想……他的额头豁然开朗。说到底，卷铺盖算得了什么？光辉人生里充实的一小时，抵得过无名无分的漫长一生；而他只要守着眼下这个位子，就永远无名无分。主编要离开十个星期。他将有十个星期来试试自己的斤两。希望在他胸中腾地跃起。十个星期里，他能把《舒适时光》改造成一份真正鲜活的报纸。他奇怪自己从前竟没想到这一层。至于这本人人看不起的杂志乃本杰明·怀特先生的产业、未经那位先生首肯他无权擅动一个指头这件区区小事，他或许也想到过；但纵然想到，也只是一闪而过，快得他自己都没留意。值此危局，人难能事事想得周全。

===Original===
“I’m on,” he said, briefly.

Psmith smiled approvingly.

“That,” he said, “is the right spirit. You will, I fancy, have little cause to regret your decision. Fortunately, if I may say so, I happen to have a certain amount of leisure just now. It is at your disposal. I have had little experience of journalistic work, but I foresee that I shall be a quick learner. I will become your subeditor, without salary.”

“Bully for you,” said Billy Windsor.

===Chinese===
「我干了。」他言简意赅地说。

普史密斯赞许地笑了。

「这才，」他说，「是正确的态度。我想，你几乎不会有什么缘由后悔这个决定。所幸——恕我自夸一句——我眼下恰好有一段闲暇，可任凭你差遣。新闻这一行我经验寥寥，但我预见自己学起来会很快。我来当你的副主编，不领薪水。」

「好样的。」比利·温莎说。

===Original===
“Comrade Jackson,” continued Psmith, “is unhappily more fettered. The exigencies of his cricket tour will compel him constantly to be gadding about, now to Philadelphia, now to Saskatchewan, anon to Onehorseville, Ga. His services, therefore, cannot be relied upon continuously. From him, accordingly, we shall expect little but moral support. An occasional congratulatory telegram. Now and then a bright smile of approval. The bulk of the work will devolve upon our two selves.”

“Let it devolve,” said Billy Windsor, enthusiastically.

“Assuredly,” said Psmith. “And now to decide upon our main scheme. You, of course, are the editor, and my suggestions are merely suggestions, subject to your approval. But, briefly, my idea is that *Cosy Moments* should become red-hot stuff. I could wish its tone to be such that the public will wonder why we do not print it on asbestos. We must chronicle all the live events of the day, murders, fires, and the like in a manner which will make our readers’ spines thrill. Above all, we must be the guardians of the People’s rights. We must be a searchlight, showing up the dark spot in the souls of those who would endeavour in any way to do the **people** in the eye. We must detect the wrongdoer, and deliver him such a series of resentful buffs that he will abandon his little games and become a model citizen. The details of the campaign we must think out after, but I fancy that, if we follow those main lines, we shall produce a bright, readable little sheet which will in a measure make this city sit up and take notice. Are you with me, Comrade Windsor?”

“Surest thing you know,” said Billy with fervour.

===Chinese===
「杰克逊同志，」普史密斯接着说，「可惜受到的束缚就更多了。他板球巡回赛的各项日程，将逼着他四处奔波，一时费城，一时萨斯喀彻温，转眼又到了佐治亚州的一马镇（Onehorseville, Ga.）。因此，他的效力无法指望长久。相应地，我们对他所求无多，道义支援足矣。偶尔来封贺电。时不时来一个明媚的赞许微笑。工作的重头，将落在我们两个肩上。」

「只管落下来。」比利·温莎热切地说。

「理所当然。」普史密斯说。「现在来定我们的总体方略。你自然是主编，我的建议终归只是建议，全凭你裁夺。不过简而言之，我的想法是：《舒适时光》应当变成红得发烫的东西。我但愿它的调门高到这般田地：公众要纳闷我们为何不用石棉纸来印。当天的种种火爆事件——凶杀、火灾之类——我们必须报道得让读者的脊椎阵阵发麻。首要的是，我们必须做人民权利的守护者。我们必须当一盏探照灯，照出那些企图变着法子蒙骗**人民**之徒灵魂里的黑斑。我们必须揪出作恶者，劈头盖脸给他一连串义愤的重击，打得他从此收起那套小把戏，做一名模范公民。战役的细节容后再议，但我相信，只要循着这几条主线走，我们就能办出一张鲜亮可读的小报，多少要让这座城市坐直了身子、刮目相看。你跟我一路吗，温莎同志？」

「那还用说，板上钉钉。」比利满腔热忱地说。

## Preface / 序
===Original===
The conditions of life in New York are so different from those of London that a story of this kind calls for a little explanation. There are several million inhabitants of New York. Not all of them eke out a precarious livelihood by murdering one another, but there is a definite section of the population which murders—not casually, on the spur of the moment, but on definitely commercial lines at so many dollars per murder. The “gangs” of New York exist in fact. I have not invented them. Most of the incidents in this story are based on actual happenings. The Rosenthal case, where four men, headed by a genial individual calling himself “Gyp the Blood” shot a fellow-citizen in cold blood in a spot as public and fashionable as Piccadilly Circus and escaped in a motorcar, made such a stir a few years ago that the noise of it was heard all over the world and not, as is generally the case with the doings of the gangs, in New York only. Rosenthal cases on a smaller and less sensational scale are frequent occurrences on Manhattan Island. It was the prominence of the victim rather than the unusual nature of the occurrence that excited the New York press. Most gang victims get a quarter of a column in small type.

P. G. Wodehouse
New York, 1915

===Chinese===
纽约的生活境况与伦敦相差悬殊，这类故事因而需要稍作说明。纽约居民有数百万之众。并非人人都要靠自相残杀来勉强糊口，但确有那么一部分人是以杀人为业的——并非一时兴起、偶然为之，而是彻头彻尾按商业路数经营，明码标价，杀一人收若干美元。纽约的「黑帮」（gangs）实有其事，并非我杜撰。本故事中的多数情节都取材于真实事件。数年前的罗森塔尔案（Rosenthal case）便是一例：四名男子，由一位自称「吸血鬼吉普」（Gyp the Blood）的和善人士领头，在一处像皮卡迪利广场一样公开而时髦的地段冷血地枪杀了一位同城公民，随后乘汽车扬长而去。此案当年闹得沸沸扬扬，声浪传遍了全世界，而不像黑帮行径通常那样，只在纽约本地有所耳闻。规模较小、轰动性较低的罗森塔尔案，在曼哈顿岛上实属屡见不鲜。真正让纽约报界群情激愤的，是受害者的显赫身份，而非事件本身有何离奇。大多数黑帮刀下的冤魂，只能在报纸上换得小字号排版的四分之一栏。

P. G. 伍德豪斯
纽约，1915 年

## XXVII Psmith Concludes His Ride / 普史密斯到站下车
===Original===
The Kid, as he had stated to Psmith at their last interview that he intended to do, had begun his training for his match with Eddie Wood, at White Plains, a village distant but a few miles from New York. It was his practice to open a course of training with a little gentle road-work; and it was while jogging along the highway a couple of miles from his training camp, in company with the two thick-necked gentlemen who acted as his sparring partners, that he had come upon the broken-down taxicab.

If this had happened after his training had begun in real earnest, he would have averted his eyes from the spectacle, however alluring, and continued on his way without a pause. But now, as he had not yet settled down to genuine hard work, he felt justified in turning aside and looking into the matter. The fact that the chauffeur, who seemed to be a taciturn man, lacking the conversational graces, manifestly objected to an audience, deterred him not at all. One cannot have everything in this world, and the Kid and his attendant thick-necks were content to watch the process of mending the tyre, without demanding the additional joy of sparkling small talk from the man in charge of the operations.

===Chinese===
小子果然如上次会见普史密斯时所说，已在怀特普莱恩斯——一个离纽约不过几英里的村子——开始了对埃迪·伍德一战的集训。他的习惯是，每期集训先来一点温和的公路跑；正是在离集训营几英里的公路上慢慢颠着、身边跟着两位充任陪练的粗脖子绅士的时候，他撞见了那辆抛锚的出租车。

这事若是发生在训练真正上强度之后，无论场面多么诱人，他都会移开目光，脚下不停，径直赶路。可眼下他还没沉下心进入真正的苦练，便自觉有理由拐到一边看个究竟。那位司机看来是个闷嘴葫芦，欠缺谈吐的风致，分明不欢迎观众——这也丝毫吓不住他。人世间难求万全，小子和他随行的两位粗脖子也就满足于观摩补胎的工序，并不强求掌勺人再添一段妙语连珠的闲谈之乐。

===Original===
“Guy’s had a breakdown, sure,” said the first of the thick-necks.

“Surest thing you know,” agreed his colleague.

“Seems to me the tyre’s punctured,” said the Kid.

All three concentrated their gaze on the machine.

“Kid’s right,” said thick-neck number one. “Guy’s been an’ bust a tyre.”

“Surest thing you know,” said thick-neck number two.

===Chinese===
「这家伙抛锚了，肯定的。」第一位粗脖子说。

「那还用说。」他的同事附和道。

「我看是轮胎扎破了。」小子说。

三双眼睛齐刷刷盯住了那台机器。

「小子说得对。」一号粗脖子说。「这家伙爆胎了。」

「那还用说。」二号粗脖子说。

===Original===
They observed the perspiring chauffeur in silence for a while.

“Wonder how he did that, now?” speculated the Kid.

“Guy ran over a nail, I guess,” said thick-neck number one.

“Surest thing you know,” said the other, who, while perhaps somewhat lacking in the matter of original thought, was a most useful fellow to have by one. A sort of Boswell.

===Chinese===
三人默默观察了那位汗涔涔的司机一会儿。

「这是怎么弄的，嗯？」小子寻思。

「压着钉子了吧，我猜。」一号粗脖子说。

「那还用说。」另一位说——此君在原创思维方面或许略有欠缺，带在身边却是个顶顶有用的人。一位鲍斯韦尔（Boswell）式的人物。

===Original===
“Did you run over a nail?” the Kid inquired of the chauffeur.

The chauffeur ignored the question.

“This is his busy day,” said the first thick-neck with satire. “Guy’s too full of work to talk to us.”

“Deaf, shouldn’t wonder,” surmised the Kid.

“Say, wonder what he’s doin’ with a taxi so far out of the city.”

“Some guy tells him to drive him out here, I guess. Say, it’ll cost him something, too. He’ll have to strip off a few from his roll to pay for this.”

===Chinese===
「你压着钉子了？」小子向司机打听。

司机不理这个问题。

「他今天忙。」一号粗脖子挖苦道。「家伙忙得没工夫搭理咱们。」

「聋子也说不定。」小子猜测。

「我说，他大老远把出租车开到城外这么远干吗？」

「我猜是有哪个家伙叫他开车送出来的。我说，这可够他破费的。他得从那卷钞票里剥下几张来付账。」

===Original===
Psmith, in the interior of the cab, glanced at Mr. Parker.

“You heard, Comrade Parker? He is right, I fancy. The bill—”

Mr. Parker dug viciously at him with the revolver.

“Keep quiet,” he whispered, “or you’ll get hurt.”

Psmith suspended his remarks.

===Chinese===
车厢里的普史密斯瞥了帕克先生一眼。

「你听见了，帕克同志？我想他说得对。这笔账单——」

帕克先生用左轮手枪狠狠捅了他一下。

「闭嘴。」他低声说，「不然有你苦头吃。」

普史密斯暂停了发言。

===Original===
Outside, the conversation had begun again.

“Pretty rich guy inside,” said the Kid, following up his companion’s train of thought. “I’m goin’ to rubber in at the window.”

Psmith, meeting Mr. Parker’s eye, smiled pleasantly. There was no answering smile on the other’s face.

===Chinese===
外头，谈话又开始了。

「车里那位是个有钱的主儿。」小子顺着同伴的思路往下说。「我要凑到车窗上去瞅瞅。」

普史密斯迎上帕克先生的目光，愉快地微微一笑。对方脸上没有回应的笑意。

===Original===
There came the sound of the Kid’s feet grating on the road as he turned; and as he heard it Mr. Parker, that eminent tactician, for the first time lost his head. With a vague idea of screening Psmith from the eyes of the man in the road he half rose. For an instant the muzzle of the pistol ceased to point at Psmith’s waistcoat. It was the very chance Psmith had been waiting for. His left hand shot out, grasped the other’s wrist, and gave it a sharp wrench. The revolver went off with a deafening report, the bullet passing through the back of the cab; then fell to the floor, as the fingers lost their hold. The next moment Psmith’s right fist, darting upwards, took Mr. Parker neatly under the angle of the jaw.

The effect was instantaneous. Psmith had risen from his seat as he delivered the blow, and it consequently got the full benefit of his weight, which was not small. Mr. Parker literally crumpled up. His head jerked back, then fell limply on his chest. He would have slipped to the floor had not Psmith pushed him on to the seat.

===Chinese===
路上传来小子转身时鞋底蹭地的声音；一听见这声音，帕克先生——那位杰出的战术家——平生第一次方寸大乱。他朦朦胧胧想着把普史密斯挡在路上那人的视线之外，半站了起来。有那么一瞬，枪口不再指着普史密斯的马甲。这正是普史密斯候了半天的良机。他左手闪电般探出，扣住对方手腕，猛地一拧。左轮手枪轰的一声震耳巨响开了火，子弹穿透了车厢后壁；随即手指一松，枪掉在了地板上。说时迟那时快，普史密斯右拳向上疾挑，干净利落地兜中帕克先生的下颌角。

效果立竿见影。普史密斯出拳时已从座位上站起，这一拳因此尽享他全身体重之助——那分量可不小。帕克先生整个人瘫软了下去。他的脑袋向后一仰，随即无力地垂到胸前。若非普史密斯把他推回座位，他早就出溜到地板上了。

===Original===
The interested face of the Kid appeared at the window. Behind him could be seen portions of the faces of the two thick-necks.

“Ah, Comrade Brady!” said Psmith genially. “I heard your voice, and was hoping you might look in for a chat.”

“What’s doin’, Mr. Smith?” queried the excited Kid.

“Much, Comrade Brady, much. I will tell you all anon. Meanwhile, however, kindly knock that chauffeur down and sit on his head. He’s a bad person.”

“De guy’s beat it,” volunteered the first thick-neck.

“Surest thing you know,” said the other.

===Chinese===
小子那张好奇的脸出现在车窗口。他身后还露出两位粗脖子的部分脸面。

「啊，布雷迪同志！」普史密斯和颜悦色地说。「我听见你的声音了，正盼着你来串门聊聊天呢。」

「出什么事了，史密斯先生？」激动的小子问。

「事情多了，布雷迪同志，多了去了。我回头一五一十告诉你。不过眼下，劳驾把那个司机放倒，坐到他脑袋上。他是个坏人。」

「那家伙已经跑了。」一号粗脖子主动报告。

「那还用说。」另一位说。

===Original===
“What’s been doin’, Mr. Smith?” asked the Kid.

“I’ll tell you about it as we go, Comrade Brady,” said Psmith, stepping into the road. “Riding in a taxi is pleasant provided it is not overdone. For the moment I have had sufficient. A bit of walking will do me good.”

“What are you going to do with this guy, Mr. Smith?” asked the Kid, pointing to Parker, who had begun to stir slightly.

Psmith inspected the stricken one gravely.

“I have no use for him, Comrade Brady,” he said. “Our ride together gave me as much of his society as I desire for today. Unless you or either of your friends are collecting Parkers, I propose that we leave him where he is. We may as well take the gun, however. In my opinion, Comrade Parker is not the proper man to have such a weapon. He is too prone to go firing it off in any direction at a moment’s notice, causing inconvenience to all.” He groped on the floor of the cab for the revolver. “Now, Comrade Brady,” he said, straightening himself up, “I am at your disposal. Shall we be pushing on?”

---

===Chinese===
「到底出什么事了，史密斯先生？」小子问。

「边走边跟你讲，布雷迪同志。」普史密斯说着，跨到路上。「坐出租车是桩乐事，只要不过量。眼下我已坐够了。走一走对我有好处。」

「这家伙你打算怎么处置，史密斯先生？」小子指了指帕克问，那人已开始微微动弹。

普史密斯郑重地检视了这位倒下的人。

「我留着没用，布雷迪同志。」他说。「今天同车一游，他的陪伴已经够我消受了。除非你或你哪位朋友正收藏帕克，我建议就把他原地留下。不过枪不妨带走。依我看，帕克同志不是持有这类武器的合适人选。他太爱说火就火、朝四面八方乱放，给大伙添乱。」他俯身在车厢地板上摸索那把左轮手枪。「好了，布雷迪同志，」他直起身来说，「我听凭你安排。咱们这就上路？」

---

===Original===
It was late in the evening when Psmith returned to the metropolis, after a pleasant afternoon at the Brady training camp. The Kid, having heard the details of the ride, offered once more to abandon his match with Eddie Wood, but Psmith would not hear of it. He was fairly satisfied that the opposition had fired their last shot, and that their next move would be to endeavour to come to terms. They could not hope to catch him off his guard a second time, and, as far as hired assault and battery were concerned, he was as safe in New York, now that Bat Jarvis had declared himself on his side, as he would have been in the middle of a desert. What Bat said was law on the East Side. No hooligan, however eager to make money, would dare to act against a protégé of the Groome Street leader.

The only flaw in Psmith’s contentment was the absence of Billy Windsor. On this night of all nights the editorial staff of *Cosy Moments* should have been together to celebrate the successful outcome of their campaign. Psmith dined alone, his enjoyment of the rather special dinner which he felt justified in ordering in honour of the occasion somewhat diminished by the thought of Billy’s hard case. He had seen Mr. William Collier in *The Man from Mexico*, and that had given him an understanding of what a term of imprisonment on Blackwell’s Island meant. Billy, during these lean days, must be supporting life on bread, bean soup, and water. Psmith, toying with the *hors d’oeuvre*, was somewhat saddened by the thought.

---

===Chinese===
在布雷迪集训营愉快地过了一下午之后，普史密斯回到大都会，已是入夜时分。小子听罢这趟车程的详情，再次提出放弃与埃迪·伍德的比赛，普史密斯却听都不要听。他基本笃定，对方已经打完了最后一发子弹，下一步必是设法讲和。他们休想再抓住他一次措手不及；而就花钱雇人动手行凶这一项而言，既然巴特·贾维斯已宣布站在他这边，他在纽约便安全得如同身在沙漠正中央。在下城东区，巴特的话就是法律。再急着捞钱的流氓，也不敢对格鲁姆街龙头的受保护人下手。

普史密斯这份惬意中唯一的瑕疵，是比利·温莎不在身边。偏偏在今晚这样的夜里，《舒适时光》的编辑部本该齐聚一堂，庆祝战役的圆满收官。普史密斯独自用晚餐，为纪念这个日子，他自觉有理由点一桌相当丰盛的饭菜，可一想到比利的苦境，那份口福便打了几分折扣。他看过威廉·科利尔（William Collier）先生主演的《墨西哥来客》（The Man from Mexico），由此明白了在布莱克韦尔岛（Blackwell's Island）服一期苦役意味着什么。这段清苦日子里，比利准是靠面包、豆汤和白水吊着命。普史密斯拨弄着开胃小菜（hors d'oeuvre），想到这里，不由得黯然了几分。

---

===Original===
All was quiet at the office on the following day. Bat Jarvis, again accompanied by the faithful Otto, took up his position in the inner room, prepared to repel all invaders; but none arrived. No sounds broke the peace of the outer office except the whistling of Master Maloney.

Things were almost dull when the telephone bell rang. Psmith took down the receiver.

“Hullo?” he said.

“I’m Parker,” said a moody voice.

Psmith uttered a cry of welcome.

===Chinese===
第二天，报社里一切太平。巴特·贾维斯仍由忠实的奥托陪同，在里间占好阵地，准备击退一切来犯之敌；结果一个也没来。外间办公室的安宁，只被马洛尼少爷的口哨声打破过。

事情几乎乏味起来，这时电话铃响了。普史密斯摘下听筒。

「喂？」他说。

「我是帕克。」一个闷闷的声音说。

普史密斯发出一声欢迎的欢呼。

===Original===
“Why, Comrade Parker, this is splendid! How goes it? Did you get back all right yesterday? I was sorry to have to tear myself away, but I had other engagements. But why use the telephone? Why not come here in person? You know how welcome you are. Hire a taxicab and come right round.”

Mr. Parker made no reply to the invitation.

“Mr. Waring would like to see you.”

“Who, Comrade Parker?”

“Mr. Stewart Waring.”

“The celebrated tenement house-owner?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，帕克同志，太好了！近来可好？昨天你平安回去了吗？抱歉不得不忍痛先走一步，我另有约会。可干吗用电话？何不亲自来一趟？你在这儿多受欢迎，你是知道的。雇辆出租车，这就过来吧。」

帕克先生对这个邀请未作回应。

「沃林先生想见你。」

「哪位，帕克同志？」

「斯图尔特·沃林先生。」

「那位大名鼎鼎的廉租公寓业主？」

===Original===
Silence from the other end of the wire. “Well,” said Psmith, “what step does he propose to take towards it?”

“He tells me to say that he will be in his office at twelve o’clock tomorrow morning. His office is in the Morton Building, Nassau Street.”

Psmith clicked his tongue regretfully.

“Then I do not see how we can meet,” he said. “I shall be here.”

“He wishes to see you at his office.”

“I am sorry, Comrade Parker. It is impossible. I am very busy just now, as you may know, preparing the next number, the one in which we publish the name of the owner of the Pleasant Street Tenements. Otherwise, I should be delighted. Perhaps later, when the rush of work has diminished somewhat.”

===Chinese===
电话那头一阵沉默。「哦，」普史密斯说，「他打算为此采取什么步骤呢？」

「他要我转告：明天上午十二点，他在自己办公室。办公室在纳索街（Nassau Street）摩顿大厦（Morton Building）。」

普史密斯遗憾地咂了咂舌。

「那我看不出咱们怎么能见面。」他说。「我到时候在这儿。」

「他希望你到他的办公室去见他。」

「很遗憾，帕克同志。不可能。我眼下很忙，这你是知道的，正筹备下一期刊物——就是我们要公布普莱森特街廉租公寓业主名字的那一期。不然的话，我乐意之至。也许过些时候，等这阵忙乱稍稍平息。」

===Original===
“Am I to tell Mr. Waring that you refuse?”

“If you are seeing him at any time and feel at a loss for something to say, perhaps you might mention it. Is there anything else I can do for you, Comrade Parker?”

“See here—”

“Nothing? Then goodbye. Look in when you’re this way.”

He hung up the receiver.

As he did so, he was aware of Master Maloney standing beside the table.

===Chinese===
「要我告诉沃林先生你拒绝吗？」

「你要是哪天见着他、正愁没话找话，不妨提上一句。还有什么别的可以为你效劳的吗，帕克同志？」

「你听我说——」

「没有？那就再会。顺路时来坐坐。」

他挂上了听筒。

就在这时，他发觉马洛尼少爷站在桌旁。

===Original===
“Yes, Comrade Maloney?”

“Telegram,” said Pugsy. “For Mr. Windsor.”

Psmith ripped open the envelope.

The message ran:

“Returning today. Will be at office tomorrow morning,” and it was signed “Wilberfloss.”

“See who’s here!” said Psmith softly.

===Chinese===
「什么事，马洛尼同志？」

「电报。」普格西说。「给温莎先生的。」

普史密斯撕开信封。

电文如下：

「今日返回。明晨到报社。」落款是「威尔伯弗洛斯」。

「瞧瞧这是谁来了！」普史密斯轻声说。

## XXIII Reductions in the Staff / 编辑部裁员
===Original===
The first member of the staff of *Cosy Moments* to arrive at the office on the following morning was Master Maloney. This sounds like the beginning of a “Plod and Punctuality,” or “How Great Fortunes have been Made” story; but, as a matter of fact, Master Maloney was no early bird. Larks who rose in his neighbourhood, rose alone. He did not get up with them. He was supposed to be at the office at nine o’clock. It was a point of honour with him, a sort of daily declaration of independence, never to put in an appearance before nine-thirty. On this particular morning he was punctual to the minute, or half an hour late, whichever way you choose to look at it.

He had only whistled a few bars of “My Little Irish Rose,” and had barely got into the first page of his story of life on the prairie when Kid Brady appeared. The Kid, as was his habit when not in training, was smoking a big black cigar. Master Maloney eyed him admiringly. The Kid, unknown to that gentleman himself, was Pugsy’s ideal. He came from the Plains; and had, indeed, once actually been a cowboy; he was a coming champion; and he could smoke black cigars. It was, therefore, without his usual well-what-is-it-now? air that Pugsy laid down his book, and prepared to converse.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，《舒适时光》编辑部头一个到达报社的成员，是马洛尼少爷。这话听着像某篇《勤勉与守时》或《万贯家财是如何炼成的》故事的开头；可事实上，马洛尼少爷绝非早起的鸟儿。他那一带的云雀要起，都是独自个儿起。他不跟它们同起。按规矩，他该九点到报社。对他而言，九点半之前绝不着面，是一桩荣誉攸关的事，一种每日发表一遍的独立宣言。而今天这个早上，他准点到岗，分秒不差——或者说迟到了半个钟头，全看您乐意怎么看了。

他刚用口哨吹了几小节《我小小的爱尔兰玫瑰》（My Little Irish Rose），那本讲草原生活的小说才翻了头一页，小子布雷迪就来了。小子有个习惯，不在集训期时，嘴里总叼着一支粗黑雪茄。马洛尼少爷钦慕地打量着他。小子本人并不知道，他正是普格西心中的偶像。他来自西部平原；实实在在当过牛仔；他是未来的冠军；他还抽得动黑雪茄。于是，普格西放下书准备攀谈时，一反平日那副「这回又怎么了？」的做派。

===Original===
“Say, Mr. Smith or Mr. Windsor about, Pugsy?” asked the Kid.
