“Naw, Mr. Brady, they ain’t came yet,” replied Master Maloney respectfully.

“Late, ain’t they?”

“Sure. Mr. Windsor generally blows in before I do.”

“Wonder what’s keepin’ them.”

“P’raps, dey’ve bin put out of business,” suggested Pugsy nonchalantly.

===Chinese===
「我说，普格西，史密斯先生或者温莎先生在吗？」小子问。

「还没呢，布雷迪先生，他们还没到。」马洛尼少爷恭恭敬敬地回答。

「够晚的，是吧？」

「可不。温莎先生平时比我到得还早。」

「不知是什么事绊住他们了。」

「没准儿，被人做了。」普格西满不在乎地猜。

===Original===
“How’s that?”

Pugsy related the events of the previous day, relaxing something of his austere calm as he did so. When he came to the part where the Table Hill allies swooped down on the unsuspecting Three Pointers, he was almost animated.

“Say,” said the Kid approvingly, “that Smith guy’s got more grey matter under his thatch than you’d think to look at him. I—”

“Comrade Brady,” said a voice in the doorway, “you do me proud.”

“Why, say,” said the Kid, turning, “I guess the laugh’s on me. I didn’t see you, Mr. Smith. Pugsy’s been tellin’ me how you sent him for the Table Hills yesterday. That was cute. It was mighty smart. But say, those guys are goin’ some, ain’t they now! Seems as if they was dead set on puttin’ you out of business.”

“Their manner yesterday, Comrade Brady, certainly suggested the presence of some sketchy outline of such an ideal in their minds. One Sam, in particular, an ebony-hued sportsman, threw himself into the task with great vim. I rather fancy he is waiting for us with his revolver to this moment. But why worry? Here we are, safe and sound, and Comrade Windsor may be expected to arrive at any moment. I see, Comrade Brady, that you have been matched against one Eddie Wood.”

===Chinese===
「这话怎么说？」

普格西讲了昨天的种种，讲的时候，他那份冷峻的平静松动了些许。讲到桌山帮盟军扑向毫无防备的三点帮众那一段时，他简直算得上眉飞色舞。

「我说，」小子赞许道，「那个姓史密斯的家伙，帽子底下垫的脑灰质，比看他的长相能猜出来的多。我——」

「布雷迪同志，」门口一个声音说，「你叫我脸上有光。」

「哎呀，我说，」小子转过身来，「这回笑话闹到我头上了。我没瞧见您，史密斯先生。普格西正跟我说呢，您昨天怎么派他去搬桌山帮。真机灵。漂亮极了。可是我说，那帮家伙来势够猛的，是不是！看着是铁了心要把您整出局啊。」

「他们昨天的做派，布雷迪同志，确实显示出他们脑中已有这一理想的粗略草图。其中一位山姆，一位乌木肤色的运动家，投入任务时尤其卖力。我倒觉得，他此刻还端着左轮枪在恭候我们。可愁什么呢？你我俱在，安然无恙，温莎同志也可望随时驾到。我还看到，布雷迪同志，你已与一位埃迪·伍德配对成赛了。」

===Original===
“It’s about that I wanted to see you, Mr. Smith. Say, now that things have been and brushed up so, what with these gang guys layin’ for you the way they’re doin’, I guess you’ll be needin’ me around here. Isn’t that right? Say the word and I’ll call off this Eddie Wood fight.”

“Comrade Brady,” said Psmith with some enthusiasm, “I call that a sporting offer. I’m very much obliged. But we mustn’t stand in your way. If you eliminate this Comrade Wood, they will have to give you a chance against Jimmy Garvin, won’t they?”

“I guess that’s right, sir,” said the Kid. “Eddie stayed nineteen rounds against Jimmy, and if I can put him away, it gets me into line with Jimmy, and he can’t sidestep me.”

“Then go in and win, Comrade Brady. We shall miss you. It will be as if a ray of sunshine had been removed from the office. But you mustn’t throw a chance away. We shall be all right, I think.”

===Chinese===
「我正是为这事来找您的，史密斯先生。我说，如今事情已经闹到这份上，那帮黑帮家伙又这么天天埋伏您，我看您这儿少不了需要我。对不对？您一句话，我就把这场埃迪·伍德的比赛取消。」

「布雷迪同志，」普史密斯颇含热忱地说，「我称这是体育家风度的提议。我不胜感激。但我们不能挡你的路。你若淘汰掉这位伍德同志，他们就得给你对阵吉米·加文的机会了，对不对？」

「我想是这么回事，先生。」小子说。「埃迪在吉米手下撑了十九个回合，我要能把他撂倒，我就排到吉米跟前了，他就没法再闪躲我。」

「那就去赢回来，布雷迪同志。我们会想你的。你一走，就像办公室里被抽走了一缕阳光。但机会是万万丢不得的。我们这边，我想，不会有事。」

===Original===
“I’ll train at White Plains,” said the Kid. “That ain’t far from here, so I’ll be pretty near in case I’m wanted. Hullo, who’s here?”

He pointed to the door. A small boy was standing there, holding a note.

“Mr. Smith?”

“Sir to you,” said Psmith courteously.

“P. Smith?”

“The same. This is your lucky day.”

“Cop at Jefferson Market give me dis to take to youse.”

===Chinese===
「我去怀特普莱恩斯集训。」小子说。「离这儿不远，要用到我时，我随叫随到。咦，谁来了？」

他指向门口。一个小男孩站在那里，手里拿着一张字条。

「史密斯先生？」

「得叫先生。」普史密斯客气地说。

「P·史密斯（P. Smith）？」

「正是鄙人。今天是你的幸运日。」

「杰斐逊市场（Jefferson Market）的一个条子让我把这个送给您。」

===Original===
“A cop in Jefferson Market?” repeated Psmith. “I did not know I had friends among the constabulary there. Why, it’s from Comrade Windsor.” He opened the envelope and read the letter. “Thanks,” he said, giving the boy a quarter-dollar.

It was apparent the Kid was politely endeavouring to veil his curiosity. Master Maloney had no such scruples.

“What’s in de letter, boss?” he inquired.

“The letter, Comrade Maloney, is from our Mr. Windsor, and relates in terse language the following facts, that our editor last night hit a policeman in the eye, and that he was sentenced this morning to thirty days on Blackwell’s Island.”

===Chinese===
「杰斐逊市场的一个条子？」普史密斯重复道。「我不知自己在那边的警界还有朋友。咦，是温莎同志的信。」他拆开信封读了信。「多谢。」他说，赏了男孩一枚两毛五的硬币。

小子显然在礼貌地掩饰自己的好奇。马洛尼少爷可没这份顾忌。

「信上写的啥，老板？」他打听。

「这封信，马洛尼同志，出自我们那位温莎先生手笔，以简练的措辞陈述了如下事实：本报主编昨夜打中一名警察的眼睛，并于今晨被判在布莱克韦尔岛监禁三十天。」

===Original===
“He’s de guy!” admitted Master Maloney approvingly.

“What’s that?” said the Kid. “Mr. Windsor bin punchin’ cops! What’s he bin doin’ that for?”

“He gives no clue. I must go and find out. Could you help Comrade Maloney mind the shop for a few moments while I push round to Jefferson Market and make inquiries?”

“Sure. But say, fancy Mr. Windsor cuttin’ loose that way!” said the Kid admiringly.

===Chinese===
「真有种！」马洛尼少爷心悦诚服。

「你说什么？」小子说。「温莎先生揍条子了！他干嘛揍啊？」

「他没有给出线索。我得去查明。可否请你帮马洛尼同志照看一会儿铺子，我去杰斐逊市场打探打探？」

「没问题。可是我说，真想不到温莎先生也有放开了干的时候！」小子叹服地说。

===Original===
The Jefferson Market Police Court is a little way down town, near Washington Square. It did not take Psmith long to reach it, and by the judicious expenditure of a few dollars he was enabled to obtain an interview with Billy in a back room.

The chief editor of *Cosy Moments* was seated on a bench, looking upon the world through a pair of much blackened eyes. His general appearance was dishevelled. He had the air of a man who has been caught in the machinery.

“Hullo, Smith,” he said. “You got my note all right then?”

Psmith looked at him, concerned.

===Chinese===
杰斐逊市场治安法庭在下城不远，挨着华盛顿广场（Washington Square）。普史密斯没花多少工夫就到了，又审慎地破费了几块美元，获准在后屋里同比利见上了一面。

《舒适时光》的主编坐在一条长凳上，透过两只乌青乌青的眼睛看世界。他整个人形容狼藉，一副被卷进机器里绞过的人的模样。

「喂，史密斯。」他说。「我的字条收到了吧？」

普史密斯关切地看着他。

===Original===
“Comrade Windsor,” he said, “what on earth has been happening to you?”

“Oh, that’s all right,” said Billy. “That’s nothing.”

“Nothing! You look as if you had been run over by a motorcar.”

“The cops did that,” said Billy, without any apparent resentment. “They always turn nasty if you put up a fight. I was a fool to do it, I suppose, but I got so mad. They knew perfectly well that I had nothing to do with any poolroom downstairs.”

===Chinese===
「温莎同志，」他说，「你到底出了什么事？」

「嗐，没事。」比利说。「这点伤不算什么。」

「不算什么！你这模样像是被汽车碾过一遍。」

「这是条子们干的。」比利说，听不出丝毫怨气。「你要是敢还手，他们向来就翻脸。我那么干是傻，这个我认，可我当时气疯了。他们明明一清二楚，我跟楼下那个赌池店半点关系都没有。」

===Original===
Psmith’s eyeglass dropped from his eye.

“Poolroom, Comrade Windsor?”

“Yes. The house where I live was raided late last night. It seems that some gamblers have been running a poolroom on the ground floor. Why the cops should have thought I had anything to do with it, when I was sleeping peacefully upstairs, is more than I can understand. Anyway, at about three in the morning there was the dickens of a banging at my door. I got up to see what was doing, and found a couple of Policemen there. They told me to come along with them to the station. I asked what on earth for. I might have known it was no use arguing with a New York cop. They said they had been tipped off that there was a poolroom being run in the house, and that they were cleaning up the house, and if I wanted to say anything I’d better say it to the magistrate. I said, all right, I’d put on some clothes and come with them. They said they couldn’t wait about while I put on clothes. I said I wasn’t going to travel about New York in pyjamas, and started to get into my shirt. One of them gave me a shove in the ribs with his nightstick, and told me to come along quick. And that made me so mad I hit out.” A chuckle escaped Billy. “He wasn’t expecting it, and I got him fair. He went down over the bookcase. The other cop took a swipe at me with his club, but by that time I was so mad I’d have taken on Jim Jeffries, if he had shown up and got in my way. I just sailed in, and was beginning to make the man think that he had stumbled on Stanley Ketchel or Kid Brady or a dynamite explosion by mistake, when the other fellow loosed himself from the bookcase, and they started in on me together, and there was a general rough house, in the middle of which somebody seemed to let off about fifty thousand dollars’ worth of fireworks all in a bunch; and I didn’t remember anything more till I found myself in a cell, pretty nearly knocked to pieces. That’s my little life-history. I guess I was a fool to cut loose that way, but I was so mad I didn’t stop to think.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯的单片眼镜从眼眶跌了下来。

「赌池店，温莎同志？」

「是啊。我住的那栋楼昨天深夜被查抄了。看样子是有几个赌棍在一楼开了一家赌池店。我在楼上睡得安安稳稳，条子们凭什么认定我跟这事有牵连，我实在想不明白。反正，凌晨三点来钟，我家门上响起一阵要命的擂门声。我起身看怎么回事，门口站着俩警察。他们叫我跟他们走一趟，去警局。我问到底为的什么。我早该知道，跟纽约条子讲道理是白费口舌。他们说接到线报，楼里开着赌池店，他们要把这楼清理干净，我要有话，最好留着急事官说。我说行，我穿上衣服就跟你们走。他们说没工夫等我把衣服穿上。我说我可不打算穿着睡衣满纽约跑，随即开始套衬衫。其中一个用警棍捅了捅我的肋骨，叫我快走。这下可把我惹毛了，我一拳挥了出去。」比利漏出一声轻笑。「他没防备，叫我结结实实捞个正着。他摔过书架倒了下去。另一个条子抡起警棍朝我招呼，可我那时火气已经大到，就算吉姆·杰夫里斯（Jim Jeffries）冒出来挡道，我也照单全收。我索性放开手脚猛冲，正打得那位开始疑心自己误打误撞撞上了斯坦利·凯特尔（Stanley Ketchel）、小子布雷迪，或是一场炸药爆炸，先前那位却从书架里挣脱出来，俩人一齐朝我下手，一场全武行就此开锣。打到半中央，仿佛有人一口气放掉了价值五万美元的烟花；再往后我什么都不记得了，等我回过神来，人已在牢房里，差不多被拆成了零件。这就是我的小传。我承认那么撒开了干是犯傻，可我火气一上来，顾不上想那么多。」

===Original===
Psmith sighed.

“You have told me your painful story,” he said. “Now hear mine. After parting with you last night, I went meditatively back to my Fourth Avenue address, and, with a courtly good night to the large policeman who, as I have mentioned in previous conversations, is stationed almost at my very door, I passed on into my room, and had soon sunk into a dreamless slumber. At about three o’clock in the morning I was aroused by a somewhat hefty banging on the door.”

“What!”

===Chinese===
普史密斯叹了口气。

「你已向我讲述了你的惨史。」他说。「现在请听我的。昨夜与你们分手后，我沉思着回到第四大道的寓所，向那位大块头警察优雅地道了声晚安——如我在先前几次谈话中所说，他几乎就驻在我的门口——然后走进房间，不久便沉入了无梦的酣睡。凌晨三点来钟，一阵分量不小的擂门声把我惊醒。」

「什么！」

===Original===
“A banging at the door,” repeated Psmith. “There, standing on the mat, were three policemen. From their remarks I gathered that certain bright spirits had been running a gambling establishment in the lower regions of the building—where, I think I told you, there is a saloon—and the Law was now about to clean up the place. Very cordially the honest fellows invited me to go with them. A conveyance, it seemed, waited in the street without. I pointed out, even as you appear to have done, that sea-green pyjamas with old rose frogs were not the costume in which a Shropshire Psmith should be seen abroad in one of the world’s greatest cities; but they assured me—more by their manner than their words—that my misgivings were out of place, so I yielded. These men, I told myself, have lived longer in New York than I. They know what is done and what is not done. I will bow to their views. So I went with them, and after a very pleasant and cosy little ride in the patrol wagon, arrived at the police station. This morning I chatted a while with the courteous magistrate, convinced him by means of arguments and by silent evidence of my open, honest face and unwavering eye that I was not a professional gambler, and came away without a stain on my character.”

Billy Windsor listened to this narrative with growing interest.

===Chinese===
「一阵擂门声。」普史密斯重复道。「门口脚垫上站着三位警察。从他们的言谈里我获悉，若干聪明人士一直在这栋楼的地下层经营一家赌场——我想我跟你说过，那儿有家酒馆——如今法律要来清扫这块地方了。那几位诚实的伙计热诚之至地邀我同行。看样子，街头还备有一辆载客的车辆恭候。我指出——正如你方才显然也指出过的——海绿色缀古玫瑰色盘扣的睡衣，不是一位什罗普郡普史密斯在这世界数一数二的大都会里抛头露面该穿的行头；可他们向我保证——多半是靠做派而非言辞——我的顾虑不合时宜，于是我从了。这几个人，我对自己说，在纽约住得比我久。他们懂什么行得通、什么行不通。我甘拜下风。于是我随他们去了，坐了一趟愉快而温馨的囚车小游，抵达警察局。今天早晨，我与那位彬彬有礼的治安法官聊了一阵，凭种种论证，再凭我这张坦白诚实的脸和坚定不移的眼神的无声证词，让他相信我并非职业赌徒，然后一身清白地脱身而回。」

比利·温莎越听兴致越浓。

===Original===
“Gum! it’s them!” he cried.

“As Comrade Maloney would say,” said Psmith, “meaning what, Comrade Windsor?”

“Why, the fellows who are after that paper. They tipped the police off about the poolrooms, knowing that we should be hauled off without having time to take anything with us. I’ll bet anything you like they have been in and searched our rooms by now.”

“As regards yours, Comrade Windsor, I cannot say. But it is an undoubted fact that mine, which I revisited before going to the office, in order to correct what seemed to me even on reflection certain drawbacks to my costume, looks as if two cyclones and a threshing machine had passed through it.”

===Chinese===
「嚯！是那帮人干的！」他叫道。

「照马洛尼同志的句式来说，」普史密斯说，「——什么意思，温莎同志？」

「还能是谁，冲着报纸来的那伙人呗。他们向条子告发了赌池店，料定我们会被拖走，来不及带任何东西。随你赌什么，这会儿他们准已进屋搜过咱们的房间了。」

「你那间，温莎同志，我无可奉告。但我那间——我今早上报社前回去过一趟，为的是修正这身行头几处纵使再想也确属欠妥的地方——如今那副样子，像是刮过两场旋风、外加开进过一台打谷机。」

===Original===
“They’ve searched it?”

“With a fine-toothed comb. Not one of my objects of vertu but has been displaced.”

Billy Windsor slapped his knee.

“It was lucky you thought of sending that paper by post,” he said. “We should have been done if you hadn’t. But, say,” he went on miserably, “this is awful. Things are just warming up for the final burst, and I’m out of it all.”

===Chinese===
「他们搜过了？」

「拿细齿梳篦过一遍。我的艺珍藏品，没有一件不被挪过窝的。」

比利·温莎一拍膝盖。

「亏你想得到把那份文件寄走。」他说。「不然咱们就全完了。可是，」他凄凄惨惨地接着说，「这太糟了。大戏眼看要到最后的高潮，我倒被晾在场外了。」

===Original===
“For thirty days,” sighed Psmith. “What *Cosy Moments* really needs is a *sitzredakteur*.”

“A what?”

“A *sitzredakteur*, Comrade Windsor, is a gentleman employed by German newspapers with a taste for *lése majesté* to go to prison whenever required in place of the real editor. The real editor hints in his bright and snappy editorial, for instance, that the Kaiser’s moustache reminds him of a bad dream. The police force swoops down *en masse* on the office of the journal, and are met by the *sitzredakteur*, who goes with them peaceably, allowing the editor to remain and sketch out plans for his next week’s article on the Crown Prince. We need a *sitzredakteur* on *Cosy Moments* almost as much as a fighting editor; and we have neither.”

===Chinese===
「一晾三十天。」普史密斯叹道。「《舒适时光》真正缺的，是一位坐牢主编（*sitzredakteur*）。」

「一位什么？」

「坐牢主编，温莎同志，是这样一位先生：受雇于某些爱好犯大不敬罪（lése majesté）的德意志报纸，一有需要便代真主编入狱。比方说，真主编在他那篇漂亮利落的社论里暗示，德皇的八字胡让他想起一场噩梦。警力便倾巢（en masse）扑向报社办公室，迎住他们的正是这位坐牢主编，他安安分分随他们而去，好让主编留下来起草下周论皇太子的文章。我们对坐牢主编的需要，几乎不亚于对战斗编辑的需要；而这两样，我们一样都没有。」

===Original===
“The Kid has had to leave then?”

“He wants to go into training at once. He very sportingly offered to cancel his match, but of course that would never do. Unless you consider Comrade Maloney equal to the job, I must look around me for someone else. I shall be too fully occupied with purely literary matters to be able to deal with chance callers. But I have a scheme.”

“What’s that?”

===Chinese===
「这么说，小子只好走了？」

「他急于立刻投入集训。他极有体育家风度地提出取消比赛，可那自然万万使不得。除非你认为马洛尼同志胜任此职，否则我必须另觅他人。纯文学事务将占满我的时间，我腾不出手应付不速之客。不过我有一计。」

「什么计？」

===Original===
“It seems to me that we are allowing much excellent material to lie unused in the shape of Comrade Jarvis.”

“Bat Jarvis.”

“The same. The cat specialist to whom you endeared yourself somewhat earlier in the proceedings by befriending one of his wandering animals. Little deeds of kindness, little acts of love, as you have doubtless heard, help, etc. Should we not give Comrade Jarvis an opportunity of proving the correctness of this statement? I think so. Shortly after you—if you will forgive me for touching on a painful subject—have been haled to your dungeon, I will push round to Comrade Jarvis’s address, and sound him on the subject. Unfortunately, his affection is confined, I fancy, to you. Whether he will consent to put himself out on my behalf remains to be seen. However, there is no harm in trying. If nothing else comes of the visit, I shall at least have had the opportunity of chatting with one of our most prominent citizens.”

===Chinese===
「依我看，我们正放任一块上好的材料闲置着——贾维斯同志这块料。」

「巴特·贾维斯。」

「正是那位。那位猫专家——在此案较早阶段，你曾善待过他一只走失的爱畜，从而深得他的好感。小善举、小义行，想必你听过那首歌，能帮上忙，云云。我们何不给贾维斯同志一个机会，验证此话的真确性？我看该给。等你——恕我触及一个伤感的话题——被押进地牢之后不久，我便登门贾维斯同志的府上，就此题探探他的口风。可惜的是，我料想他的情谊仅限于你一人。他肯不肯为我费心，尚待分晓。不过试试总无妨。这趟拜访纵然一无所获，我至少也得了个机会，同本市最显赫的公民之一聊上一聊。」

===Original===
A policeman appeared at the door.

“Say, pal,” he remarked to Psmith, “you’ll have to be fading away soon, I guess. Give you three minutes more. Say it quick.”

He retired. Billy leaned forward to Psmith.

“I guess they won’t give me much chance,” he whispered, “but if you see me around in the next day or two, don’t be surprised.”

===Chinese===
一名警察出现在门口。

「我说，哥们儿。」他对普史密斯说，「你也差不多该撤了。再给你三分钟。有话快说。」

他退了出去。比利朝普史密斯凑近。

「我想他们不会给我多少机会。」他悄声说，「可这两天你要是瞧见我在附近转悠，别吃惊。」

===Original===
“I fail to follow you, Comrade Windsor.”

“Men have escaped from Blackwell’s Island before now. Not many, it’s true; but it has been done.”

Psmith shook his head.

“I shouldn’t,” he said. “They’re bound to catch you, and then you will be immersed in the soup beyond hope of recovery. I shouldn’t wonder if they put you in your little cell for a year or so.”

===Chinese===
「我跟不上你的思路了，温莎同志。」

「从前也有人从布莱克韦尔岛逃出来过。为数不多，这不假；可确实有人办到过。」

普史密斯摇了摇头。

「换我不这么干。」他说。「他们准会把你抓回来，到那时你就深陷汤锅，捞都捞不回来了。他们把你关回那间小号房，关上一年上下，我也不会意外。」

===Original===
“I don’t care,” said Billy stoutly. “I’d give a year later on to be round and about now.”

“I shouldn’t,” urged Psmith. “All will be well with the paper. You have left a good man at the helm.”

“I guess I shan’t get a chance, but I’ll try it if I do.”

===Chinese===
「我不在乎。」比利硬气地说。「拿往后再坐一年牢，换现在出去自由转悠，我干。」

「换我不干。」普史密斯力劝。「报纸自有我照应，一切都会安好。你已经把一位可靠的人留在了舵位上。」

「我看机会渺茫，可真有机会我就试。」

===Original===
The door opened and the policeman reappeared.

“Time’s up, I reckon.”

“Well, goodbye, Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith regretfully. “Abstain from undue worrying. It’s a walkover from now on, and there’s no earthly need for you to be around the office. Once, I admit, this could not have been said. But now things have simplified themselves. Have no fear. This act is going to be a scream from start to finish.”

===Chinese===
门开了，那名警察又露面了。

「时间到了，我估摸着。」

「那么，再会了，温莎同志。」普史密斯怅然道。「切勿忧思过度。从此往后是一马平川，你实在没有半点必要守在报社。我承认，这话从前说不得。可如今局面已经简化。放宽心。这一幕从头到尾，都将是满堂喝彩的爆笑好戏。」

## XIII Reviewing the Situation / 复盘时局
===Original===
Arriving at the bed-sitting-room, Billy proceeded to occupy the rocking chair, and, as was his wont, began to rock himself rhythmically to and fro. Psmith seated himself gracefully on the couch-bed. There was a silence.

The events of the evening had been a revelation to Psmith. He had not realised before the extent of the ramifications of New York’s underworld. That members of the gangs should crop up in the Astor roof garden and in gorgeous raiment in the middle of Broadway was a surprise. When Billy Windsor had mentioned the gangs, he had formed a mental picture of low-browed hooligans, keeping carefully to their own quarter of the town. This picture had been correct, as far as it went, but it had not gone far enough. The bulk of the gangs of New York are of the hooligan class, and are rarely met with outside their natural boundaries. But each gang has its more prosperous members; gentlemen, who, like the man of the Astor roof garden, support life by more delicate and genteel methods than the rest. The main body rely for their incomes, except at election time, on such primitive feats as robbing intoxicated pedestrians. The aristocracy of the gangs soar higher.

It was a considerable time before Billy spoke.

===Chinese===
回到那间卧室兼起居室，比利占了那把摇椅，照他的老习惯，开始有节奏地前摇后晃。普史密斯优雅地在沙发床上落了座。一时无人说话。

今晚的种种，对普史密斯不啻一场启蒙。他先前不曾意识到纽约地下世界的枝蔓竟伸展得如此之广。黑帮分子居然会冒出来，出现在阿斯特饭店的屋顶花园里、一身华服丽饰现身于百老汇正中，实在出人意料。当初比利·温莎提起黑帮，他脑海里浮现的是低眉骨的小流氓，小心缩在本城自己那一亩三分地里。这幅图画就其范围而论没有错，只是画得不够远。纽约的黑帮，主体确属流氓阶层，很少越出它们的天然疆界。但每个帮都有几名较为兴旺的成员；几位绅士——像阿斯特屋顶花园那位一样——靠比同伙更精巧、更文雅的手段维持生计。帮众的主体，除选举季外，收入全靠抢劫醉汉行人这类原始技艺。黑帮中的贵族则飞得更高。

过了好半天，比利才开口。

===Original===
“Say,” he said, “this thing wants talking over.”

“By all means, Comrade Windsor.”

“It’s this way. There’s no doubt now that we’re up against a mighty big proposition.”

“Something of the sort would seem to be the case.”

“It’s like this. I’m going to see this through. It isn’t only that I want to do a bit of good to the poor cusses in those tenements, though I’d do it for that alone. But, as far as I’m concerned, there’s something to it besides that. If we win out, I’m going to get a job out of one of the big dailies. It’ll give me just the chance I need. See what I mean? Well, it’s different with you. I don’t see that it’s up to you to run the risk of getting yourself put out of business with a blackjack, and maybe shot. Once you get mixed up with the gangs there’s no saying what’s going to be doing. Well, I don’t see why you shouldn’t quit. All this has got nothing to do with you. You’re over here on a vacation. You haven’t got to make a living this side. You want to go about and have a good time, instead of getting mixed up with—”

He broke off.

“Well, that’s what I wanted to say, anyway,” he concluded.

Psmith looked at him reproachfully.

===Chinese===
「我说，」他说，「这事得好好合计合计。」

「务必如此，温莎同志。」

「是这么回事。如今毫无疑问，咱们顶上的是一桩顶大的买卖。」

「看来情况确有点类似。」

「事情是这样。我打算把这事干到底。倒不只是因为我想给廉租公寓里那些穷苦人做点好事——单为这个我也干。可就我而言，这里头还有别的名堂。要是咱们赢了，我就能从哪家大日报手里谋个差事。那正好给我需要的机会。明白我的意思吗？可你不一样。我看不出来，你凭什么该冒这个险，让人家用铅头短棍把你打出局，没准还挨枪子儿。一旦跟黑帮搅上，谁也说不准会出什么事。所以呢，我看你退出也没什么不行。这整件事跟你毫不相干。你是来这边度假的。你用不着在这边挣钱糊口。你要做的是四处走走、痛痛快快玩一场，而不是跟——」

他刹住了话头。

「反正，我要说的就是这些。」他收尾道。

普史密斯责备地望着他。

===Original===
“Are you trying to *sack* me, Comrade Windsor?”

“How’s that?”

“In various treatises on ‘How to Succeed in Literature,’” said Psmith sadly, “which I have read from time to time, I have always found it stated that what the novice chiefly needed was an editor who believed in him. In you, Comrade Windsor, I fancied that I had found such an editor.”

“What’s all this about?” demanded Billy. “I’m making no kick about your work.”

“I gathered from your remarks that you were anxious to receive my resignation.”

===Chinese===
「你这是要开除我吗，温莎同志？」

「这话怎讲？」

「在种种《如何在文学界成功》的论著里，」普史密斯哀伤地说，「那些我时不时拿来读一读的论著里，我总看到这样一条：新手最需要的，是一位信任他的编辑。我原以为，温莎同志，在你身上我找到了这样一位编辑。」

「这都是哪儿跟哪儿？」比利质问道，「你的工作我可没有半个不字。」

「我从你的话里听出来，你正巴不得收下我的辞呈。」

===Original===
“Well, I told you why. I didn’t want you to be blackjacked.”

“Was that the only reason?”

“Sure.”

“Then all is well,” said Psmith, relieved. “For the moment I fancied that my literary talents had been weighed in the balance and adjudged below par. If that is all—why, these are the mere everyday risks of the young journalist’s life. Without them we should be dull and dissatisfied. Our work would lose its fire. Men such as ourselves, Comrade Windsor, need a certain stimulus, a certain fillip, if they are to keep up their high standards. The knowledge that a low-browed gentleman is waiting round the corner with a sandbag poised in air will just supply that stimulus. Also that fillip. It will give our output precisely the edge it requires.”

“Then you’ll stay in this thing? You’ll stick to the work?”

“Like a conscientious leech, Comrade Windsor.”

“Bully for you,” said Billy.

===Chinese===
「我不是说了原因嘛。我不想让你挨铅头短棍。」

「就这一个原因？」

「那当然。」

「那就都好说了，」普史密斯松了口气，「方才有一瞬，我还以为我的文学才华被放上天平一称，判了个成色不足。若仅此而已——嗐，这些不过是青年记者生涯的日常风险。没有它们，我们就会又沉闷又不满。我们的文笔会失了火气。像你我这样的人，温莎同志，要想保持高标准，需要某种刺激，某种鞭策。知道有位低眉骨的先生正埋伏在街角、沙袋高高举在半空，恰好能提供那种刺激。还有那种鞭策。它会给我们的产出添上恰恰需要的那股锋芒。」

「那么你要留下干这一场？这活儿你干到底？」

「像一条尽职的水蛭，温莎同志。」

「好样的！」比利说。

===Original===
It was not Psmith’s habit, when he felt deeply on any subject, to exhibit his feelings; and this matter of the tenements had hit him harder than anyone who did not know him intimately would have imagined. Mike would have understood him, but Billy Windsor was too recent an acquaintance. Psmith was one of those people who are content to accept most of the happenings of life in an airy spirit of tolerance. Life had been more or less of a game with him up till now. In his previous encounters with those with whom fate had brought him in contact there had been little at stake. The prize of victory had been merely a comfortable feeling of having had the best of a battle of wits; the penalty of defeat nothing worse than the discomfort of having failed to score. But this tenement business was different. Here he had touched the realities. There was something worth fighting for. His lot had been cast in pleasant places, and the sight of actual raw misery had come home to him with an added force from that circumstance. He was fully aware of the risks that he must run. The words of the man at the Astor, and still more the episodes of the family friend from Missouri and the taximeter cab, had shown him that this thing was on a different plane from anything that had happened to him before. It was a fight without the gloves, and to a finish at that. But he meant to see it through. Somehow or other those tenement houses had got to be cleaned up. If it meant trouble, as it undoubtedly did, that trouble would have to be faced.

===Chinese===
普史密斯素来不惯在深感某事时流露感情；而这桩廉租公寓的事，对他的打击之重，是不深知他的人万万想不到的。迈克会懂他，可比利·温莎相识未久。普史密斯属于那种人：人生的大部分遭际，都以一种轻灵的宽容态度受之。迄今为止，人生于他多多少少是一场游戏。他从前与命运替他安排的那些对手过招，赌注从来微薄。赢的彩头，不过是斗智斗赢了一场的那种舒坦感觉；输的罚金，至多是没能得分的那点不痛快。但廉租公寓这桩事不同。在这里他触到了实实在在的现实。有值得为之而战的东西了。他自己的命运素来抛在安乐窝里，正因如此，赤裸裸的惨状出现在他眼前时，才带着加倍的分量直击心口。他完全清楚自己要冒的风险。阿斯特饭店那人的一番话，更兼密苏里来的世交和计程车那两桩事故，都已向他表明：这一场与前此发生在他身上的任何事都不在一个层面上。这是一场不戴拳套的搏斗，而且要打到底。但他打算奉陪到底。不管怎么说，那些廉租公寓非得整治干净不可。若这意味着麻烦——无疑意味着——那麻烦也只能迎上去。

===Original===
“Now that Comrade Jarvis,” he said, “showing a spirit of forbearance which, I am bound to say, does him credit, has declined the congenial task of fracturing our occiputs, who should you say, Comrade Windsor, would be the chosen substitute?”

Billy shook his head. “Now that Bat has turned up the job, it might be any one of three gangs. There are four main gangs, you know. Bat’s is the biggest. But the smallest of them’s large enough to put us away, if we give them the chance.”

“I don’t quite grasp the nice points of this matter. Do you mean that we have an entire gang on our trail in one solid mass, or will it be merely a section?”

“Well, a section, I guess, if it comes to that. Parker, or whoever fixed this thing up, would go to the main boss of the gang. If it was the Three Points, he’d go to Spider Reilly. If it was the Table Hill lot, he’d look up Dude Dawson. And so on.”

===Chinese===
「如今贾维斯同志，」他说，「以一股克制之功——我必须说，这为他增光——回绝了敲碎我们后脑壳这份称心的差事，那么依你看，温莎同志，谁会是中选的替补？」

比利摇了摇头。「巴特既然推了这个活，那就可能是三个帮里的随便哪一个。你知道的，纽约有四大黑帮。巴特那帮最大。可最小的那个，也大得足以要咱们的命，只要咱们给他们机会。」

「这件事的精微之处我还没太抓住。你是说，会有一整帮人马铁板一块地缀在咱们身后，还是只来一支分遣队？」

「唔，真到那一步的话，大概是一小股。帕克，或者不管是谁张罗的这桩事，会去找那个帮的总龙头。要是三点帮，他就去找蜘蛛莱利。要是桌山帮那伙，他就去找纨绔子道森。依此类推。」

===Original===
“And what then?”

“And then the boss would talk it over with his own special partners. Every gang leader has about a dozen of them. A sort of Inner Circle. They’d fix it up among themselves. The rest of the gang wouldn’t know anything about it. The fewer in the game, you see, the fewer to split up the dollars.”

“I see. Then things are not so black. All we have to do is to look out for about a dozen hooligans with a natural dignity in their bearing, the result of intimacy with the main boss. Carefully eluding these aristocrats, we shall win through. I fancy, Comrade Windsor, that all may yet be well. What steps do you propose to take by way of self-defence?”

===Chinese===
「然后呢？」

「然后龙头会跟他自己的心腹伙计们合计。每个帮头目都有那么一打。算是个内圈。他们自己内部就把事情定了。帮里其余的人什么都不会知道。入局的人越少，分钱的人越少，你懂吧。」

「我明白了。那事情还不算太黑。咱们只需提防一打上下的小流氓，他们因与总龙头过从甚密，天然带着几分威仪。小心避开这帮贵族，我们就能凯旋。我看，温莎同志，一切终会圆满。为自卫计，你打算采取什么步骤？」

===Original===
“Keep out in the middle of the street, and not go off the Broadway after dark. You’re pretty safe on Broadway. There’s too much light for them there.”

“Now that our sleuthhound friend in the taximeter has ascertained your address, shall you change it?”

“It wouldn’t do any good. They’d soon find where I’d gone to. How about yours?”

“I fancy I shall be tolerably all right. A particularly massive policeman is on duty at my very doors. So much for our private lives. But what of the daytime? Suppose these sandbag specialists drop in at the office during business hours. Will Comrade Maloney’s frank and manly statement that we are not in be sufficient to keep them out? I doubt it. All unused to the nice conventions of polite society, these rugged persons will charge through. In such circumstances good work will be hard to achieve. Your literary man must have complete quiet if he is to give the public of his best. But stay. An idea!”

“Well?”

===Chinese===
「走街中间，天黑后别离开百老汇。在百老汇上你相当安全。那儿灯太亮，他们下不了手。」

「如今咱们那位计程车里的猎犬朋友已经查明你的住址，你要换一个吗？」

「换了也没用。他们很快就能查到我搬哪儿去了。你的呢？」

「我想我大致不会出事。我家门口正站着一位格外魁伟的警察。私生活方面就说到这儿。可白天呢？万一这批沙袋专家在办公时间光临编辑部怎么办？马洛尼同志那句直率而有男子气概的『我们不在』，足以把他们挡在门外吗？我深表怀疑。这帮粗人对上流社会的雅规矩全然生疏，会一路硬冲进来。那种情形下，好文章就难产了。文人要拿出最好的东西给公众，必须享有彻底的清静。且慢。有了！」

「哦？」

===Original===
“Comrade Brady. The Peerless Kid. The man *Cosy Moments* is running for the lightweight championship. We are his pugilistic sponsors. You may say that it is entirely owing to our efforts that he has obtained this match with—who exactly is the gentleman Comrade Brady fights at the Highfield Club on Friday night?”

“Cyclone Al. Wolmann, isn’t it?”

“You are right. As I was saying, but for us the privilege of smiting Comrade Cyclone Al. Wolmann under the fifth rib on Friday night would almost certainly have been denied to him.”

===Chinese===
「布雷迪同志。无双小子（The Peerless Kid）。《舒适时光》正捧他去夺轻量级冠军的那个人。我们是他的拳坛保荐人。可以说，全亏我们的努力，他才捞到了与——布雷迪同志礼拜五晚在海菲尔德俱乐部交手的那位先生，究竟是谁来着？」

「『旋风』阿尔·沃尔曼，不是吗？」

「你说得对。正如我方才所言，若不是有我们，礼拜五晚上击打旋风阿尔·沃尔曼同志第五根肋骨的殊荣，几乎肯定与他无缘。」

===Original===
It almost seemed as if he were right. From the moment the paper had taken up his cause, Kid Brady’s star had undoubtedly been in the ascendant. People began to talk about him as a likely man. Edgren, in the *Evening World*, had a paragraph about his chances for the lightweight title. Tad, in the *Journal*, drew a picture of him. Finally, the management of the Highfield Club had signed him for a ten-round bout with Mr. Wolmann. There were, therefore, reasons why *Cosy Moments* should feel a claim on the Kid’s services.

“He should,” continued Psmith, “if equipped in any degree with finer feelings, be bubbling over with gratitude towards us. ‘But for *Cosy Moments*,’ he should be saying to himself, ‘where should I be? Among the also-rans.’ I imagine that he will do any little thing we care to ask of him. I suggest that we approach Comrade Brady, explain the facts of the case, and offer him at a comfortable salary the post of fighting editor of *Cosy Moments*. His duties will be to sit in the room opening out of ours, girded as to the loins and full of martial spirit, and apply some of those half-scissor hooks of his to the persons of any who overcome the opposition of Comrade Maloney. We, meanwhile, will enjoy that leisure and freedom from interruption which is so essential to the artist.”

“It’s not a bad idea,” said Billy.

“It is about the soundest idea,” said Psmith, “that has ever been struck. One of your newspaper friends shall supply us with tickets, and Friday night shall see us at the Highfield.”

===Chinese===
这话听起来几乎就像是对的。自报纸接手为他造势那一刻起，小子布雷迪的星运无疑节节上升。人们开始把他当作一个有出息的人谈论。埃德格伦（Edgren）在《晚间世界报》（*Evening World*）上写了段评论他轻量级夺冠前景的文字。泰德（Tad）在《新闻报》（*Journal*）上给他画了幅画。最后，海菲尔德俱乐部的经理班子与他签下了对阵沃尔曼先生的十回合比赛。因此，《舒适时光》有理由觉得，小子欠自己一份效力。

「他若还有几分细腻的情感，」普史密斯接着说，「就该对我们感激得冒泡。『若不是《舒适时光》，』他应该对自己说，『我如今在哪里？在一群落榜生中间。』我想，我们乐意请他办点什么小事，他都会办。我提议：去找布雷迪同志，说明案情，以一份舒坦的薪水，把《舒适时光》战斗编辑的职位授给他。他的职责是坐在我们外间那间屋里，腰间束紧，满怀尚武精神，对任何突破马洛尼同志防线的人，赏几记他的半剪式勾拳。而我们呢，则可享受那份清静与免于打扰——艺术家最不可或缺的正是这两样。」

「这主意不赖。」比利说。

「这大约是有史以来被敲出来的最响当当的主意，」普史密斯说，「叫你哪位报界朋友给咱们弄几张票，礼拜五晚上咱们海菲尔德见。」

## XXVIII Standing Room Only / 只剩站票
===Original===
In the light of subsequent events it was perhaps the least bit unfortunate that Mr. Jarvis should have seen fit to bring with him to the office of *Cosy Moments* on the following morning two of his celebrated squad of cats, and that Long Otto, who, as usual, accompanied him, should have been fired by his example to the extent of introducing a large and rather boisterous yellow dog. They were not to be blamed, of course. They could not know that before the morning was over space in the office would be at a premium. Still, it was unfortunate.

Mr. Jarvis was slightly apologetic.

“T’ought I’d bring de kits along,” he said. “Dey started in scrappin’ yesterday when I was here, so today I says I’ll keep my eye on dem.”

Psmith inspected the menagerie without resentment.

===Chinese===
事后来看，贾维斯先生第二天早晨把他的猫班子里两只名猫带到《舒适时光》报社来，而照例随行的高个奥托受他榜样的感召，也引进了一只个头不小、相当闹腾的黄狗——这或许多少有点不凑巧。当然，怪不得他们。他们无从知道，不等这个上午过完，报社里的空间就会变得寸土寸金。尽管如此，终究是有点不凑巧。

贾维斯先生略带歉意。

「我想把猫咪们带来。」他说。「昨儿我在这儿的时候它们就打起来了，所以今儿我说，我得亲自盯着它们。」

普史密斯察看这支小小动物园，并无愠色。

===Original===
“Assuredly, Comrade Jarvis,” he said. “They add a pleasantly cosy and domestic touch to the scene. The only possible criticism I can find to make has to do with their probable brawling with the dog.”

“Oh, dey won’t scrap wit de dawg. Dey knows him.”

“But is he aware of that? He looks to me a somewhat impulsive animal. Well, well, the matter’s in your hands. If you will undertake to look after the refereeing of any pogrom that may arise, I say no more.”

Mr. Jarvis’s statement as to the friendly relations between the animals proved to be correct. The dog made no attempt to annihilate the cats. After an inquisitive journey round the room he lay down and went to sleep, and an era of peace set in. The cats had settled themselves comfortably, one on each of Mr. Jarvis’s knees, and Long Otto, surveying the ceiling with his customary glassy stare, smoked a long cigar in silence. Bat breathed a tune, and scratched one of the cats under the ear. It was a soothing scene.

===Chinese===
「尽管带来，贾维斯同志。」他说。「他们为这场景平添了一抹宜人的温馨家宅气息。我唯一挑得出的毛病，与它们同那条狗可能爆发的斗殴有关。」

「嗐，它们不会跟狗干的。它们认得它。」

「可它自己也知道吗？依我看，它是头有点冲动的牲口。罢了罢了，这事归你掌管。你若肯担当执法，照看任何可能爆发的集体械斗，我便无话可说。」

贾维斯先生关于兽际友好关系的陈述，被证明属实。狗没有半点歼灭猫群的企图。它满屋好奇地巡视一周后，卧下睡了，一个和平时代就此开启。两只猫各自舒舒服服地安顿在贾维斯先生的一边膝盖上；高个奥托以他惯常的呆滞凝望仰视天花板，默默抽着一支长雪茄。巴特用鼻音哼着一支小调，搔着其中一只猫的耳根。这是一幕安神怡人的场景。

===Original===
But it did not last. Ten minutes had barely elapsed when the yellow dog, sitting up with a start, uttered a whine. In the outer office could be heard a stir and movement. The next moment the door burst open and a little man dashed in. He had a peeled nose and showed other evidences of having been living in the open air. Behind him was a crowd of uncertain numbers. Psmith recognised the leaders of this crowd. They were the Reverend Edwin T. Philpotts and Mr. B. Henderson Asher.

“Why, Comrade Asher,” he said, “this is indeed a Moment of Mirth. I have been wondering for weeks where you could have got to. And Comrade Philpotts! Am I wrong in saying that this is the maddest, merriest day of all the glad New Year?”

The rest of the crowd had entered the room.

“Comrade Waterman, too!” cried Psmith. “Why we have all met before. Except—”

===Chinese===
但这光景没能持久。十分钟还没满，那条黄狗猛然坐起，呜了一声。外间办公室传来一阵骚动。下一刻，门被撞开，一个小个子男人冲了进来。他鼻子上脱了皮，还带着其他风餐露宿的痕迹。他身后跟着一伙人数难辨的人群。普史密斯认出了这伙人的领头者：埃德温·T·菲尔波茨牧师和 B·亨德森·阿舍先生。

「哎呀，阿舍同志，」他说，「这当真是一刻『欢笑时光』。几个星期来我一直在纳闷，您究竟去了哪里。还有菲尔波茨同志！难道我说错了——今天不是这欢乐新岁里最疯最乐的一天？」

人群的其余部分也进了屋。

「沃特曼同志也在！」普史密斯呼道。「瞧，咱们全都见过。只除了——」

===Original===
He glanced inquiringly at the little man with the peeled nose.

“My name is Wilberfloss,” said the other with austerity. “Will you be so good as to tell me where Mr. Windsor is?”

A murmur of approval from his followers.

“In one moment,” said Psmith. “First, however, let me introduce two important members of our staff. On your right, Mr. Bat Jarvis. On your left, Mr. Long Otto. Both of Groome Street.”

===Chinese===
他探询地瞥了一眼那个鼻子脱皮的小个子。

「我叫威尔伯弗洛斯。」对方板着脸说。「可否劳驾告诉我，温莎先生在哪里？」

他的追随者们发出一片赞许的低语。

「稍安毋躁。」普史密斯说。「不过先容我介绍本报两位重要成员。您右手边，巴特·贾维斯先生。您左手边，高个奥托先生。二位都是格鲁姆街人士。」

===Original===
The two Bowery boys rose awkwardly. The cats fell in an avalanche to the floor. Long Otto, in his haste, trod on the dog, which began barking, a process which it kept up almost without a pause during the rest of the interview.

“Mr. Wilberfloss,” said Psmith in an aside to Bat, “is widely known as a cat fancier in Brooklyn circles.”

“Honest?” said Mr. Jarvis. He tapped Mr. Wilberfloss in friendly fashion on the chest. “Say,” he asked, “did youse ever have a cat wit one blue and one yellow eye?”

Mr. Wilberfloss sidestepped and turned once more to Psmith, who was offering B. Henderson Asher a cigarette.

===Chinese===
两位鲍厄里小子笨拙地站起身。两只猫雪崩般跌落地板。高个奥托慌乱中踩着了狗，狗开始狂吠——这门功课，它在余下的会谈里几乎不曾间断地温习着。

「威尔伯弗洛斯先生，」普史密斯侧头对巴特说，「在布鲁克林各圈子里是远近闻名的爱猫人士。」

「真的？」贾维斯先生说。他友好地拍了拍威尔伯弗洛斯先生的胸口。「我说，」他问，「你养过一只眼蓝一只眼黄的猫吗？」

威尔伯弗洛斯先生侧身避开，重新转向普史密斯——后者正给 B·亨德森·阿舍敬烟。

===Original===
“Who are you?” he demanded.

“Who am *I*?” repeated Psmith in an astonished tone.

“Who are you?”

“I am Psmith,” said the old Etonian reverently. “There is a preliminary P before the name. This, however, is silent. Like the tomb. Compare such words as ptarmigan, psalm, and phthisis.”

===Chinese===
「你是谁？」他质问。

「*我*是谁？」普史密斯用吃惊的口气重复道。

「你是谁？」

「我是普史密斯。」这位老伊顿生虔敬地说。「名字前头有一个起首的 P。不过这个 P 不发音。像坟墓一样静。可参照 ptarmigan（雷鸟）、psalm（诗篇）、phthisis（痨病）一类词。」

===Original===
“These gentlemen tell me you’re acting editor. Who appointed you?”

Psmith reflected.

“It is rather a nice point,” he said. “It might be claimed that I appointed myself. You may say, however, that Comrade Windsor appointed me.”

“Ah! And where is Mr. Windsor?”

“In prison,” said Psmith sorrowfully.

===Chinese===
「这几位先生告诉我，你是代理主编。谁任命的你？」

普史密斯想了想。

「这问题相当微妙。」他说。「也可以主张，是我自己任命了自己。不过您大可以说，是温莎同志任命的我。」

「啊！那么温莎先生在哪里？」

「在坐牢。」普史密斯悲伤地说。

===Original===
“In prison!”

Psmith nodded.

“It is too true. Such is the generous impulsiveness of Comrade Windsor’s nature that he hit a policeman, was promptly gathered in, and is now serving a sentence of thirty days on Blackwell’s Island.”

Mr. Wilberfloss looked at Mr. Philpotts. Mr. Asher looked at Mr. Wilberfloss. Mr. Waterman started, and stumbled over a cat.

===Chinese===
「坐牢！」

普史密斯点点头。

「不幸正是如此。温莎同志天性豪爽冲动，一拳打了警察，当即被收进网里，此刻正在布莱克韦尔岛服三十天的刑。」

威尔伯弗洛斯先生看看菲尔波茨先生。阿舍先生看看威尔伯弗洛斯先生。沃特曼先生一惊，绊在了一只猫身上。

===Original===
“I never heard of such a thing,” said Mr. Wilberfloss.

A faint, sad smile played across Psmith’s face.

“Do you remember, Comrade Waterman—I fancy it was to you that I made the remark—my commenting at our previous interview on the rashness of confusing the unusual with the improbable? Here we see Comrade Wilberfloss, big-brained though he is, falling into error.”

“I shall dismiss Mr. Windsor immediately,” said the big-brained one.

===Chinese===
「我从没听说过这种事。」威尔伯弗洛斯先生说。

一抹淡淡而哀伤的微笑掠过普史密斯的脸。

「你可记得，沃特曼同志——我记得这话正是对你说的——上次会谈时我评论过，把罕见与不可能混为一谈是何等轻率？眼前我们就看到威尔伯弗洛斯同志，尽管脑容量可观，也栽进了这个错误。」

「我立刻就把温莎先生解雇。」那位大脑袋说。

===Original===
“From Blackwell’s Island?” said Psmith. “I am sure you will earn his gratitude if you do. They live on bean soup there. Bean soup and bread, and not much of either.”

He broke off, to turn his attention to Mr. Jarvis and Mr. Waterman, between whom bad blood seemed to have arisen. Mr. Jarvis, holding a cat in his arms, was glowering at Mr. Waterman, who had backed away and seemed nervous.

“What is the trouble, Comrade Jarvis?”

“Dat guy dere wit two left feet,” said Bat querulously, “goes and treads on de kit. I—”

===Chinese===
「从布莱克韦尔岛上解雇？」普史密斯说。「您若办得到，他一定感激不尽。那儿的人靠豆汤过活。豆汤加面包，两样都还不管够。」

他话锋一收，把注意力转向贾维斯先生和沃特曼先生——这两位之间似乎结下了梁子。贾维斯先生怀抱一只猫，正虎视眈眈地瞪着沃特曼先生；后者连连后退，神色慌张。

「出了什么岔子，贾维斯同志？」

「那个长了两只左脚的家伙，」巴特嘟嘟囔囔地说，「偏就踩了猫咪一脚。我——」

===Original===
“I assure you it was a pure accident. The animal—”

Mr. Wilberfloss, eyeing Bat and the silent Otto with disgust, intervened.

“Who are these persons, Mr. Smith?” he inquired.

“Poisson yourself,” rejoined Bat, justly incensed. “Who’s de little guy wit de peeled breezer, Mr. Smith?”

===Chinese===
「我向您保证，纯属意外。这牲口——」

威尔伯弗洛斯先生嫌恶地扫了巴特和闷声不响的奥托一眼，插了进来。

「这几位是什么人，史密斯先生？」他质问。

「你才是那号人。」巴特义愤填膺地回敬。「那个帽子脱了皮的小个子又是谁，史密斯先生？」

===Original===
Psmith waved his hands.

“Gentlemen, gentlemen,” he said, “let us not descend to mere personalities. I thought I had introduced you. This, Comrade Jarvis, is Mr. Wilberfloss, the editor of this journal. These, Comrade Wilberfloss—Zam-buk would put your nose right in a day—are, respectively, Bat Jarvis and Long Otto, our acting fighting editors, vice Kid Brady, absent on unavoidable business.”

“Kid Brady!” shrilled Mr. Wilberfloss. “I insist that you give me a full explanation of this matter. I go away by my doctor’s orders for ten weeks, leaving Mr. Windsor to conduct the paper on certain well-defined lines. I return yesterday, and, getting into communication with Mr. Philpotts, what do I find? Why, that in my absence the paper has been ruined.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯摆了摆手。

「诸位，诸位，」他说，「咱们犯不着堕入人身攻讦。我记得已为你们引见过。这位，贾维斯同志，是威尔伯弗洛斯先生，本报主编。这几位，威尔伯弗洛斯同志——赞姆巴克药膏（Zam-buk）一天就能治好您的鼻子——分别是巴特·贾维斯和高个奥托，本报代理战斗编辑，接替因要务缺席的小子布雷迪。」

「小子布雷迪！」威尔伯弗洛斯先生尖声叫道。「我要求你对这件事给我一个完整的交代。我遵医嘱离开十周，把报纸交给温莎先生，让他在若干界定明确的路线上主持报务。我昨天回来，同菲尔波茨先生一接头，你猜我发现了什么？嘿，我不在的这些日子，报纸已经被毁了。」

===Original===
“Ruined?” said Psmith. “On the contrary. Examine the returns, and you will see that the circulation has gone up every week. *Cosy Moments* was never so prosperous and flourishing. Comrade Otto, do you think you could use your personal influence with that dog to induce it to suspend its barking for a while? It is musical, but renders conversation difficult.”

Long Otto raised a massive boot and aimed it at the animal, which, dodging with a yelp, cannoned against the second cat and had its nose scratched. Piercing shrieks cleft the air.

“I demand an explanation,” roared Mr. Wilberfloss above the din.

“I think, Comrade Otto,” said Psmith, “it would make things a little easier if you removed that dog.”

===Chinese===
「毁了？」普史密斯说。「恰恰相反。查查发行数据，您会看到销量周周上涨。《舒适时光》从来没有这般兴旺繁荣过。奥托同志，你可否对那条狗动用一下你的私人影响力，劝它暂停吠叫片刻？叫声固然悦耳，却妨碍交谈。」

高个奥托抬起一只厚重的大靴，朝那牲口瞄去；那狗呜地一闪，撞上第二只猫，鼻头挨了一记抓挠。凄厉的尖叫声划破空气。

「我要求一个交代。」威尔伯弗洛斯先生在喧闹之上吼道。

「我看，奥托同志，」普史密斯说，「你把那条狗请出去，事情会好办一些。」

===Original===
He opened the door. The dog shot out. They could hear it being ejected from the outer office by Master Maloney. When there was silence, Psmith turned courteously to the editor.

“You were saying, Comrade Wilberfloss?”

“Who is this person Brady? With Mr. Philpotts I have been going carefully over the numbers which have been issued since my departure—”

“An intellectual treat,” murmured Psmith.

===Chinese===
他打开门。狗一溜烟蹿了出去。众人听得见它被马洛尼少爷从外间轰出去的动静。等安静下来，普史密斯客气地转向主编。

「您方才说到哪儿了，威尔伯弗洛斯同志？」

「这个叫布雷迪的是什么人？我同菲尔波茨先生把我离开后已出的各期仔仔细细过了一遍——」

「一场智识的飨宴。」普史密斯喃喃道。

===Original===
“—and in each there is a picture of this young man in a costume which I will not particularise—”

“There is hardly enough of it to particularise.”

“—together with a page of disgusting autobiographical matter.”

Psmith held up his hand.

“I protest,” he said. “We court criticism, but this is mere abuse. I appeal to these gentlemen to say whether this, for instance, is not bright and interesting.”

===Chinese===
「——每一期里都有这个年轻人的画像，穿的那身行头我不屑细说——」

「也几乎没有多少可供细说的。」

「——外加一整页令人作呕的自传材料。」

普史密斯举起手。

「我抗议。」他说。「我们欢迎批评，可这纯属谩骂。我请诸位先生评评理，比方说这一篇，难道不鲜活有趣吗？」

===Original===
He picked up the current number of *Cosy Moments*, and turned to the Kid’s page.

“This,” he said. “Describing a certain ten-round unpleasantness with one Mexican Joe. ‘Joe comes up for the second round and he gives me a nasty look, but I thinks of my mother and swats him one in the lower ribs. He hollers foul, but nix on that. Referee says, “Fight on.” Joe gives me another nasty look. “All right, Kid,” he says; “now I’ll knock you up into the gallery.” And with that he cuts loose with a right swing, but I falls into the clinch, and then—!’”

“Bah!” exclaimed Mr. Wilberfloss.

“Go on, boss,” urged Mr. Jarvis approvingly. “It’s to de good, dat stuff.”

===Chinese===
他拿起最新一期《舒适时光》，翻到小子的专版。

「就这篇。」他说。「描述与一位墨西哥人乔（Mexican Joe）十回合的不痛快。『乔上来打第二回合，恶狠狠瞅了我一眼，可我想起俺娘，照他下肋就是一家伙。他嚎犯规，没那回事。裁判说：「接着打。」乔又恶狠狠瞅我一眼。「行，小子，」他说；「这回我把你打上看台。」说着他甩开一记右摆拳，可我顺势缠抱上去，然后——！』」

「呸！」威尔伯弗洛斯先生喝道。

「接着念啊，老板。」贾维斯先生赞许地怂恿。「这玩意儿，地道好货。」

===Original===
“There!” said Psmith triumphantly. “You heard? Comrade Jarvis, one of the most firmly established critics east of Fifth Avenue, stamps Kid Brady’s reminiscences with the hallmark of his approval.”

“I falls fer de Kid every time,” assented Mr. Jarvis.

“Assuredly, Comrade Jarvis. You know a good thing when you see one. Why,” he went on warmly, “there is stuff in these reminiscences which would stir the blood of a jellyfish. Let me quote you another passage to show that they are not only enthralling, but helpful as well. Let me see, where is it? Ah, I have it. ‘A bully good way of putting a guy out of business is this. You don’t want to use it in the ring, because by Queensberry Rules it’s a foul; but you will find it mighty useful if any thick-neck comes up to you in the street and tries to start anything. It’s this way. While he’s setting himself for a punch, just place the tips of the fingers of your left hand on the right side of his chest. Then bring down the heel of your left hand. There isn’t a guy living that could stand up against that. The fingers give you a leverage to beat the band. The guy doubles up, and you uppercut him with your right, and out he goes.’ Now, I bet you never knew that before, Comrade Philpotts. Try it on your parishioners.”

===Chinese===
「听见没有！」普史密斯得意地说。「你们都听见了？贾维斯同志，第五大道以东根基最稳的评论家之一，给小子布雷迪的回忆录打上了他嘉许的检验钢印。」

「小子写的，我回回都服。」贾维斯先生表同意。

「那是自然，贾维斯同志。好东西一到眼前你便识货。何况，」他热情地继续道，「这些回忆录里有些货色，能把海蜇的血都搅沸腾了。容我再引一段，证明它们不仅引人入胜，而且大有益处。让我想想，在哪儿来着？啊，找到了。『把一个家伙送出局的一个绝妙法子是这样。拳台上使不得，按昆斯伯里规则（Queensberry Rules）这算犯规；可要是哪个粗脖子在街上凑上来寻衅，你会发现它用处大得很。法子是这样。趁他扎马要出拳，只消把你左手的指尖搭在他胸口的右侧。然后左手的掌根压下去。天底下没有一个家伙扛得住这个。指尖给你的杠杆力，盖了帽了。那家伙一折腰，你右手一记上勾，他就出局了。』怎么样，菲尔波茨同志，我敢打赌您从前不知道这一手。拿您的教区居民试试吧。」

===Original===
“*Cosy Moments*,” said Mr. Wilberfloss irately, “is no medium for exploiting low prizefighters.”

“Low prizefighters! Comrade Wilberfloss, you have been misinformed. The Kid is as decent a little chap as you’d meet anywhere. You do not seem to appreciate the philanthropic motives of the paper in adopting Comrade Brady’s cause. Think of it, Comrade Wilberfloss. There was that unfortunate stripling with only two pleasures in life, to love his mother and to knock the heads off other youths whose weight coincided with his own; and misfortune, until we took him up, had barred him almost completely from the second pastime. Our editorial heart was melted. We adopted Comrade Brady. And look at him now! Matched against Eddie Wood! And Comrade Waterman will support me in my statement that a victory over Eddie Wood means that he gets a legitimate claim to meet Jimmy Garvin for the championship.”

“It is abominable,” burst forth Mr. Wilberfloss. “It is disgraceful. I never heard of such a thing. The paper is ruined.”

===Chinese===
「《舒适时光》，」威尔伯弗洛斯先生怒冲冲地说，「不是捧下三滥拳手的园地。」

「下三滥拳手！威尔伯弗洛斯同志，您得到的是错误情报。小子是您走到哪儿都碰不着的好孩子。您似乎没有领会本报收留布雷迪同志这一义举的慈善动机。设身处地想想，威尔伯弗洛斯同志。有这么一位不幸的少年，人生仅有两桩乐趣：一是孝敬母亲，二是把跟他同一体重的其他少年的脑袋敲下来；而不幸——直到我们收留他之前——几乎把他拦在第二桩消遣之外。我们编辑的心融化了。我们收留了布雷迪同志。再看看他如今！与埃迪·伍德配赛了！而沃特曼同志会支持我的论断：战胜埃迪·伍德，就意味着取得正当资格，与吉米·加文争夺冠军。」

「可恶至极。」威尔伯弗洛斯先生爆发了。「丢人现眼。我从没听说过这种事。报纸毁了。」

===Original===
“You keep reverting to that statement, Comrade Wilberfloss. Can nothing reassure you? The returns are excellent. Prosperity beams on us like a sun. The proprietor is more than satisfied.”

“The proprietor?” gasped Mr. Wilberfloss. “Does *he* know how you have treated the paper?”

“He is cognisant of our every move.”

“And he approves?”

“He more than approves.”

===Chinese===
「您不断回到这句断语上，威尔伯弗洛斯同志。就没有什么能安您的心吗？发行数据好极了。兴旺如一轮太阳照临我们。老板满意得不能再满意。」

「老板？」威尔伯弗洛斯先生倒抽一口气。「*他*知道你们把报纸糟蹋成了什么样吗？」

「我们的每一步他都了然于胸。」

「而且他赞成？」

「他何止赞成。」

===Original===
Mr. Wilberfloss snorted.

“I don’t believe it,” he said.

The assembled ex-contributors backed up this statement with a united murmur. B. Henderson Asher snorted satirically.

“They don’t believe it,” sighed Psmith. “Nevertheless, it is true.”

“It is not true,” thundered Mr. Wilberfloss, hopping to avoid a perambulating cat. “Nothing will convince me of it. Mr. Benjamin White is not a maniac.”

===Chinese===
威尔伯弗洛斯先生嗤了一声。

「我不信。」他说。

济济一堂的前撰稿人们齐声低语，为这句断言背书。B·亨德森·阿舍讽刺地哼了一声。

「他们不信。」普史密斯叹道。「然而，这是事实。」

「这不是事实。」威尔伯弗洛斯先生雷霆大怒，跳脚避开一只四处溜达的猫。「什么都休想让我信。本杰明·怀特先生不是疯子。」

===Original===
“I trust not,” said Psmith. “I sincerely trust not. I have every reason to believe in his complete sanity. What makes you fancy that there is even a possibility of his being—er—?”

“Nobody but a lunatic would approve of seeing his paper ruined.”

“Again!” said Psmith. “I fear that the notion that this journal is ruined has become an obsession with you, Comrade Wilberfloss. Once again I assure you that it is more than prosperous.”

===Chinese===
「但愿不是。」普史密斯说。「我由衷但愿不是。我有十足的理由相信他神志健全。是什么让您觉得，他哪怕有一丝可能——呃——？」

「除了疯子，谁也不会赞成眼睁睁看着自己的报纸被毁。」

「又来了！」普史密斯说。「恐怕『本报已毁』这个念头已成了您的执念，威尔伯弗洛斯同志。我再一次向您保证，它兴旺得不能再兴旺。」

===Original===
“If,” said Mr. Wilberfloss, “you imagine that I intend to take your word in this matter, you are mistaken. I shall cable Mr. White today, and inquire whether these alterations in the paper meet with his approval.”

“I shouldn’t, Comrade Wilberfloss. Cables are expensive, and in these hard times a penny saved is a penny earned. Why worry Comrade White? He is so far away, so out of touch with our New York literary life. I think it is practically a certainty that he has not the slightest inkling of any changes in the paper.”

===Chinese===
「倘若，」威尔伯弗洛斯先生说，「您以为此事我打算听信您的一面之词，那就错了。我今天就去给怀特先生发电报，问明报纸的这些改动是否征得了他的首肯。」

「换我就不拍，威尔伯弗洛斯同志。电报费不便宜，这年头省一便士就是赚一便士。何必去搅扰怀特同志？他远在天边，同我们纽约文坛如此隔绝。我几乎可以断言，报纸有什么改动，他连一丝风声都没有。」

===Original===
Mr. Wilberfloss uttered a cry of triumph.

“I knew it,” he said, “I knew it. I knew you would give up when it came to the point, and you were driven into a corner. Now, perhaps, you will admit that Mr. White has given no sanction for the alterations in the paper?”

A puzzled look crept into Psmith’s face.

“I think, Comrade Wilberfloss,” he said, “we are talking at cross-purposes. You keep harping on Comrade White and his views and tastes. One would almost imagine that you fancied that Comrade White was the proprietor of this paper.”

===Chinese===
威尔伯弗洛斯先生发出一声得胜的欢呼。

「我就知道。」他说，「我就知道。我就知道事到临头、您被逼进死角，总会招的。那么现在，您也许肯承认了：怀特先生从未批准过报纸的这些改动？」

一抹困惑爬上普史密斯的脸。

「我看，威尔伯弗洛斯同志，」他说，「咱们这是鸡同鸭讲。您翻来覆去纠缠怀特同志和他的见解口味。人家几乎要以为，您把怀特同志当成了本报的老板。」

===Original===
Mr. Wilberfloss stared. B. Henderson Asher stared. Everyone stared, except Mr. Jarvis, who, since the readings from the Kid’s reminiscences had ceased, had lost interest in the discussion, and was now entertaining the cats with a ball of paper tied to a string.

“Fancied that Mr. White …?” repeated Mr. Wilberfloss. “I don’t follow you. Who is, if he isn’t?”

Psmith removed his monocle, polished it thoughtfully, and put it back in its place.

“I am,” he said.

===Chinese===
威尔伯弗洛斯先生目瞪口呆。B·亨德森·阿舍目瞪口呆。人人目瞪口呆——只有贾维斯先生除外，自从小子回忆录的朗读停止后，他对这场讨论已然兴味索然，这会儿正拿一个拴着线的纸团逗猫玩。

「把怀特先生当成……？」威尔伯弗洛斯先生重复道。「我没听懂。他不是，谁是？」

普史密斯摘下单片眼镜，若有所思地擦拭一番，装回原位。

「我是。」他说。

## XXI The Battle of Pleasant Street / 普莱森特街之战
===Original===
The new arrival was a young man with a shock of red hair, an ingrowing Roman nose, and a mouth from which force or the passage of time had removed three front teeth. He held on to the edges of the trap with his hands, and stared in a glassy manner into Psmith’s face, which was within a foot of his own.

There was a momentary pause, broken by an oath from Mr. Gooch, who was still undergoing treatment in the background.

“Aha!” said Psmith genially. “Historic picture. ‘Doctor Cook discovers the North Pole.’”

The redheaded young man blinked. The strong light of the open air was trying to his eyes.

===Chinese===
新来的是个红发蓬乱的青年，长着一只向内弯的罗马鼻，一张嘴里三颗门牙已被外力或岁月请了出去。他双手扒着活板门的边，目光呆滞地盯着普史密斯的脸——那张脸离他自己的不过一英尺。

片刻的停顿，被古奇先生的一声咒骂打破——他还在后头接受治疗。

「啊哈！」普史密斯和颜悦色地说。「历史名画：《库克医生发现北极》（Doctor Cook discovers the North Pole）。」

红发青年眨了眨眼。户外强烈的亮光考验着他的眼睛。

===Original===
“Youse had better come down,” he observed coldly. “We’ve got youse.”

“And,” continued Psmith, unmoved, “is instantly handed a gumdrop by his faithful Eskimo.”

As he spoke, he brought the stick down on the knuckles which disfigured the edges of the trap. The intruder uttered a howl and dropped out of sight. In the room below there were whisperings and mutterings, growing gradually louder till something resembling coherent conversation came to Psmith’s ear, as he knelt by the trap making meditative billiard shots with the stick at a small pebble.

“Aw g’wan! Don’t be a quitter!”

“Who’s a quitter?”

===Chinese===
「你们最好下来。」他冷冷地说。「你们跑不了了。」

「接着，」普史密斯不动声色地续道，「就被他忠实的爱斯基摩人塞了一颗橡皮糖。」

说话间，他把大棒挥落，砸在那几只扒着活板门边、有碍观瞻的指关节上。闯入者嚎了一声，缩得没了影。楼下屋里起先是窃窃私语、嘟嘟囔囔，渐渐响起来，直到某种近似连贯的对话传进普史密斯耳朵——他正跪在活板门旁，用大棒对着一颗小石子，打出一杆杆沉思的台球。

「嗐，别怂啊！」

「谁怂了？」

===Original===
“Youse is a quitter. Get on top de roof. He can’t hoit youse.”

“De guy’s gotten a big stick.”

Psmith nodded appreciatively.

“I and Roosevelt,” he murmured.

A somewhat baffled silence on the part of the attacking force was followed by further conversation.

“Gum! some guy’s got to go up.”

===Chinese===
「你就怂了。上房顶去。他伤不了你。」

「那家伙有根大棒。」

普史密斯赞许地点点头。

「我与罗斯福。」他喃喃道。

进攻方一阵颇为受挫的沉默之后，谈话继续。

「嚯！总得有个家伙上去。」

===Original===
Murmur of assent from the audience.

A voice, in inspired tones: “Let Sam do it!”

This suggestion made a hit. There was no doubt about that. It was a success from the start. Quite a little chorus of voices expressed sincere approval of the very happy solution to what had seemed an insoluble problem. Psmith, listening from above, failed to detect in the choir of glad voices one that might belong to Sam himself. Probably gratification had rendered the chosen one dumb.

“Yes, let Sam do it!” cried the unseen chorus. The first speaker, unnecessarily, perhaps—for the motion had been carried almost unanimously—but possibly with the idea of convincing the one member of the party in whose bosom doubts might conceivably be harboured, went on to adduce reasons.

===Chinese===
观众席上一片附和的低语。

一个声音灵光乍现：「让山姆（Sam）去！」

这项提议大获成功。这一点毫无疑问。它从头就是一桩成功。相当不小的一支合唱队纷纷发言，对这道原本看似无解的难题的圆满解法表示由衷赞许。普史密斯在上头听着，没能从这支欢欣的合唱队里分辨出可能属于山姆本人的嗓音。想必是天降的荣宠让这位当选人失了声。

「对，让山姆去！」看不见的合唱队高喊。第一位发言人——或许多此一举，因为动议已近乎全票通过——但大概是抱着说服队中唯一可能心存疑虑的那一位的念头，继续列举理由。

===Original===
“Sam bein’ a coon,” he argued, “ain’t goin’ to git hoit by no stick. Youse can’t hoit a coon by soakin’ him on de coco, can you, Sam?”

Psmith waited with some interest for the reply, but it did not come. Possibly Sam did not wish to generalise on insufficient experience.

“*Solvitur ambulando*,” said Psmith softly, turning the stick round in his fingers. “Comrade Windsor!”

“Hullo?”

===Chinese===
「山姆是个黑鬼，」他论证道，「哪能叫棍子伤着。你往黑鬼的脑瓜上来一家伙也伤不了他，对吧，山姆？」

普史密斯颇感兴趣地等候答复，答复却没有来。想必山姆不愿凭不足的经验妄下概论。

「走着解决（*Solvitur ambulando*）。」普史密斯轻声说，手指间转着大棒。「温莎同志！」

「哎？」

===Original===
“Is it possible to hurt a coloured gentleman by hitting him on the head with a stick?”

“If you hit him hard enough.”

“I knew there was some way out of the difficulty,” said Psmith with satisfaction. “How are you getting on up at your end of the table, Comrade Windsor?”

“Fine.”

===Chinese===
「用棍子敲一位有色人种先生的脑袋，有可能敲伤他吗？」

「敲得够狠就行。」

「我就知道这道难题总有出路。」普史密斯满意地说。「你那头的台子进展如何，温莎同志？」

「顺得很。」

===Original===
“Any result yet?”

“Not at present.”

“Don’t give up.”

“Not me.”

“The right spirit, Comrade Win—”

A report like a cannon in the room below interrupted him. It was merely a revolver shot, but in the confined space it was deafening. The bullet sang up into the sky.

===Chinese===
「有结果了吗？」

「眼下还没有。」

「别放弃。」

「我才不。」

「这就对了，温莎同——」

楼下屋里一声炮弹般的巨响打断了他。那不过是一发左轮枪弹，但在密闭空间里震耳欲聋。子弹唱着歌蹿上了天。

===Original===
“Never hit me!” said Psmith with dignified triumph.

The noise was succeeded by a shuffling of feet. Psmith grasped his stick more firmly. This was evidently the real attack. The revolver shot had been a mere demonstration of artillery to cover the infantry’s advance.

Sure enough, the next moment a woolly head popped through the opening, and a pair of rolling eyes gleamed up at the old Etonian.

===Chinese===
「没打中我！」普史密斯带着庄严的胜利感说。

响声过后，接着是一阵挪动的脚步。普史密斯把大棒握得更紧了。这显然是真正的进攻。那发左轮枪弹不过是炮火示范，为的是掩护步兵推进。

果然，下一刻，一颗毛茸茸的脑袋从洞口冒了出来，一双骨碌乱转的眼睛朝这位老伊顿生（Etonian）闪着光。

===Original===
“Why, Sam!” said Psmith cordially, “this is well met! I remember *you*. Yes, indeed, I do. Wasn’t you the feller with the open umbereller that I met one rainy morning on the Av-en-ue? What, are you coming up? Sam, I hate to do it, but—”

A yell rang out.

“What was that?” asked Billy Windsor over his shoulder.

“Your statement, Comrade Windsor, has been tested and proved correct.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，山姆！」普史密斯热络地说，「真是幸会！我记得*你*。没错，千真万确，我记得。你不就是那个下雨天的早晨、我在「大道」（Av-en-ue）上碰见的、撑着一把打开的雨伞的家伙吗？怎么，你要上来了？山姆，我实在不愿下手，可是——」

一声惨叫响起。

「那是什么？」比利·温莎回头问。

「你的论断，温莎同志，刚刚经过检验，证明无误。」

===Original===
By this time the affair had begun to draw a “gate.” The noise of the revolver had proved a fine advertisement. The roof of the house next door began to fill up. Only a few of the occupants could get a clear view of the proceedings, for a large chimney-stack intervened. There was considerable speculation as to what was passing between Billy Windsor and Mr. Gooch. Psmith’s share in the entertainment was more obvious. The early comers had seen his interview with Sam, and were relating it with gusto to their friends. Their attitude towards Psmith was that of a group of men watching a terrier at a rat-hole. They looked to him to provide entertainment for them, but they realised that the first move must be with the attackers. They were fair-minded men, and they did not expect Psmith to make any aggressive move.

Their indignation, when the proceedings began to grow slow, was directed entirely at the dilatory Three Pointers. With an aggrieved air, akin to that of a crowd at a cricket match when batsmen are playing for a draw, they began to “barrack.” They hooted the Three Pointers. They begged them to go home and tuck themselves up in bed. The men on the roof were mostly Irishmen, and it offended them to see what should have been a spirited fight so grossly bungled.

===Chinese===
到这时，事态已开始「卖出门票」。左轮枪的响声证明是一则上好的广告。隔壁房子的屋顶渐渐上人了。看客中只有少数几位能看清全貌，因为中间隔着一座大烟囱。至于比利·温莎和古奇先生之间进行着什么，众说纷纭。普史密斯在余兴节目中的戏份则显眼得多。早到的看客亲眼见了他与山姆的会谈，正起劲地讲给朋友们听。他们对普史密斯的态度，好比一群人围观守在鼠洞前的猎狐梗：他们指望他供给娱乐，却也明白第一步须由攻方迈出。他们都是讲究公道的人，并不指望普史密斯主动出击。

等战事渐渐拖沓下来，看客们的义愤便一股脑儿全发泄到磨磨蹭蹭的三点帮众头上。他们一脸委屈——那神情酷似板球场上眼看击球手们磨时间求平局的观众——开始起哄。他们朝三点帮众喝倒彩，央求他们回家钻被窝去。屋顶上这些看客多为爱尔兰人，眼看一场本该生龙活虎的恶战被搞得这般稀烂，实在有伤他们的感情。

===Original===
“G’wan away home, ye quitters!” roared one.

“Call yersilves the Three Points, do ye? An’ would ye know what *I* call ye? The Young Ladies’ Seminary!” bellowed another with withering scorn.

A third member of the audience alluded to them as “stiffs.”

“I fear, Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith, “that our blithe friends below are beginning to grow a little unpopular with the many-headed. They must be up and doing if they wish to retain the esteem of Pleasant Street. Aha!”

===Chinese===
「滚回家去吧，你们这群怂包！」一个吼道。

「还自称三点帮，是吧？那你们知道*我*管你们叫什么吗？青年女子学校！」另一位雷霆般地吼道，轻蔑入骨。

观众中的第三位则称他们为「废物」。

「恐怕，温莎同志，」普史密斯说，「咱们楼下这班欢快的朋友，在多头兽面前开始有点失人心了。他们若想保住普莱森特街的敬重，就得振作行动起来。啊哈！」

===Original===
Another and a longer explosion from below, and more bullets wasted themselves on air. Psmith sighed.

“They make me tired,” he said. “This is no time for a *feu de joie*. Action! That is the cry. Action! Get busy, you blighters!”

The Irish neighbours expressed the same sentiment in different and more forcible words. There was no doubt about it—as warriors, the Three Pointers had failed to give satisfaction.

===Chinese===
楼下又一声更长的爆响，更多子弹在空气里白白糟蹋了自己。普史密斯叹了口气。

「他们真叫我乏味。」他说。「现在不是放欢乐排枪（feu de joie）的时候。行动！这才是呼声。行动！干起来呀，你们这帮混蛋！」

爱尔兰邻居们用另一套更有力的字眼，表达了同一种情绪。这一点毫无疑问——作为战士，三点帮众未能令人满意。

===Original===
A voice from the room called up to Psmith.

“Say!”

“You have our ear,” said Psmith.

“What’s that?”

“I said you had our ear.”

“Are youse stiffs comin’ down off out of dat roof?”

===Chinese===
屋里一个声音朝普史密斯喊上来。

「喂！」

「我们洗耳恭听。」普史密斯说。

「你说啥？」

「我说我们洗耳恭听。」

「你们这群废物，到底从那房顶上下来不下来？」

===Original===
“Would you mind repeating that remark?”

“Are youse guys goin’ to quit off out of dat roof?”

“Your grammar is perfectly beastly,” said Psmith severely.

“Hey!”

“Well?”

===Chinese===
「劳驾把这句话再说一遍？」

「你们这帮家伙打不打算从那房顶上撤？」

「你的语法恶劣透顶。」普史密斯严厉地说。

「嘿！」

「怎么？」

===Original===
“Are youse guys—?”

“No, my lad,” said Psmith, “since you ask, we are not. And why? Because the air up here is refreshing, the view pleasant, and we are expecting at any moment an important communication from Comrade Gooch.”

“We’re goin’ to wait here till youse come down.”

“If you wish it,” said Psmith courteously, “by all means do. Who am I that I should dictate your movements? The most I aspire to is to check them when they take an upward direction.”

===Chinese===
「你们这帮家伙——？」

「不下来，小伙子，」普史密斯说，「既然你问了。为什么？因为上头这空气清爽宜人，风景可人，而且我们正时刻盼着古奇同志的重要通信。」

「我们就守在这儿，等你们下来。」

「你们乐意的话，」普史密斯彬彬有礼地说，「尽管守着。我算老几，岂能对诸位的行动发号施令？我最大的抱负，不过是当它们取向上行方向时加以拦截。」

===Original===
There was silence below. The time began to pass slowly. The Irishmen on the other roof, now definitely abandoning hope of further entertainment, proceeded with hoots of scorn to climb down one by one into the recesses of their own house.

Suddenly from the street far below there came a fusillade of shots and a babel of shouts and counter-shouts. The roof of the house next door, which had been emptying itself slowly and reluctantly, filled again with a magical swiftness, and the low wall facing into the street became black with the backs of those craning over.

===Chinese===
楼下静了下来。时间开始走得慢了。对面屋顶上的爱尔兰人，至此彻底断了再看余兴的念头，在一片嗤之以鼻的嘘声中，一个个爬回了自己房子的深处。

突然，下方远处的街上响起一阵连珠枪声，喊声与对喊声乱成一锅粥。隔壁房子的屋顶本正慢吞吞、恋恋不舍地清空，这会儿又魔术般飞快地满了，临街的矮墙被探头探脑者的后背挤成了一道黑。

===Original===
“What’s that?” inquired Billy.

“I rather fancy,” said Psmith, “that our allies of the Table Hill contingent must have arrived. I sent Comrade Maloney to explain matters to Dude Dawson, and it seems as if that golden-hearted sportsman had responded. There appear to be great doings in the street.”

In the room below confusion had arisen. A scout, clattering upstairs, had brought the news of the Table Hillites’ advent, and there was doubt as to the proper course to pursue. Certain voices urged going down to help the main body. Others pointed out that that would mean abandoning the siege of the roof. The scout who had brought the news was eloquent in favour of the first course.

===Chinese===
「那是什么？」比利问。

「我倒是揣测，」普史密斯说，「咱们桌山帮的盟友怕是到了。我派马洛尼同志去向纨绔子道森说明情况，看来那位金子心的运动家已经响应了。街上似乎正有一场大手笔。」

楼下屋里乱成了一团。一名探子蹬蹬蹬冲上楼来，带来了桌山帮众杀到的消息，下一步该走哪步棋，众议纷纭。有的声音力主下楼驰援大队人马。另一些则指出，那将意味着放弃对房顶的围困。报信的探子舌灿莲花，力挺前一方案。

===Original===
“Gum!” he cried, “don’t I keep tellin’ youse dat de Table Hills is here? Sure, dere’s a whole bunch of dem, and unless youse come on down dey’ll bite de hull head off of us lot. Leave those stiffs on de roof. Let Sam wait here with his canister, and den dey can’t get down, ’cos Sam’ll pump dem full of lead while dey’re beatin’ it t’roo de trapdoor. Sure.”

Psmith nodded reflectively.

“There is certainly something in what the bright boy says,” he murmured. “It seems to me the grand rescue scene in the third act has sprung a leak. This will want thinking over.”

===Chinese===
「嚯！」他嚷道，「我不是一直跟你们说桌山帮来了吗？真的，来了一大批，你们再不下去，他们非把咱们这窝人的脑袋整个咬掉不可。房顶上那几个废物随他们去。让山姆带他的铁罐子守在这儿，他们照样下不来，因为等他们钻活板门开溜的时候，山姆会往他们身上灌满铅。真的。」

普史密斯若有所思地点点头。

「这位机灵鬼的话里确实有点东西。」他喃喃道。「依我看，第三幕的盛大救援场面漏了气。这事得好好合计合计。」

===Original===
In the street the disturbance had now become terrific. Both sides were hard at it, and the Irishmen on the roof, rewarded at last for their long vigil, were yelling encouragement promiscuously and whooping with the unfettered ecstasy of men who are getting the treat of their lives without having paid a penny for it.

The behaviour of the New York policeman in affairs of this kind is based on principles of the soundest practical wisdom. The unthinking man would rush in and attempt to crush the combat in its earliest and fiercest stages. The New York policeman, knowing the importance of his own safety, and the insignificance of the gangsman’s, permits the opposing forces to hammer each other into a certain distaste for battle, and then, when both sides have begun to have enough of it, rushes in himself and clubs everything in sight. It is an admirable process in its results, but it is sure rather than swift.

===Chinese===
街上的骚乱至此已惊天动地。双方都打得热火朝天，屋顶上的爱尔兰人久守终有回报，正不论敌我地大喊助威，纵情狂呼——那是不花一分钱白看一场世纪好戏的人才有的、无拘无束的狂喜。

纽约警察在这类事端中的行事之道，建立在最健全的实用智慧之上。欠考虑的人会一冲而入，企图在战斗最初、最烈的阶段就将其扑灭。纽约警察深知自身安全之重、帮派分子性命之轻，便听任敌对双方把彼此捶打出几分厌战情绪，等两边都开始腻了，再亲自冲进去，见什么抡什么。就效果而言，这套程序令人赞叹；只是稳妥有余，神速不足。

===Original===
Proceedings in the affair below had not yet reached the police interference stage. The noise, what with the shots and yells from the street and the ear-piercing approval of the roof audience, was just working up to a climax.

Psmith rose. He was tired of kneeling by the trap, and there was no likelihood of Sam making another attempt to climb through. He walked towards Billy.

===Chinese===
下方事态尚未推进到警察介入的阶段。枪声、街上的喊杀，加上屋顶看客震耳的喝彩，这场喧嚣正步步推向最高潮。

普史密斯站起身。他跪守活板门跪累了，再说山姆也不像还会再来钻一回。他朝比利走去。

===Original===
As he did so, Billy got up and turned to him. His eyes were gleaming with excitement. His whole attitude was triumphant. In his hand he waved a strip of paper.

“I’ve got it,” he cried.

“Excellent, Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith. “Surely we must win through now. All we have to do is to get off this roof, and fate cannot touch us. Are two mammoth minds such as ours unequal to such a feat? It can hardly be. Let us ponder.”

===Chinese===
就在这时，比利也站起来，转向他。他双眼兴奋地放光，整个人一副得胜回朝的架势，手里挥着一张纸条。

「拿到了。」他喊道。

「妙极，温莎同志。」普史密斯说。「这回咱们必胜无疑了。只要下得这房顶，命运就奈何不得我们。像你我这样两颗巨脑，难道还玩不转这点伎俩？断不至于。且容我们凝神细想。」

===Original===
“Why not go down through the trap? They’ve all gone to the street.”

Psmith shook his head.

“All,” he replied, “save Sam. Sam was the subject of my late successful experiment, when I proved that coloured gentlemen’s heads could be hurt with a stick. He is now waiting below, armed with a pistol, ready—even anxious—to pick us off as we climb through the trap. How would it be to drop Comrade Gooch through first, and so draw his fire? Comrade Gooch, I am sure, would be delighted to do a little thing like that for old friends of our standing or—but what’s that!”

===Chinese===
「干吗不从活板门下去？他们全上街去了。」

普史密斯摇了摇头。

「全去了，」他答道，「除了山姆。山姆是我方才那场成功实验的受试者——实验证明，有色人种先生的脑袋是可以用棍子打伤的。他此刻正在下头守着，持枪待命，只等——甚至巴不得——我们从活板门往外爬时把我们一一点名。先让古奇同志掉下去、引开他的火力，这主意如何？这样一件小事，古奇同志肯定会乐意为咱们这样交情的老朋友效劳，要不——咦，那是什么！」

===Original===
“What’s the matter?”

“Is that a ladder that I see before me, its handle to my hand? It is! Comrade Windsor, we win through. *Cosy Moments*’ editorial staff may be tree’d, but it cannot be put out of business. Comrade Windsor, take the other end of that ladder and follow me.”

The ladder was lying against the farther wall. It was long, more than long enough for the purpose for which it was needed. Psmith and Billy rested it on the coping, and pushed it till the other end reached across the gulf to the roof of the house next door, Mr. Gooch eyeing them in silence the while.

===Chinese===
「怎么了？」

「我眼前所见、柄正对我手的，莫非是一架梯子？正是！温莎同志，咱们赢定了。《舒适时光》编辑部可以被逼上树，却不能被清出局。温莎同志，抄起梯子那头，跟我来。」

梯子斜靠在远端墙上。它很长，比所需的长度长出不止一点。普史密斯和比利把它架上矮墙压顶，往前推，直到另一头跨过深渊，搭上隔壁房子的屋顶——古奇先生全程默不作声地瞧着。

===Original===
Psmith turned to him.

“Comrade Gooch,” he said, “do nothing to apprise our friend Sam of these proceedings. I speak in your best interests. Sam is in no mood to make nice distinctions between friend and foe. If you bring him up here, he will probably mistake you for a member of the staff of *Cosy Moments*, and loose off in your direction without waiting for explanations. I think you had better come with us. I will go first, Comrade Windsor, so that if the ladder breaks, the paper will lose merely a subeditor, not an editor.”

===Chinese===
普史密斯转向他。

「古奇同志，」他说，「切莫向咱们的朋友山姆通报这些动作。我是为你的利益着想。山姆眼下没有心情细分敌我。你若把他引上来，他很可能会把你错认成《舒适时光》编辑部的一员，不等解释就朝你放枪。我看你最好跟我们一道走。我先上，温莎同志，这样梯子若是断了，报社损失的不过是一位副主编，而不是一位主编。」

===Original===
He went down on all fours, and in this attitude wormed his way across to the opposite roof, whose occupants, engrossed in the fight in the street, in which the police had now joined, had their backs turned and did not observe him. Mr. Gooch, pallid and obviously ill-attuned to such feats, followed him; and finally Billy Windsor reached the other side.

“Neat,” said Psmith complacently. “Uncommonly neat. Comrade Gooch reminded me of the untamed chamois of the Alps, leaping from crag to crag.”

===Chinese===
他手脚并用趴下，以这副姿态匍匐着爬过对面屋顶——那边的看客正全神贯注于街上的混战（警察也已加入），个个背对着他，无人察觉。古奇先生面无人色，明摆着与这类绝技格格不入，也跟了过去；最后，比利·温莎抵达了对岸。

「利落。」普史密斯怡然自得地说。「利落得非同寻常。古奇同志让我想起阿尔卑斯山未驯的岩羚羊，从一块峭壁跃向另一块峭壁。」

===Original===
In the street there was now comparative silence. The police, with their clubs, had knocked the last remnant of fight out of the combatants. Shooting had definitely ceased.

“I think,” said Psmith, “that we might now descend. If you have no other engagements, Comrade Windsor, I will take you to the Knickerbocker, and buy you a square meal. I would ask for the pleasure of your company also, Comrade Gooch, were it not that matters of private moment, relating to the policy of the paper, must be discussed at the table. Some other day, perhaps. We are infinitely obliged to you for your sympathetic cooperation in this little matter. And now goodbye. Comrade Windsor, let us debouch.”

===Chinese===
街上这时相对安静了。警察用警棍把参战者身上最后一丝斗志敲了个精光。枪声确已停歇。

「我想，」普史密斯说，「咱们现在可以下楼了。温莎同志，你若无其他约在身，我带你去尼克博克饭店，请你吃一顿正餐。本也想邀古奇同志赏光同往，怎奈席间须商谈关乎本报方针的私密事项，改日再叙吧。承蒙你在这桩小事上通力合作，我们感激不尽。那么就此别过。温莎同志，杀出去。」

## XVI The First Battle / 第一战
===Original===
The promptitude and despatch with which the Kid had attended to the gentleman with the blackjack had not been without its effect on the followers of the stricken one. Physical courage is not an outstanding quality of the New York hooligan. His personal preference is for retreat when it is a question of unpleasantness with a stranger. And, in any case, even when warring among themselves, the gangs exhibit a lively distaste for the hard knocks of hand-to-hand fighting. Their chosen method of battling is to lie down on the ground and shoot. This is more suited to their physique, which is rarely great. The gangsman, as a rule, is stunted and slight of build.

The Kid’s rapid work on the present occasion created a good deal of confusion. There was no doubt that much had been hoped for from speedy attack. Also, the generalship of the expedition had been in the hands of the fallen warrior. His removal from the sphere of active influence had left the party without a head. And, to add to their discomfiture, they could not account for the Kid. Psmith they knew, and Billy Windsor they knew, but who was this stranger with the square shoulders and the uppercut that landed like a cannonball? Something approaching a panic prevailed among the gang.

===Chinese===
小子收拾那位持铅头短棍的绅士时的干净利落，对倒下那位的手下并非没有影响。体魄上的勇气，本不是纽约流氓的过人之处。一旦与生人起了不愉快，他个人的偏好是撤退。况且，即便在自己人内部开战时，各帮派对白刃硬碰的皮肉之苦也怀有一种活蹦乱跳的厌恶。他们中意的战斗方式，是趴在地上开枪。这更契合他们的体格——那体格素来谈不上魁伟。帮派分子通常身量矮小，体形单薄。

小子此番的快手快脚造成了相当大的混乱。毫无疑问，他们原本对速攻寄予厚望。再者，这支远征队的将才本来握在倒下的勇士手里。他一离开积极影响的范围，队伍便群龙无首。更添他们狼狈的是，小子这个人他们摸不着底。普史密斯他们认得，比利·温莎他们认得，可这个肩膀方正、上勾拳如炮弹落地的生人是谁？帮派中弥漫起一种近乎恐慌的情绪。

===Original===
It was not lessened by the behaviour of the intended victims. Billy Windsor, armed with the big stick which he had bought after the visit of Mr. Parker, was the first to join issue. He had been a few paces behind the others during the blackjack incident; but, dark as it was, he had seen enough to show him that the occasion was, as Psmith would have said, one for the Shrewd Blow rather than the Prolonged Parley. With a whoop of the purest Wyoming brand, he sprang forward into the confused mass of the enemy. A moment later Psmith and the Kid followed, and there raged over the body of the fallen leader a battle of Homeric type.

It was not a long affair. The rules and conditions governing the encounter offended the delicate sensibilities of the gang. Like artists who feel themselves trammelled by distasteful conventions, they were damped and could not do themselves justice. Their forte was long-range fighting with pistols. With that they felt *en rapport*. But this vulgar brawling in the darkness with muscular opponents who hit hard and often with sticks and hands was distasteful to them. They could not develop any enthusiasm for it. They carried pistols, but it was too dark and the combatants were too entangled to allow them to use these. Besides, this was not the dear, homely old Bowery, where a gentleman may fire a pistol without exciting vulgar comment. It was uptown, where curious crowds might collect at the first shot.

===Chinese===
猎物们的表现更令恐慌有增无减。比利·温莎手持那根帕克先生来访后买下的大棒，第一个接了战。铅头短棍事件发生时，他落在众人身后几步；但天色虽黑，他已看清了足够多，足以明白这场合——照普史密斯的说法——宜于果断一击，而不宜于漫长谈判。他发出一声最纯正的怀俄明风味的呐喊，扑进敌人乱作一团的人堆。片刻之后，普史密斯和小子跟上，在那位倒下的首领躯体上空，一场荷马史诗式的战斗轰然展开。

这场战事为时不久。支配这场遭遇的规则与条件，冒犯了帮派诸君的细腻情操。就像自觉被讨厌的清规戒律捆住手脚的艺术家，他们意兴阑珊，无从施展真本领。他们的强项是用手枪远距离交火。对此他们才感觉合拍投缘。可在黑暗中同肌肉结实、棍拳又狠又密的对手打这种低俗的烂仗，他们实无心恋战。他们提着手枪，可天太黑，交战双方缠斗太紧，容不得开枪。何况，此地也不是那条亲切家常的老鲍厄里，在那儿绅士开一枪不至于招来市井闲话。这儿是上城，第一声枪响就可能引来围观的人群。

===Original===
There was but one thing to be done. Reluctant as they might be to abandon their fallen leader, they must tear themselves away. Already they were suffering grievously from the stick, the blackjack, and the lightning blows of the Kid. For a moment they hung, wavering; then stampeded in half a dozen different directions, melting into the night whence they had come.

Billy, full of zeal, pursued one fugitive some fifty yards down the street, but his quarry, exhibiting a rare turn of speed, easily outstripped him.

He came back, panting, to find Psmith and the Kid examining the fallen leader of the departed ones with the aid of a match, which went out just as Billy arrived.

===Chinese===
摆在前面的只有一条路。纵然舍不得抛弃倒下的首领，他们也必须忍痛割舍。棍棒、铅头短棍加上小子闪电般的拳头，已经让他们吃足了苦头。他们迟疑地悬停了片刻，随即朝六七个不同方向溃逃，消融在来时的夜色里。

比利满腔热忱，追着一名逃兵沿街跑了五十来码，无奈他的猎物展现出一副罕见的脚力，轻易把他甩在了身后。

他气喘吁吁地折回来，只见普史密斯和小子正借着一根火柴，检视那伙已逃散者留下的倒地首领——火柴恰在比利赶到时熄灭了。

===Original===
“It is our friend of the earlier part of the evening, Comrade Windsor,” said Psmith. “The merchant with whom we hobnobbed on our way to the Highfield. In a moment of imprudence I mentioned *Cosy Moments*. I fancy that this was his first intimation that we were in the offing. His visit to the Highfield was paid, I think, purely from sport-loving motives. He was not on our trail. He came merely to see if Comrade Brady was proficient with his hands. Subsequent events must have justified our fighting editor in his eyes. It seems to be a moot point whether he will ever recover consciousness.”

“Mighty good thing if he doesn’t,” said Billy uncharitably.

“From one point of view, Comrade Windsor, yes. Such an event would undoubtedly be an excellent thing for the public good. But from our point of view, it would be as well if he were to sit up and take notice. We could ascertain from him who he is and which particular collection of horny-handeds he represents. Light another match, Comrade Brady.”

===Chinese===
「这正是今晚前半晌的那位朋友，温莎同志。」普史密斯说。「咱们去海菲尔德路上与之攀交情的那位买卖人。我一时刻不谨慎，提到了《舒适时光》。我想，这是他头一回得到风声，知道我们在附近转悠。他光临海菲尔德，依我看，纯粹出于热爱体育的动机。他并非盯我们的梢。他来，只是想瞧瞧布雷迪同志的双手灵不灵。后续的事态，想必已让我们的战斗编辑在他眼里得到了正名。他还能不能恢复知觉，看来尚在未定之天。」

「他要是再也醒不过来才好呢。」比利不厚道地说。

「从一个角度看，是的，温莎同志。那样一件事无疑将是公益之福。但从我们的角度看，他要是能坐起来张望张望倒更好。我们可以从他口中查明他的身份，以及他代表的是哪一窝手上长茧的汉子。再划一根火柴，布雷迪同志。」

===Original===
The Kid did so. The head of it fell off and dropped upon the upturned face. The hooligan stirred, shook himself, sat up, and began to mutter something in a foggy voice.

“He’s still woozy,” said the Kid.

“Still—what exactly, Comrade Brady?”

“In the air,” explained the Kid. “Bats in the belfry. Dizzy. See what I mean? It’s often like that when a feller puts one in with a bit of weight behind it just where that one landed. Gum! I remember when I fought Martin Kelly; I was only starting to learn the game then. Martin and me was mixing it good and hard all over the ring, when suddenly he puts over a stiff one right on the point. What do you think I done? Fall down and take the count? Not on your life. I just turns round and walks straight out of the ring to my dressing room. Willie Harvey, who was seconding me, comes tearing in after me, and finds me getting into my clothes. ‘What’s doing, Kid?’ he asks. ‘I’m going fishin’, Willie,’ I says. ‘It’s a lovely day.’ ‘You’ve lost the fight,’ he says. ‘Fight?’ says I. ‘What fight?’ See what I mean? I hadn’t a notion of what had happened. It was a half an hour and more before I could remember a thing.”

===Chinese===
小子照办了。火柴头脱落，掉在那张仰着的脸上。流氓动了动，抖了抖身子，坐了起来，开始用一团浆糊似的嗓音咕哝什么。

「他还晕着呢。」小子说。

「还——究竟还什么，布雷迪同志？」

「人在半空。」小子解释道。「钟楼里进了蝙蝠。头晕。明白我的意思吗？一拳带着分量正落在那个位置，常常就这德行。嚯！我记得跟马丁·凯利（Martin Kelly）打那回，那会儿我才刚学这一行。马丁和我在拳台上满场打得难解难分，他冷不丁一记重拳正中下巴尖。你猜我怎么着？躺下吃秒数？想都别想。我一转身，径直走出拳台，回了更衣室。给我当助手的威利·哈维（Willie Harvey）跟着冲进来，看见我正在穿衣服。『干嘛呢，小子？』他问。『钓鱼去，威利。』我说。『今天天儿真好。』『你输了比赛了。』他说。『比赛？』我说。『什么比赛？』明白我的意思吗？出了什么事我一点概念都没有。过了半个多钟头，我才想起一星半点。」

===Original===
During this reminiscence, the man on the ground had contrived to clear his mind of the mistiness induced by the Kid’s uppercut. The first sign he showed of returning intelligence was a sudden dash for safety up the road. But he had not gone five yards when he sat down limply.

The Kid was inspired to further reminiscence. “Guess he’s feeling pretty poor,” he said. “It’s no good him trying to run for a while after he’s put his chin in the way of a real live one. I remember when Joe Peterson put me out, way back when I was new to the game—it was the same year I fought Martin Kelly. He had an awful punch, had old Joe, and he put me down and out in the eighth round. After the fight they found me on the fire escape outside my dressing room. ‘Come in, Kid,’ says they. ‘It’s all right, chaps,’ I says, ‘I’m dying.’ Like that. ‘It’s all right, chaps, I’m dying.’ Same with this guy. See what I mean?”

===Chinese===
这段忆旧讲完之前，地上那位已经设法从小子上勾针造成的迷雾中缓过了神。他智力恢复的第一个迹象，是猛地窜上路去逃命。可他没跑出五码，就一屁股瘫坐了下去。

小子受了触动，继续忆旧。「我看他是难受得很。」他说。「下巴颏送上去吃了一记真家伙，这一阵子他想跑也是白搭。我记得乔·彼得森（Joe Peterson）把我打出局那回——早在我还是新手的年月，就是跟马丁·凯利交手的那一年。老乔的拳头可吓人了，第八回合把我打得倒地不起。赛后他们在更衣室外的太平梯上找到了我。『进来吧，小子。』他们说。『没事的，哥们儿，』我说，『我快死了。』就是那样。『没事的，哥们儿，我快死了。』这家伙也一样。明白我的意思吗？」

===Original===
They formed a group about the fallen blackjack expert.

“Pardon us,” said Psmith courteously, “for breaking in upon your reverie; but, if you could spare us a moment of your valuable time, there are one or two things which we should like to know.”

“Sure thing,” agreed the Kid.

“In the first place,” continued Psmith, “would it be betraying professional secrets if you told us which particular bevy of energetic sandbaggers it is to which you are attached?”

===Chinese===
三人在倒下的铅头短棍行家身旁围成一圈。

「恕我们冒昧，」普史密斯彬彬有礼地说，「打断您的沉思；不过，您若能从百忙之中拨冗片刻，有一两件事我们愿闻其详。」

「那是必须的。」小子附和。

「首先，」普史密斯接着说，「可否请您告知，您所效忠的，究竟是哪一窝子干劲十足的沙袋客？说出来算不算泄露行业机密？」

===Original===
“Gent,” explained the Kid, “wants to know what’s your gang.”

The man on the ground muttered something that to Psmith and Billy was unintelligible.

“It would be a charity,” said the former, “if some philanthropist would give this blighter elocution lessons. Can you interpret, Comrade Brady?”

“Says it’s the Three Points,” said the Kid.

===Chinese===
「这位先生，」小子解说，「想知道你是哪个帮的。」

地上那位咕哝了一句什么，普史密斯和比利都没听懂。

「哪位慈善家要是肯出钱给这家伙上几堂朗诵课，」前者说，「真是积德行善。你能当翻译吗，布雷迪同志？」

「他说是三点帮。」小子说。

===Original===
“The Three Points? Let me see, is that Dude Dawson, Comrade Windsor, or the other gentleman?”

“It’s Spider Reilly. Dude Dawson runs the Table Hill crowd.”

“Perhaps this *is* Spider Reilly?”

“Nope,” said the Kid. “I know the Spider. This ain’t him. This is some other mutt.”

===Chinese===
「三点帮？我想想，那是纨绔子道森的地盘呢，温莎同志，还是另一位绅士的？」

「那是蜘蛛莱利的。纨绔子道森管的是桌山那伙人。」

「没准这位*正是*蜘蛛莱利？」

「不是。」小子说。「蜘蛛我认得。这不是他。这是另外哪只蠢驴。」

===Original===
“Which other mutt in particular?” asked Psmith. “Try and find out, Comrade Brady. You seem to be able to understand what he says. To me, personally, his remarks sound like the output of a gramophone with a hot potato in its mouth.”

“Says he’s Jack Repetto,” announced the interpreter.

There was another interruption at this moment. The bashful Mr. Repetto, plainly a man who was not happy in the society of strangers, made another attempt to withdraw. Reaching out a pair of lean hands, he pulled the Kid’s legs from under him with a swift jerk, and, wriggling to his feet, started off again down the road.
